# BrandSpace — Decision Register

> **الملخص التنفيذي بالعربية**
>
> هذا المستند هو **سجل القرارات** ومركز المراجعة لمالك المنتج. وهو مقسّم إلى أربعة أقسام واضحة:
>
> 1. **افتراضات معتمدة** — مأخوذة مباشرة من متطلبات المالك، لا تحتاج نقاشًا.
> 2. **قرارات موصى بها** — اختيارات تقنية اتخذها المهندس المعماري مع الأسباب والبدائل؛ تُطبَّق ما لم يعترض المالك.
> 3. **قرارات منتج غير محسومة** — تحتاج بحثًا أو تجربة أو معلومات إضافية قبل الحسم.
> 4. **قرارات تتطلب موافقة المالك قبل البدء** — **لا تبدأ المرحلة الأولى من التنفيذ قبل الإجابة عليها**.
>
> **الأهم:** القسم الرابع يحتوي على القرارات التي تحجب التنفيذ فعليًا — خصوصًا: مزودو الذكاء الاصطناعي، هيكل الخطط والأسعار،
> قيمة الرصيد وسياسته، مزود الدفع والأسواق المستهدفة، وأولوية منصات التواصل الاجتماعي.
> يُرجى مراجعة القسم الرابع أولًا.

---

## How to Use This Document

| Section                         | Meaning                                                  | Action needed                                    |
| ------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| §1 Approved assumptions         | Stated in the owner's requirements; treated as settled   | None — correct me if any is wrong                |
| §2 Recommended decisions        | Engineering choices with reasoning and alternatives      | Accept, or amend; silence is taken as acceptance |
| §3 Unresolved product decisions | Genuinely open; needs research, data, or a business call | Answer when ready; does not block Phase 1        |
| §4 Requires owner approval      | **Blocks implementation**                                | Answer before Phase 1 begins                     |

Each entry has an ID (`A-`, `R-`, `U-`, `D-`) so it can be referenced in code reviews and commits.
When a decision is made, record the date and the choice here — this file is the project's memory.

---

## 1. Approved Assumptions

Taken directly from the product requirements. These are settled and shape everything else.

| ID   | Assumption                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ---- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A-01 | BrandSpace is a **bilingual Arabic/English** platform; Arabic RTL is first-class, not a translation layer                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| A-02 | The platform is **multi-tenant**; every customer belongs to an isolated **Workspace**                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| A-03 | There are **three architecturally separated interfaces**: public website, customer dashboard, and Platform Admin                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| A-04 | Platform Admin is for the BrandSpace owner and authorized internal staff only, with platform-level permissions and future 2FA (this design makes 2FA mandatory for Owner and Admin from Phase 2)                                                                                                    |
| A-05 | A user in Workspace A must never read, modify, search, export, or **infer** data from Workspace B                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| A-06 | The platform is **configuration-driven**; the owner runs normal operations without editing code                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| A-07 | Providers, models, routing, plans, prices, limits, credit costs, feature availability, integration credentials, templates, billing config, currencies, trial duration, usage limits, and default policies are **never hard-coded**                                                                  |
| A-08 | Configuration is **versioned** with validation, activation, rollback, change history, and audit logging                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| A-09 | Secrets are encrypted at rest, never exposed to frontend code, never in logs or responses, and readable only as masked metadata                                                                                                                                                                     |
| A-10 | The AI Gateway is **provider-agnostic**; the customer never sees the platform API key                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| A-11 | **Optional customer BYOK** is available in higher plans                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| A-12 | AI credits abstract away provider tokens; failed requests are never charged; retries never double-charge                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| A-13 | Customers connect social accounts via **OAuth only** — BrandSpace never asks for a social password                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| A-14 | Platform Admin holds the social platform application credentials                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| A-15 | Only approved content is published when approval is required                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| A-16 | Every external publish, delete, disconnect, or bulk operation requires a confirmation policy and an audit record                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| A-17 | The AI Copilot is permission-aware and must never silently publish, delete, disconnect, pay, or send external communications                                                                                                                                                                        |
| A-18 | The architecture must not be bound to a single payment provider                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| A-19 | The MVP is a **modular monolith** that can be separated into services later; unnecessary microservices are avoided                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| A-20 | ~~Brand colors are Blue `#00ADEE` and Yellow `#FFDD15`~~ — **SUPERSEDED by D-42, D-60 and D-61.** The brand colours are Purple `#7935FE` and Yellow `#FFDD15`, everywhere including the public website; blue is retired. The original assumption is kept verbatim because it was true when recorded |
| A-21 | The first vertical slice uses **mock providers** and excludes real social publishing                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| A-22 | ~~Customer types supported: individual founder, startup, company marketing team, creator, agency, enterprise team~~ — **NARROWED by D-62.** The MVP serves businesses, founders, in-house brand teams and multi-brand companies. Agencies are possible future expansion, outside MVP scope          |
| A-23 | Customer roles: Workspace Owner, Workspace Admin, Marketing Manager, Content Creator, Copywriter, Designer, Approver, Analyst, Viewer (read-only) — stored as `client_viewer`, relabelled by D-58                                                                                                   |
| A-24 | Platform roles: Platform Owner, Platform Admin, Support Agent, Billing Manager, Operations Viewer                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| A-25 | Permissions exist at platform, workspace, brand, and campaign levels                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| A-26 | Automated tests must prove tenant isolation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| A-27 | Implementation does not begin until these architecture documents are reviewed and approved                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

## 2. Recommended Decisions

Engineering choices made in this phase. Each states the decision, the reasoning, the alternatives rejected,
and what it would cost to change later.

| ID   | Decision                                                                                                      | Reasoning                                                                                                                     | Rejected alternatives                                                                                            | Cost to reverse                   |
| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| R-01 | **Modular monolith + monorepo**                                                                               | Small team velocity, transactional integrity across billing/credits, single deploy; boundaries preserved for later extraction | Microservices from day one (operational overhead, distributed transactions); separate repos (version skew)       | Low — designed for extraction     |
| R-02 | **Shared database, shared schema, `workspaceId` + RLS**                                                       | Scales to self-serve sign-up; RLS gives defense in depth; enterprise-dedicated instances remain possible                      | Schema-per-tenant (migration cost explodes); database-per-tenant (operationally heavy)                           | High — change early or not at all |
| R-03 | **TypeScript everywhere** (Next.js, Node API, Node workers)                                                   | Shared types, schemas, and domain packages across web/api/worker                                                              | Polyglot backend (Go/Python) — doubles toolchain, loses type sharing                                             | High                              |
| R-04 | **PostgreSQL 16+** as the only primary datastore                                                              | Relational integrity for money, JSONB for config, RLS for isolation, pgvector for embeddings                                  | MySQL (no RLS), MongoDB (wrong for financial invariants)                                                         | High                              |
| R-05 | **Prisma** with a tenant-scoped client extension                                                              | Best-in-class TS ergonomics + migrations; scoping enforced by construction, not by memory                                     | Drizzle (better SQL control, thinner tooling), raw SQL (unacceptable human-error risk)                           | Medium                            |
| R-06 | **pgvector** for Brand Brain, not a dedicated vector DB                                                       | Embeddings inherit the same RLS tenant isolation; MVP volumes are small                                                       | Pinecone/Weaviate/Qdrant — second, weaker isolation boundary and another vendor                                  | Low — extractable later           |
| R-07 | **Redis + BullMQ** for queues, delayed jobs, locks, rate limits                                               | Delayed jobs are essential for scheduling; local-dev parity; no cloud lock-in                                                 | SQS/Cloud Tasks (lock-in), Temporal (heavier; revisit at Phase 6 for publishing sagas), pg-boss (fewer features) | Low                               |
| R-08 | **Database is the source of truth for schedules**; the queue is an accelerator, with a reconciliation sweeper | A Redis outage must cost punctuality, not correctness                                                                         | Queue-only scheduling                                                                                            | Low                               |
| R-09 | **S3-compatible object storage** behind a `StorageProvider` interface, pre-signed uploads, no public buckets  | Vendor-swappable; API never proxies bytes; security by construction                                                           | Direct SDK coupling; serving files from the app origin (XSS risk)                                                | Low                               |
| R-10 | **Zod** as the single schema language (HTTP, jobs, webhooks, config, AI output, env)                          | One definition reused front and back; config validation gets it for free                                                      | Separate validators per layer; JSON Schema (worse TS ergonomics)                                                 | Low                               |
| R-11 | **Auth.js** with two isolated realms + custom RBAC                                                            | Fast to build, covers OAuth/credentials; realm separation is ours, not the library's                                          | Custom auth from scratch (risk), a hosted IdP now (cost and lock-in before product-market fit)                   | Medium                            |
| R-12 | **2FA mandatory for Platform Owner and Platform Admin from Phase 2** (stronger than "future support")         | Admin compromise is catastrophic; TOTP is cheap to add now                                                                    | Optional 2FA at launch                                                                                           | Low                               |
| R-13 | **Vitest + Testcontainers + Playwright + k6**; isolation suite is blocking in CI                              | Real Postgres/Redis in tests is the only way to prove RLS behavior                                                            | Mocked database tests (would not prove isolation)                                                                | Low                               |
| R-14 | **OpenTelemetry**, vendor-neutral export                                                                      | Avoids observability vendor lock-in; consistent trace context across API and workers                                          | Vendor-specific agents                                                                                           | Low                               |
| R-15 | **Separate `apps/admin` deployment on a dedicated hostname**                                                  | Hard separation of session realm, permissions, and blast radius                                                               | Admin routes inside the dashboard                                                                                | Medium                            |
| R-16 | **Reserve → confirm → settle** credit model with an immutable ledger and a materialized balance               | The only design that satisfies "no charge on failure", "no double charge", and "never negative" under concurrency             | Charge-after-success (overspend under concurrency); charge-before (refund complexity)                            | High                              |
| R-17 | **FIFO-by-expiry credit consumption**                                                                         | Customers are never surprised by credits expiring that they could have used                                                   | LIFO; proportional consumption                                                                                   | Low                               |
| R-18 | **Downgrades take effect at period end; upgrades immediately**                                                | Avoids refund complexity and abrupt capability loss; standard SaaS expectation                                                | Immediate downgrade with proration                                                                               | Low                               |
| R-19 | **Hosted checkout and hosted billing portal**                                                                 | Keeps PCI scope minimal; card data never touches BrandSpace                                                                   | Custom payment forms (PCI burden)                                                                                | Low                               |
| R-20 | **Entitlements resolved from our own `Subscription`/`Plan`**, never directly from webhooks                    | A provider outage or spoofed event can never change access                                                                    | Provider as the entitlement source of truth                                                                      | Medium                            |
| R-21 | **Uncertain publish outcomes are verified, never blindly retried**                                            | The single most important rule for avoiding duplicate posts                                                                   | Retry on timeout                                                                                                 | Low                               |
| R-22 | **Copilot tool calls are authorized server-side per call**; the model's claims are ignored                    | Prevents prompt-injection privilege escalation                                                                                | Trusting the agent's plan                                                                                        | Low                               |
| R-23 | **Raw AI prompts and completions are not persisted by default**                                               | Privacy by default; audit-safe summaries suffice for support and billing                                                      | Full logging by default                                                                                          | Low                               |
| R-24 | **Mock adapters for AI and social are first-class and permanent**                                             | Enables the whole vertical slice, CI, and local dev without real credentials                                                  | Real providers first                                                                                             | Low                               |
| R-25 | **404, not 403, for cross-tenant access**, with identical shape and timing                                    | Prevents existence inference                                                                                                  | 403 (leaks existence)                                                                                            | Low                               |
| R-26 | **Cursor-based pagination** on all tenant data                                                                | Correctness under concurrent writes; no deep-offset cost                                                                      | Offset pagination                                                                                                | Low                               |
| R-27 | **Expand/contract migrations**, deploys always backward-compatible                                            | Zero-downtime deploys; safe rollback                                                                                          | Breaking migrations with downtime windows                                                                        | Low                               |
| R-28 | **Single region at MVP**, region selection deferred                                                           | Complexity and cost are not justified before demand exists                                                                    | Multi-region from day one                                                                                        | Medium                            |
| R-29 | **Agency support via both models** (many brands in one workspace, or one workspace per client)                | Different agencies want different isolation/billing shapes; the data model supports both with no extra machinery              | Forcing one model                                                                                                | Low                               |
| R-30 | **True impersonation is prohibited at MVP**; support acts as a labeled platform actor                         | Impersonation destroys audit clarity and is the most abusable feature in any SaaS admin                                       | "Log in as user"                                                                                                 | Low                               |
| R-31 | **Dual control on financial configuration** (pricing, credit costs): one actor drafts, another activates      | Prevents single-actor pricing mistakes and insider risk                                                                       | Single-actor activation                                                                                          | Low                               |
| R-32 | **Tailwind + Radix** for the design system                                                                    | Native RTL support via logical properties; accessible unstyled primitives                                                     | Component libraries with opinionated, hard-to-RTL styling                                                        | Medium                            |

## 3. Unresolved Product Decisions

Open questions that need research, data, or a business call. **None of these block Phase 1.**

| ID   | Question                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Why it is open                                                                  | When it must be answered      | Recommendation                                                                                                                      |
| ---- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| U-01 | ~~What exactly is 1 AI credit worth to a customer?~~ **ANSWERED provisionally by D-07/D-12.**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Needs a price point that is understandable and defensible                       | Before Phase 3 pricing config | Anchor credits to a round money value (e.g. 1 credit ≈ a small fixed amount) and set task costs so a typical caption costs 1 credit |
| U-02 | ~~Do we allow credit rollover, and how much?~~ **ANSWERED by D-12:** capped at 1× monthly allowance.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Affects perceived value vs. revenue predictability                              | Phase 3                       | Capped rollover (e.g. up to 1× monthly allowance) — generous enough to feel fair, bounded enough to protect revenue                 |
| U-03 | Is BYOK a discount, a higher-tier perk, or both?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Affects plan positioning and margin                                             | Phase 4                       | Enterprise/top-tier perk with a reduced platform fee, not a discount on lower tiers                                                 |
| U-04 | Agency white-labeling — how far? **Moot for the MVP under D-62** (not an agency product); revisit only if agency expansion is approved.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Significant build cost; affects pricing tier design                             | Post-launch                   | Defer; validate demand with early agency customers first                                                                            |
| U-05 | ~~Is there a free tier, or trial-only?~~ **ANSWERED by D-09:** trial-only, no permanent free plan at launch.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Affects acquisition, support load, and abuse exposure                           | Before Phase 9                | Trial-only at launch (14 days, no card); revisit a free tier once unit economics are measured                                       |
| U-06 | ~~Read-only Viewer approval rights — can a viewer approve content?~~ **ANSWERED by D-130: NO, for the MVP.** D-121 briefly answered "yes, per brand"; that contradicted **D-62**, which excludes client portals and hand-off workflows from the MVP. Viewer is strictly read-only. The capability is deferred to a future **External Review / Guest Approval** actor — not a repurposed `client_viewer`. | Affects agency workflows                                                        | Deferred (post-MVP)           | Keep Viewer read-only; design External Review as its own narrow actor                                                               |
| U-07 | Are custom workspace roles needed, or are the nine fixed roles enough?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Enterprise ask; adds permission-model complexity                                | Post-launch                   | Fixed roles at launch; custom roles as an enterprise feature later                                                                  |
| U-08 | ~~Content retention default for the customer's own drafts and published history~~ **ANSWERED by D-116:** retained while the subscription is active, then a 30-day grace period; audit, billing and credit records are excluded.                                                                                                                                                                          | Storage cost vs. customer expectation                                           | Phase 5                       | Unlimited for active subscriptions; 30-day grace after cancellation                                                                 |
| U-09 | Do we store AI-generated images indefinitely?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Storage cost grows fast                                                         | Phase 5                       | Count against the plan's storage quota, same as uploads                                                                             |
| U-10 | Which analytics metrics do customers actually care about most?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Drives dashboard design and ingestion priorities                                | Phase 7                       | Interview 5–10 target customers during Phases 5–6                                                                                   |
| U-11 | ~~Arabic dialect handling in generation (MSA vs. Gulf/Egyptian/Levantine)~~ **ANSWERED by D-115:** a configured workspace/brand attribute defaulting to MSA, with Gulf/Saudi, Egyptian and Levantine supported; no dialect is hard-coded as the default.                                                                                                                                                 | Materially affects output quality and perceived value                           | Phase 5                       | Make dialect a Brand Brain tone attribute; default to MSA; evaluate model quality per dialect                                       |
| U-12 | Does the Copilot get a conversational memory across sessions?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Privacy and cost implications                                                   | Phase 7                       | Per-brand memory, workspace-scoped, customer-clearable                                                                              |
| U-13 | How aggressive should automations be allowed to be (auto-publish without a human)?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Trust and liability                                                             | Phase 7                       | Require an explicit, separately-granted standing authorization per rule, off by default                                             |
| U-14 | Public template gallery — curated by BrandSpace, or community-contributed?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Moderation burden                                                               | Post-launch                   | Curated at launch                                                                                                                   |
| U-15 | Status page — self-hosted or third-party?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Cost vs. independence (a self-hosted status page can go down with the platform) | Phase 10                      | Third-party, so it survives our own outages                                                                                         |
| U-16 | Do we need an in-product support chat at launch?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Support load and cost                                                           | Phase 10                      | Email + in-app ticket form at launch; chat once volume justifies it                                                                 |

## 4. Decisions Requiring the Product Owner's Approval

**These block implementation. Phase 1 should not begin until §4.1–§4.5 are answered, and each later phase
needs its own items answered before it starts.**

### 4.1 Blocking Phase 1 (Foundations)

| ID       | Decision needed                                                            | Options                                                                                                   | Recommendation                                                                                                                                                                      | Impact if wrong                                                                               |
| -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-01** | **Approve the technology stack** (§2, R-01 to R-14, R-32)                  | Accept as recommended · amend specific choices · request alternatives                                     | Accept as recommended                                                                                                                                                               | Changing R-02 (tenancy) or R-04 (database) later is a rewrite; the rest are cheaper to change |
| **D-02** | **Hosting and deployment target**                                          | Managed platform (Vercel + managed Postgres/Redis) · a single cloud (AWS/GCP/Azure) · a regional provider | Managed platform for MVP: lowest operational load for a small team; migratable because everything is containerized and standard                                                     | Affects cost, latency to target markets, and compliance posture                               |
| **D-03** | **Primary data region**                                                    | EU · US · Middle East (UAE/Saudi)                                                                         | Choose by where the first customers are. If the primary market is MENA, pick the closest available region — latency and data-residency questions will be asked by enterprise buyers | Migrating regions after launch is disruptive                                                  |
| **D-04** | **Confirm the three-interface separation and the admin hostname strategy** | Separate hostname (recommended) · path-based separation                                                   | Separate hostname                                                                                                                                                                   | Path-based separation weakens the isolation guarantee                                         |
| **D-05** | **Legal entity, invoicing jurisdiction, and tax obligations**              | —                                                                                                         | Needed before billing design is finalized, and it affects currency and tax configuration                                                                                            | Wrong invoicing setup is a legal and accounting problem, not just a code change               |

### 4.2 Blocking Phase 3 (Plans, Entitlements, Credits) — **ANSWERED 2026-09-07**

All seven are now decided. The full text of each answer is in the decision log (§5); this table is the
index. **The values are configuration, never source** — `AC-04.3` fails the build if a plan name, price,
limit or trial duration appears in application code.

| ID       | Decision needed                                 | Owner's answer                                                                                                                         |
| -------- | ----------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-06** | Plan structure: how many tiers, and their names | **Starter · Growth · Scale · Enterprise.** "Agency" is not used as a plan name (D-62)                                                  |
| **D-07** | Price points per tier, per currency             | **Provisional:** $29 / SAR 109 · $79 / SAR 299 · $199 / SAR 749 · custom. Annual = 10 monthly payments. Review again before production |
| **D-08** | Supported currencies at launch                  | **SAR + USD**, no runtime conversion. Matches the shipped `supportedCurrencies` default                                                |
| **D-09** | Trial length and card requirement               | **14 days, no card, one per workspace, 200 trial credits.** No permanent free plan at launch                                           |
| **D-10** | Quota values per tier                           | Seats 2/8/20 · brands 1/3/10 · social accounts 3/12/40 · posts/month 100/500/2,000 · storage 5/50/250 GB · analytics 90d/12mo/24mo     |
| **D-11** | Overage policy                                  | **Hard stop on every plan. Prepaid top-ups only. No postpaid overage, no surprise invoice charges** — stricter than was recommended    |
| **D-12** | Credit expiry policy                            | Packs 12 months · promotional 3 months · plan credits roll over up to 1× allowance · FIFO by nearest expiry                            |

**Still unanswered and still blocking**: nothing in §4.2. Phase 3 implementation is unblocked on the
commercial decisions; it has not been authorised to start.

### 4.3 Blocking Phase 4 (AI Gateway) — **ANSWERED 2026-09-13**

> D-13, D-15, D-16 and D-17 were approved by the product owner on 2026-09-13. The rows below are the
> recommendations as put; the approvals, with the conditions attached to them, are in §4.3.1.

| ID       | Decision needed                     | Options                                                  | Recommendation                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| -------- | ----------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-13** | **Which AI providers for launch**   | One primary + one fallback · multi-provider from day one | One primary provider plus one fallback per modality. **Requirement: the provider's terms must include no-training / zero-retention for our data** — this is a hard filter, and it must be verified before Phase 4 |
| **D-14** | **Credit granularity**              | Whole credits (integer) · milli-credits (bigint)         | **Milli-credits internally, whole credits displayed.** Cheap tasks otherwise round badly, and changing this after launch means migrating the ledger                                                               |
| **D-15** | **Target gross margin on AI**       | 50% · 60% · 70%+                                         | 60–70% on AI usage, so provider price rises can be absorbed without repricing customers                                                                                                                           |
| **D-16** | **Which modalities are in the MVP** | Text only · text + image · text + image + video          | **Text + image.** Video generation is expensive, slow, and immature — a Phase 7+ candidate                                                                                                                        |
| **D-17** | **Arabic model quality bar**        | —                                                        | Requires a side-by-side evaluation of candidate models on Arabic marketing copy **before** Phase 4 routing is configured. Budget time for this; it is the single biggest quality risk in the product              |

#### 4.3.1 As approved — 2026-09-13

| ID       | Approved                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-13** | **Provider architecture approved; exact providers pending benchmark, privacy verification and owner approval.** One primary provider plus one fallback per supported modality. Vendor selection happens only after: Arabic and English quality benchmarking · current pricing comparison · privacy and data-processing review · confirmation that customer data is not used for provider training · confirmation of zero retention or an acceptable equivalent · owner approval of the final provider/model routing table. Provider names, models and routing remain configuration-driven. **No real production provider adapter is built or enabled.**                                                                                   |
| **D-15** | **Target gross margin on AI usage: 65%.** An internal commercial target, NOT a hard-coded markup. Provider cost, customer credit charge, routing and margin calculation remain configuration-driven and versioned. Prices are derived as `customer price = provider cost / (1 - target gross margin)`. Published package prices are unchanged and final per-action credit prices are **not** set here — they will be calibrated from real provider benchmarks.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **D-16** | **MVP modalities: text generation and transformation, and image generation and editing.** Video generation is **excluded from the MVP** and recorded as a **Phase 7+ candidate requiring a separate cost, latency and product review**. Voice remains post-MVP. Embedding and moderation are internal plumbing rather than customer-facing generation modalities and are unaffected.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **D-17** | **No provider/model may be enabled for production customer routing until it passes a documented side-by-side Arabic marketing-content benchmark.** The benchmark must evaluate, at minimum: Arabic grammar and spelling · Modern Standard Arabic quality · Saudi/Gulf localisation where requested · Brand Voice adherence · instruction following · marketing usefulness and originality · English/Arabic consistency · safety and factual reliability · structured-output reliability · latency and cost. Defined in **`docs/AI-QUALITY-BENCHMARK.md`**. **No paid provider evaluation has been run and no winning model has been selected** — both need the owner's go-ahead.                                                          |
| **D-78** | **AI output persistence policy.** `persistOutput` defaults to `false`. The minimum operational metadata required for usage, cost, credits, idempotency, auditing and diagnostics is always retained. Raw provider prompts and raw provider responses are never persisted by default. A user-facing AI result is persisted only when the calling product feature explicitly requires it. Anything persisted is tenant-isolated and covered by a defined retention and deletion policy. Sensitive data, secrets and provider credentials are never stored in prompts, outputs, logs or audit metadata. The feature/domain that requests persistence owns the saved artifact — **the AI Gateway must not become a permanent content store.** |

**How each is enforced rather than merely recorded.** A decision that lives only in this file is one
a future change can contradict without anything noticing:

| Decision | Where it binds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| D-13     | `ai.providers` carries `noTrainingGuarantee`, `dataRetentionPolicy` and `privacyReviewRef`; configuration validation **refuses to activate** a provider that has not cleared all three. The no-training check was a warning while the decision was open and is now an error |
| D-15     | `ai.credit-rules.targetGrossMarginPercent` (nullable, no invented value) plus `requiredPriceMicroMinor()`, which implements the division rather than a markup. A unit test asserts the two are not the same number                                                          |
| D-16     | The task catalogue marks `video.generate` and `voice.synthesize` as outside MVP scope, and `resolveRoute` refuses them — so an out-of-scope task cannot be served even if a rule for it were activated                                                                      |
| D-17     | `ai.models.qualityBenchmarkRef`; validation refuses status `available` without it. `beta` is exempt, because beta is the status a model sits in while it is being benchmarked                                                                                               |
| D-78     | `persistOutput` defaults false; validation refuses it without `outputRetentionDays`; `purgeExpiredOutputs()` clears expired payloads and leaves the accounting intact                                                                                                       |

### 4.4 Blocking Phase 6 (Social Publishing)

| ID       | Decision needed                                             | Options                           | Recommendation                                                                                                                                                         |
| -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-18** | **Launch platform priority**                                | All six at once · a phased subset | **Instagram + Facebook + LinkedIn first**, then TikTok, YouTube, X. This covers most SMB and agency demand with the most stable APIs                                   |
| **D-19** | **Start platform app review and business verification now** | —                                 | **This is a lead-time item that must start during Phase 1–2, not Phase 6.** Meta and TikTok reviews can take weeks and can block launch even when the code is finished |
| **D-20** | **X (Twitter) API tier**                                    | Free · Basic · Pro                | Depends on expected posting volume; the paid tiers are a real, ongoing cost that must be priced into plans                                                             |

### 4.5 Blocking Phase 9 (Commerce & Onboarding)

| ID       | Decision needed                                      | Options                                                                                 | Recommendation                                                                                                                                                     |
| -------- | ---------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **D-21** | **Payment provider(s)**                              | A single global provider · a global provider + a regional one for local payment methods | Start with one global provider for speed; the abstraction lets a regional provider be added when local methods (mada, KNET, local cards) matter for conversion     |
| **D-22** | **Tax handling**                                     | Provider tax engine · manual configuration                                              | Provider tax engine where available — VAT rules are not worth hand-maintaining                                                                                     |
| **D-23** | **Refund policy**                                    | No refunds · 14-day money-back · pro-rated                                              | 14-day money-back on first purchase; pro-rated at the owner's discretion afterwards                                                                                |
| **D-24** | **Launch market and go-to-market language priority** | MENA-first (Arabic-primary) · global (English-primary) · both equally                   | Affects which locale is the default, which currency leads, SEO strategy, and content investment — please decide explicitly                                         |
| **D-25** | **Legal documents**                                  | —                                                                                       | Terms, Privacy Policy, DPA, Acceptable Use, and Refund Policy need legal review before launch. Engineering provides the technical facts; the owner arranges review |

### 4.6 Cross-cutting approvals

| ID       | Decision needed                                                                                             | Recommendation                                                                                                                                             |
| -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-26** | **Approve the MVP vertical slice scope** as defined in `docs/MVP-ACCEPTANCE-CRITERIA.md`                    | Approve as written; it is deliberately narrow so the hard invariants (isolation, credits, configuration, audit) are proven before feature breadth is added |
| **D-27** | **Approve mandatory 2FA for platform roles from Phase 2** (stronger than the stated "future 2FA support")   | Approve — admin compromise is the highest-severity risk in the system                                                                                      |
| **D-28** | **Approve the prohibition on true impersonation** in support mode                                           | Approve — support gets read-only, audited, time-boxed access instead                                                                                       |
| **D-29** | **Approve the isolation-test CI gate** (a new tenant-owned model without an isolation test fails the build) | Approve — this is what makes A-05 enforceable over time rather than aspirational                                                                           |
| **D-30** | **Confirm no real credentials are used before Phase 6** and that sandbox/test credentials are used first    | Approve                                                                                                                                                    |

### 4.7 Blocking the first production release of the Asset Library

**The library is complete and tested end to end against the development stores. It refuses to run in production until these two are answered**, because the alternative is accepting a customer's file and quietly losing it, or reporting it as scanned when nothing scanned it.

| ID        | Decision needed                                                                     | Options                                                                                                     | Recommendation                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-110** | **Object-storage provider and region for customer files** (F-77)                    | Any S3-compatible service · a managed provider bundled with the hosting choice in D-02 · self-hosted        | **Decide alongside D-02 and D-03**, so files and the database share a region. The adapter goes behind the existing `ObjectStore` interface; nothing outside `packages/storage` changes.                    |
| **D-111** | **Virus-scanning engine, and whether customer bytes may leave the platform** (F-78) | Hosted scanning API (bytes leave) · self-managed engine in the worker network (bytes stay, you run updates) | **Self-managed, if the data region in D-03 is a privacy commitment.** A hosted API is less operational work and a larger privacy surface; that trade is the owner's to make, not an implementation detail. |

### 4.8 Raised by the current execution Phase 3 (Billing & Entitlements Operations)

**None of these blocks the phase's engineering work, which is delivered around them.** Each is a
decision about the product or the commercial relationship, and guessing one would mean writing an
approved answer into code where nobody had approved it.

| ID        | Decision needed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Options                                                                                                                                                                           | Why it is not ours to take                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-233** | **What is a seat, and when is one taken?** `limit.seats` is configured, projected and shown, and no path consumes it. A seat could be an active membership (taken at acceptance) or a committed seat (taken at invitation and returned when one lapses).                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Membership only · membership + pending invitation · something else                                                                                                                | It changes what a customer is charged for and what an invitation means. There is also a hard constraint either way: the founder's own membership is written by the transaction that CREATES the workspace, before a plan can exist, so failing closed there stops a workspace existing at all rather than stopping a seat being added. |
| **D-234** | **What does a billing suspension take away?** (CANCELLED and EXPIRED are settled by D-246; only SUSPENDED remains open.) §3.5 says "AI + publishing stop; data retained; export available". The platform's only suspension mechanism (`Workspace.status`) removes the workspace from its members' sessions entirely, which would also remove the export the same paragraph promises. `WorkspaceSubscription.status` is not read by the entitlements engine at all. | Withdraw a named set of capabilities through the entitlements engine · suspend the workspace outright and add an export-only mode · leave access intact and rely on dunning email | It is the moment a paying relationship turns into a refusal, and the two existing mechanisms disagree about what it means.                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| **D-235** | **Does a PAST_DUE subscription receive the next cycle's credit allowance?** §11 says the grant happens on the billing-cycle boundary and does not condition it on payment, so that is what is implemented.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Grant as documented · withhold until payment recovers · grant a reduced amount                                                                                                    | It is credit given to a customer who has not paid. The documented behaviour is implemented and easy to change; inventing a different one would have been an unapproved commercial decision.                                                                                                                                            |
| **D-236** | **Should replaying a dead-lettered billing event have its own permission key?** A replay can activate a subscription and grant purchased credits, so it now requires BOTH `platform.plan.assign` and `platform.credit.adjust` — strictly narrower than either alone, and no existing key was widened.                                                                                                                                                              | Keep the union · add `platform.billing.event.replay` and grant it to named roles                                                                                                  | Adding a permission key and deciding which roles hold it is an RBAC decision (docs/SECURITY.md §4.4), not an engineering convenience.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

Hiding them behind `INTERNAL` made a wall the customer could clear look like a platform fault, and left the dashboard with no way to offer the top-up or upgrade path D-11 requires. |

---

### Phase 4 decisions taken during implementation

These were made while building the gateway, are recorded so they are visible, and are open to the owner
reversing any of them.

| ID       | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-71** | **Money in the AI path is counted in micro-minor units and credits in milli-credits, both as integers; the workspace multiplier is in basis points.** No floating-point number appears in any money or credit calculation. A minor unit cannot hold a token price (F-64) and a float multiplier of 1.1 is not 1.1.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **D-72** | **Margin is reported as UNKNOWN, never as a number, until the owner prices a credit.** `ai.credit-rules.creditValueMicroMinor` defaults to null and the margin floor does not run while it is. Reporting a healthy margin for an unpriced platform would be worse than reporting nothing. **Pricing a credit remains an owner decision (D-15/D-16).**                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **D-73** | **The task catalogue is code; everything about how a task is served is configuration.** A task key is the name application code calls the gateway by — the same status a feature key has — and its modality is intrinsic. Which model serves it, in what order, with what timeout, temperature, retry policy, ceiling and credit cost is configuration and appears nowhere in source.                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **D-74** | **Retryability and fallback eligibility are derived from the failure taxonomy, and `retryOn` is not configurable.** Making `content_filtered` retryable from a config screen would turn a moderation refusal into a paid retry loop. `maxAttempts` is capped at 5 for the same reason.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **D-75** | **Input moderation fails open.** If the moderation model is unreachable the request proceeds; a model that answers "flagged" always blocks. A moderation outage that silently blocked every customer's work would be a far larger incident than the content it exists to catch, and it is visible through the same failure metrics as any other provider call. This is the only place in the gateway where an error is not fatal.                                                                                                                          |
| **D-76** | **The Admin AI screens show accounting, never customer content** — not even for a routing rule that opted into persisting output. Reading a customer's generated content is a Support Mode decision with its own time box and audit trail, not a side effect of opening an operations page.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **D-78** | **AI output persistence policy** (owner-approved 2026-09-13). `persistOutput` defaults to false; operational metadata is always retained; raw prompts and raw provider responses are never persisted by default; a user-facing result is persisted only when the calling feature requires it; anything persisted is tenant-isolated and has a defined retention and deletion policy; secrets never appear in prompts, outputs, logs or audit metadata; the requesting feature owns the artifact and the gateway must not become a permanent content store. |
| **D-77** | **`platform.ai.usage.read` is its own permission** and does not ride on `platform.workspace.read`. AI usage is a per-workspace financial record; letting "View any workspace" imply it is exactly how the configuration screens once became reachable by every admin-capable role (R-02). Granted to the Platform Owner, Platform Admin, Billing Manager and Operations Viewer; withheld from the Support Agent.                                                                                                                                           |

### Phase 5A implementation decisions (D-79 … D-84)

Taken during implementation, all reversible, each recorded because a future change that contradicts
one should have to argue with it rather than discover it.

| ID       | Decision                                                                                                                                                                   | Reasoning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-79** | **Per-area completion requirements are CODE, not configuration.** Upload rules, retention windows and freshness intervals are configuration; what "complete" MEANS is not. | A plan- or tenant-tunable definition would make the same badge mean different things to different customers, and a customer comparing their screen to the documentation would be right to call it a lie.                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **D-80** | **LEARNINGS does not count toward completion.** It is reported, and never held against the brand.                                                                          | It is written back by the system from performance evidence (D-64). Counting it would leave a customer who did everything asked of them permanently short of 100% through no fault of their own.                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **D-81** | **An accepted candidate lands as `DOCUMENT`, not `AI_INFERRED`.**                                                                                                          | A candidate a person read, judged and accepted is no longer an inference — they have taken responsibility for it. Had it stayed AI_INFERRED, `mayOverwrite` would afterwards refuse edits from the very people who approved it, and the review queue would produce knowledge nobody could maintain.                                                                       |
| **D-82** | **A chat refusal makes no gateway call and costs nothing.**                                                                                                                | Retrieval runs first; if nothing relevant is approved, the insufficient-knowledge answer is returned without a reservation. Charging a customer to be told their Brand Brain is empty would be indefensible.                                                                                                                                                              |
| **D-83** | **Prompt-injection containment applies to KNOWLEDGE, not only to document chunks.**                                                                                        | A candidate accepted from a poisoned document becomes a knowledge item, which retrieval trusts MORE than a raw chunk. Containment that stopped at the chunk boundary would be strongest exactly where the risk is lowest.                                                                                                                                                 |
| **D-84** | **`brand_brain.review` is a separate permission from `brand_brain.edit`, and `approver` now holds it.** No new role was introduced.                                        | Approving a candidate is what turns machine output into brand truth, and D-65 makes that a human decision with a named owner. Collapsing it into `edit` would mean anyone who can fix a typo can also approve everything a document proposed. `analyst` and `client_viewer` stay strictly read-only, including no `brand_brain.chat`, because a chat turn spends credits. |

### Phase 5A corrective pass (D-85 … D-97)

| ID       | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Reasoning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-85** | **The approved demo is a binding UI SPECIFICATION for every customer route, recorded in `docs/UI-FIDELITY-CONTRACT.md`.** The Brand Brain orb is re-ported mechanically from `demo/brand-brain-native.css` and `demo/brand-brain-native.js` at Landing commit `b01d947`.                                                                    | The first Phase 5A delivery re-interpreted the demo — a lavender container it does not have, a black rounded square where it has a transparent circle, white cards where it has 12px dots — and every automated check passed anyway. Accessible, correct, well-tested and wrong. Treating the demo as a specification removes the option.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **D-86** | **`prefers-reduced-motion` draws ONE STATIC FRAME of the orb.** An authorised deviation: the demo implements no reduced-motion path at all.                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `CLAUDE.md` §4 sets WCAG 2.2 AA as a product requirement and a prototype's omission does not outrank it.
