The accommodation shows the same orb and only stops animating, so a reader who asked for less motion still gets the knowledge map rather than a blank box.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **D-87** | **Brand Brain keeps SIX orbit nodes and TEN area cards below the hero.**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | The demo's orb carries exactly six nodes. The product has ten knowledge areas, all of which appear in the grid. Moving all ten into the orb because ten exist in the database would be a redesign, not a data binding.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| **D-88** | **Where a lint rule forbids a literal, a Brand Brain token carrying the demo's EXACT value is added** rather than substituting the nearest existing design-system token.                                                                                                                                                                    | Substituting an approximate token to satisfy a linter changes the approved result. It is a redesign wearing a compliance costume, and it is how the first delivery drifted.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **D-89** | **Five of the demo's small-text colours are darkened to the nearest step of the SAME hue that clears WCAG 2.2 AA**, listed with measured before/after ratios in `packages/ui/src/brand-brain.css` §2. Nothing else about them changes.                                                                                                      | `.bb-count` reads 2.85:1 and three greens read between 3.72:1 and 4.09:1 — a serious AA failure for anyone with low vision, at 8–9px. Darkening the same hue is the smallest change that fixes it; `.bb-count` takes the demo's own `--bb-muted`, so even the replacement is a value the demo chose.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **D-90** | **The chat lives INSIDE the hero's right-hand column and swaps with the stats view**, as the demo does, rather than floating over the page.                                                                                                                                                                                                 | The demo's `.bb-hero-stats.chat-open` hides the stats and shows the chat in the same fixed-height box. The floating panel in the first delivery changed the page's composition whenever chat opened, which is a different design and not a data binding.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **D-91** | **The orbit nodes' position and scale are quantised to whole pixels and two decimal places.** A sub-pixel departure from the demo, which writes fractional values.                                                                                                                                                                          | A 12px target whose box changes on every frame cannot be clicked reliably by assistive tooling or by a browser's own click stabilisation, and Playwright refuses it outright — which is how the orb's own nodes went untested through Phase 5A. The rendered result is identical at any zoom a person uses.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **D-92** | **A MOCK AI routing configuration is activated for development and end-to-end runs**, through the real Configuration Service with no validation relaxed, no real provider chosen and no credential resolved. The mock answers from retrieved knowledge outside production.                                                                  | Brand Brain chat had never been observed to produce an answer outside a unit test: with no active routing rule the gateway raises `RoutingError` and the panel shows the generic failure, correctly. A customer-facing capability that exists only in unit tests is not delivered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **D-93** | **Images are not a supported source format.** PNG and JPEG are removed from the `brand-brain` upload allow-list and no extractor claims them.                                                                                                                                                                                               | The only way to get text out of an image is OCR, and no option clears this feature's bar: the maintained JavaScript engine fetches its language model over the network at run time, its output is not reproducible across versions — which D-65 requires — and its Arabic accuracy is far below its Latin accuracy, so a bilingual product would be writing mistranscribed Arabic into approved knowledge. Refusing the upload is the honest answer until an option exists without those properties.                                                                                                                      |
| **D-94** | **Word and PowerPoint are read with a bounded ZIP reader and an element scan, not a document-conversion library; PDF is read with Mozilla's pdf.js (Apache-2.0), configured so it cannot compile, cannot fetch and cannot exceed its budget.**                                                                                              | The parser IS the attack surface for a file a stranger uploaded. A conversion library brings styles, numbering, image handling and HTML generation — all running on hostile bytes — to produce prose whose formatting we then discard. The smallest thing that does the job is a size- and ratio-checked inflate plus a scan for one element, and it makes XXE structurally impossible because no XML parser exists to attack.                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **D-95** | **Queue definitions move to `packages/jobs`, and Brand Brain ingestion runs on the `media-processing` queue.** The dashboard writes the job row and dispatches; `apps/worker` consumes and parses.                                                                                                                                          | The definitions lived inside `apps/worker`, where an app may not import them — so a producer had nowhere to dispatch from, and Phase 5A parsed documents inside a server action by default rather than by choice. Inline parsing holds a request connection for the length of a parse, lets one slow document delay every other request on the instance, and loses the work on a deploy with nothing recording that it was lost.                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **D-96** | **Background maintenance is scheduled in `apps/api`, not in the worker.** The reconciliation sweep and the retention purge enumerate across tenants; the per-tenant work is dispatched to the worker or performed inside that tenant's own RLS context.                                                                                     | Both sweeps begin with a cross-tenant question, and F-07 and the architecture keep the platform identity out of "ordinary workers". Putting the enumeration on the designated platform surface keeps the boundary intact instead of widening it for a maintenance job's convenience.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **D-97** | **The development and test object store is backed by the local filesystem, not by process memory.** `createObjectStore` returns a filesystem store outside production, rooted at `BRANDSPACE_OBJECT_STORE_DIR` (default: a directory under the system temporary path). Production still refuses until a real storage adapter is configured. | D-95 split the producer from the consumer: the dashboard accepts the upload and `apps/worker` reads the bytes back in a DIFFERENT process. A `Map` in the dashboard heap is invisible to the worker, so every dispatched ingestion failed on a file that had just been written — the defect the end-to-end run caught only once the queue was actually taken. Keys are split into path segments and each is refused unless it matches a narrow alphabet, so a key can never address a directory outside the root; writes stage and rename, so a worker woken the instant the row commits cannot read a half-written file. |

### Phase 5B-1 — Asset Library (D-98 … D-109)

| ID        | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Reasoning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-98**  | **A required screen with no approved reference is BUILT from the design system and recorded as an approved DESIGN-SYSTEM EXTENSION**, not stopped on and not invented freely. `CLAUDE.md` §4.2 and `docs/UI-FIDELITY-CONTRACT.md` §6 state the rule; `/[locale]/assets` is the first entry in its manifest.                     | The demo route for Media library is `simpleFeaturePage('media')` — a "Future product preview" kicker, a heading, one sentence and three identical placeholder cards. There is nothing to port, and §1 rule 2 as written invited two wrong readings: invent a screen, which rule 3 forbids, or refuse to build a required module until the owner designs it, which makes missing artwork an indefinite blocker on functional delivery. Composing from tokens, typography, spacing, surfaces, controls, drawers and states that already ship keeps the screen unmistakably part of this product; the cost is that it looks slightly generic, which a parity pass can correct and a screen with its own colour family cannot. |
| **D-99**  | **Every intra-Asset-Library foreign key is COMPOSITE with `workspaceId`** — the folder tree, the folder an asset sits in, and the asset a version, derivative, session or job belongs to.                                                                                                                                       | PostgreSQL performs referential integrity checks WITH RLS BYPASSED, so a plain key lets a tenant reference a row it cannot read. The isolation suite asserted that a tenant could not nest a folder under another tenant folder and GOT A CREATED ROW. Nothing is disclosed directly, but the insert succeeds only when the id names a real row, which turns any reference column into an existence oracle over the whole platform — the "infer" leak CLAUDE.md §2.1 names. Pairing each reference with the workspace collapses it to "is this a row in my own workspace".                                                                                                                                                 |
| **D-100** | **Duplicate protection is a PARTIAL unique index over the live rows** — `(workspaceId, checksumSha256) WHERE "deletedAt" IS NULL` — beside the plain index the model declares.                                                                                                                                                  | A plain index detects nothing on its own, and a full unique constraint would mean a customer who deleted a file could never upload it again, because the soft-deleted row keeps the checksum forever. The partial index refuses two live copies even when two requests race past the service check, and stops reserving the checksum the moment the row is deleted. Prisma cannot express a partial index, so it is raw SQL; `prisma migrate diff` does not model one, so there is no drift — verified against a fresh migrations-only database rather than assumed.                                                                                                                                                       |
| **D-101** | **A workspace-level asset (NULL `brandId`) is a first-class shape, not a degenerate one.**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | docs/DATABASE.md §4.6 says "null = workspace-level", and a multi-brand company's logo pack, font files and legal boilerplate genuinely belong to the workspace rather than to one of its brands. MATCH SIMPLE exempts the null case from the composite key by construction, so no special case was written — and the isolation suite asserts BOTH halves, because an exemption nobody tested is an exemption nobody can rely on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **D-102** | **`asset_version` is append-only in three separated layers** — revoked privilege, FORCE RLS with no owner policy, and a trigger.                                                                                                                                                                                                | The same construction as `brand_knowledge_version`, for a sharper reason: restoring a version promises the customer "this is exactly the file you uploaded", and "exactly" is only true if nothing could have edited the row in between. The suite reaches the trigger by granting the privilege back exactly as a careless migration would, because the first two layers otherwise hide whether the third still works.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| **D-103** | **The download path is an opaque, HMAC-signed, time-limited GRANT** — never a filesystem path and never a provider object key. Forged, expired, malformed and wrong-workspace all refuse identically as `NOT_FOUND`.                                                                                                            | docs/SECURITY.md §11.6-11.7 requires a signed short-TTL URL and forbids serving from the application origin, and both are properties of the CONTRACT rather than of a vendor. Distinguishing "expired" from "forged" confirms the token was once real, which confirms the object exists. A real adapter redeems the same grant by issuing its own pre-signed URL; the shape does not change, which is the point of putting the boundary in `packages/storage`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| **D-104** | **The object store moved to `@brandspace/storage`, and Brand Brain re-exports it.**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | It lived in `packages/brand-brain` while Brand Brain was its only consumer. Two copies of a storage driver is how one product ends up with two key layouts, two safety checks and two answers to "may production run without a real adapter". The file was moved, not rewritten, so no caller changed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **D-105** | **Production FAILS CLOSED until a real storage adapter is configured.** `createObjectStore()` returns the filesystem-backed store only outside production; with `NODE_ENV=production` and no `integrations.storage` adapter it throws rather than writing customer files to a container disk that the next deployment discards. | No storage vendor is approved (F-77), and the alternative to failing closed is pretending an upload is durable when it is not. A customer who uploads a logo and finds it gone after a deploy has lost data the product promised to keep. Refusing the upload is recoverable; accepting it and losing it is not.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **D-106** | **Virus scanning ships with a DETERMINISTIC MOCK and nothing else, and production fails closed the same way.** `MockVirusScanner` recognises the EICAR test string and a small set of planted markers; it makes no claim about real malware. No scanner runs in production until one is selected and reviewed (F-78).           | A scanner is a network service or a native binary with its own update channel, and choosing one is a security, licensing and operations decision the owner has not made. A mock that returns CLEAN for everything in production would be worse than no scanner at all, because the quarantine gate would report a file as scanned and safe.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **D-107** | **Derivative RECORDS exist; derivative BYTES do not.** `ENCODABLE_KINDS` is empty, so `planDerivatives()` plans nothing and the pipeline writes no thumbnails. The `asset_derivative` model, its RLS, its bounds and its lifecycle are complete and tested.                                                                     | Generating a thumbnail means decoding untrusted image bytes, which is the single most exploited surface in a media pipeline. Every candidate is a native dependency needing a maintenance, licence, security and offline-installation review that this task did not carry out (F-79). The schema is built so adding an encoder later is a configuration and worker change, not a migration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **D-108** | **SVG is NOT in the allowed-type list.** It is absent from `AssetKind` detection and from the signature table, so an upload named `.svg` is refused with the same mismatch error any other undetectable type gets.                                                                                                              | SVG is an executable document: it carries script, external references and entity expansion. Accepting it means either sanitising it — another unreviewed dependency — or serving active content from the product's own origin. Refusing it is the honest position until the owner decides sanitisation is worth the surface.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **D-109** | **The tenant entitlement-catalogue projection is PARSED before it reaches the precedence engine.** `TenantCatalogueSource.load()` now runs `parseConfigPayload(domain, row?.payload ?? {})` rather than handing the raw JSON column through.                                                                                    | The projection is a JSON column, so a document written before a schema field existed — or by a fixture that omitted an optional array — reached `precedence.ts` with `undefined` where an array was expected and crashed on `.includes`. The platform-side reader already parsed; the tenant-side reader did not, which meant the two identities disagreed about the shape of the same document. Parsing on both sides makes schema defaults the single answer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

### F-80 / F-83 corrective pass — Brand Brain composite foreign keys (D-112 … D-114)

| ID        | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Made by / source                                                        | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-112** | **D-99 is not an Asset Library rule; it is the platform rule.** Every foreign key from a tenant-owned table to another tenant-owned table is composite with `workspaceId`, whichever module it lives in. All EIGHT Phase 5A keys — the three F-80 named and the five F-83 found — are now composite, and `brand_source_document`, `brand_brain_conversation` and `brand_knowledge_item` carry the `@@unique([workspaceId, id])` they reference. The rule is enforced by a test that asks the CATALOGUE whether any single-column foreign key to a tenant-owned parent remains, rather than by a list somebody maintains. | F-80 corrective pass                                                    | D-99 was written while designing the Asset Library and reads as a property of that module. It is not: the mechanism it defends against — referential integrity evaluated with RLS bypassed — is a property of PostgreSQL, so every module inherits it. Stating it once, generally, is what stops the next module rediscovering it the way Phase 5B-1 rediscovered it in Phase 5A.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **D-113** | **A migration that VALIDATES tenant data lifts `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` for the duration of its own transaction, and asserts it back on before committing.** The F-80 migration does this for the five Brand Brain tables it touches.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | F-80 corrective pass (measured against a real PostgreSQL, not reasoned) | These tables are FORCE RLS with policies for `brandspace_app` and `brandspace_platform` only, and migrations run as the OWNER, `brandspace_migrator`, which no policy names. The owner therefore sees ZERO ROWS — so a pre-flight anti-join returns 0 offending rows however many exist, and, far worse, `ALTER TABLE … ADD CONSTRAINT FOREIGN KEY` validates only the visible rows and still marks the constraint `convalidated = true`. The migration would have reported success over data that violates it. `ALTER TABLE` holds an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock to commit, so no other session can observe the lifted state, and a `DO` block refuses to commit unless all five tables are ENABLED and FORCED again. This is PostgreSQL's own prescribed remedy — `SET LOCAL row_security = off` fails here with a HINT naming it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **D-114** | **A composite `ON DELETE SET NULL` NAMES THE COLUMN IT NULLS.** Every workspace-scoped key whose action is `SET NULL` is written `ON DELETE SET NULL ("<reference column>")`, never bare, so a referential action can never write the tenant key. Three keys use it: `brand_knowledge_candidate.targetItemId`, `brand_knowledge_item.sourceDocumentId` and `brand_knowledge_item.conflictsWithItemId`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | F-83 (measured against a real PostgreSQL)                               | Making a key composite changes what `SET NULL` means. PostgreSQL nulls EVERY referencing column of the key, so `(workspaceId, targetItemId)` would null `workspaceId` too — and because `workspaceId` is `NOT NULL` the parent delete does not corrupt the tenant key, it FAILS: deleting a knowledge item that any candidate targets, or a source document any item cites, would have started erroring on a path that worked the day before. A leak fix that introduces a data-loss-shaped defect is not a fix. The column list (PostgreSQL 15+; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` targets 16+ and CI runs 16) restricts the nulling to the one nullable reference. Prisma has no syntax for it, so `prisma validate` warns "`onDelete: SetNull` … when a referenced field is required" on these three relations — that warning is Prisma describing the SQL IT would emit, which is precisely why the migration is hand-written. `prisma migrate diff` does not model `confdelsetcols`, so there is no drift (same precedent as D-100's partial index), verified against a fresh migrations-only database rather than assumed. The migration itself refuses to commit if any such key lacks a single-column list or names `workspaceId`. |

### Phase 5B-2 — AI Content Studio + Content Calendar (D-115 … D-120)

D-115 to D-117 were approved by the product owner on 2026-09-15. D-118 to D-120 are implementation
decisions taken while building the calendar, recorded here because each one closes a question a
reader of the schema would otherwise have to re-derive.

| ID        | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Made by / source                    | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-115** | **ARABIC DIALECT IS A CONFIGURED WORKSPACE/BRAND ATTRIBUTE, DEFAULTING TO MSA.** Resolves U-11. Dialect resolves brand → workspace → the activated default, which ships as **Modern Standard Arabic**. Dialect-specific generation is supported for at minimum **Gulf/Saudi, Egyptian and Levantine**. Translation and rewriting preserve the workspace glossary, terminology, brand voice and the configured dialect — never a literal translation. **No dialect is hard-coded as the global default, and Saudi in particular is not.**    | Product Owner, 2026-09-15 (U-11)    | The dialect a brand speaks is a property of that brand, not of the platform, and not of where the platform happens to be sold. The supported list and the default both live in the activated `content` configuration domain rather than in an enum, so adding a dialect is an operator change and not a migration (CLAUDE.md §2.2) — and so that the default cannot silently become whichever dialect a developer had in mind. A dialect a workspace has not configured is MSA, which is the form every Arabic reader can read. |
| **D-116** | **CONTENT IS RETAINED WHILE THE SUBSCRIPTION IS ACTIVE, THEN FOR A 30-DAY GRACE PERIOD.** Resolves U-08. Drafts, generated variants and published history are retained while the workspace subscription is active; after cancellation they are retained for a configured grace window (default 30 days) and then purged under the platform retention policy. Records with their own statutory or operational retention — audit events, billing, the credit ledger — are **never** touched by this path. Purge is tenant-scoped and audited. | Product Owner, 2026-09-15 (U-08)    | A customer's drafts are the work they paid for; deleting them on a timer while they are still paying would be indefensible. A grace period after cancellation is the window in which people actually export. The carve-out is not a convenience: the ledger is a financial record and the audit log is a security record, and a content-retention control that could erase either would be a control that erases evidence.                                                                                                      |
| **D-117** | **AI OUTPUT PERSISTENCE IS A WORKSPACE-LEVEL SETTING, ENFORCED SERVER-SIDE.** Resolves F-73. Settings carries a workspace control over how long persisted AI output is kept; the **service** applies it, never the screen. Every feature that persists generated output must declare a **retention owner** and its retention behaviour in a registry, and a test fails the build if one does not. AI Content Studio obeys the setting. Audit, billing, credit and security records are outside its reach.                                   | Product Owner, 2026-09-15 (F-73)    | §11 of `docs/SECURITY.md` describes opt-in retention, and a per-feature notice is not a setting. A control enforced only in the UI is not a control — the next caller is an API client. The registry exists because the failure mode is silent: a future feature that persists output and forgets to declare an owner leaves customer content with nobody responsible for deleting it, and nothing else in the system would notice.                                                                                             |
| **D-118** | **THE CALENDAR SLOT IS THE _WHEN_; `ContentItem` REMAINS THE SOURCE OF TRUTH.** `calendar_slot` carries a time, a zone, a target and a link, and nothing `content_item` already answers — no caption, no status copy, no channel list beyond the plan's own record of what was planned. Scheduling moves the item to `SCHEDULED` and cancelling moves it back, in the SAME transaction as the slot write, and `ContentLibraryService.transition()` refuses to move a scheduled item at all.                                                 | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15 | Two rows that both claim to know whether something is on the calendar will disagree the first time one is updated without the other. Making the slot the only writer of that edge — in both directions — means the disagreement is not expressible. The alternative, a `scheduledAt` column on the item, cannot carry the local intent AC-14.2 requires, and cannot be cancelled without losing the audit trail.                                                                                                                |
| **D-119** | **A SCHEDULED TIME STORES THE INTENT, NOT ONLY THE INSTANT.** `scheduledLocalTime` (a `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm` wall-clock, TEXT) and `timezone` (IANA, copied from the workspace) sit beside `scheduledAtUtc`. A **skipped** wall-clock resolves FORWARD to the instant the clock jumps to; an **ambiguous** one resolves to the EARLIER occurrence and reports that it was ambiguous. `packages/content/src/timezone.ts` derives all of it from `Intl`, with no date library.                                                                    | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15 | AC-14.3 asks for the correct local time "across a DST boundary", and a single timestamp cannot express "09:00, whenever that is": 09:00 converted to UTC in January and read back in July is an hour out. Copying the zone rather than joining it means a workspace that relocates does not silently move every post it already scheduled. The two DST resolutions are the conventions every calendar application follows, and are written down because picking either silently is how a post goes out an hour late.            |
| **D-120** | **THE APPROVAL-BEFORE-SCHEDULING GATE SHIPS OFF.** AC-14.6's gate is implemented and tested — with `content.calendar.requireApprovalBeforeScheduling` on, only an `APPROVED` item may be scheduled — and the activated default is `false`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15 | Nothing in Phase 5B-2 can move an item into `APPROVED`: the Approvals workflow is 5B-3. A default of `true` would make the calendar unusable while appearing to enforce a policy nobody can satisfy, which is worse than an honest `false`. Building the gate now and leaving it off is what lets 5B-3 turn it on as a configuration change rather than a migration.                                                                                                                                                            |

---

### Phase 5B-3 — Approvals, Command Center, Activity Log, Notifications (D-121 … D-125)

D-121 and D-122 were approved by the product owner on 2026-09-15. D-123 to D-125 are implementation
decisions taken while building the milestone, recorded here because each one closes a question a
reader of the schema would otherwise have to re-derive.

| ID        | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Made by / source                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **D-121** | ~~**VIEWER APPROVAL IS A PER-BRAND POLICY, OFF BY DEFAULT.**~~ **SUPERSEDED BY D-130 (D-62 is authoritative) — never shipped as active behaviour.** It would have made `docs/SECURITY.md` §4.3's "🟡 optional client approval" cell real via `approval_policy.clientApprovalEnabled`. Withdrawn before merge: see D-130 and `docs/SECURITY.md` §26.9.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Product Owner, 2026-09-15 (U-06); **withdrawn 2026-09-15**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | The reasoning was sound about HOW to grant such a right — from the brand rather than the permission bag — and wrong that the right should exist at all in the MVP. D-62 had already excluded client portals, hand-off workflows and agency-specific surfaces, and an approval grant for a "client viewer" is a client-review workflow by another name. The corrective pass then spent effort making an unreachable feature reachable, which was the wrong repair for a feature that should not have been there.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **D-122** | **SELF-APPROVAL IS CONFIGURABLE AND DENIED BY DEFAULT.** A member holding `content.approve` may not approve content they authored **or** submitted, unless `approval_policy.allowSelfApproval` is switched on for that brand. The rule is enforced in the service, and the policy in force is snapshotted onto every approval it affects.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Product Owner, 2026-09-15                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | An approval whose approver is its author is not evidence of a second pair of eyes, and the whole value of the record is that it is evidence. It is a DEFAULT rather than a law because a one-person workspace would otherwise be unable to move its own content past review at all — a safety property that becomes a deadlock stops being a safety property. Both the author and the requester are barred, because an author who asks a colleague to submit on their behalf would otherwise approve their own words.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **D-123** | **NOTIFICATIONS ARE IN-APP ONLY IN THIS MILESTONE.** `notification.channel` declares `IN_APP`, `EMAIL`, `SMS`, `WHATSAPP` and `PUSH` because `docs/DATABASE.md` §9.3 designs them, and a database CHECK pins every row to `IN_APP`. External delivery is Phase 10 launch hardening.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `docs/ROADMAP.md` scope item 8 says "in-app + email", but no mail transport exists anywhere in this platform — there is nothing to send with. A `channel` column that accepted `EMAIL` would be a row claiming a delivery that never happened, and a preferences screen offering it would be a promise the product cannot keep. The CHECK is what makes the limitation a fact rather than a convention, and removing it is the visible act that admits a sender.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **D-124** | **THE ACTIVITY LOG IS A READ MODEL OVER `audit_event`, AND ADDS NO TABLE.** The customer screen reads the append-only audit trail directly, scoped by a graded permission, and renders actor, action, resource and outcome — never the `before`/`after` diffs.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | A customer-facing event table beside the audit trail would be two records of the same facts, drifting apart at the first writer that updated one and forgot the other — and a MUTABLE one, which is exactly what AC-15.7 and the append-only trigger exist to prevent. The diffs are withheld because "redacted" is a property of every past and future writer having got it right, and the customer screen should not have to depend on that; `docs/SECURITY.md` §4.4's platform surface is where diffs are read.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **D-125** | **THE ACTIVITY LOG GRADES `audit.read` RATHER THAN REPLACING IT.** `audit.read_workspace` sits above the existing `audit.read` and `audit.read_own` below it, matching §4.3's four grades. Scope is resolved from PERMISSIONS, never from the role key, and the most privileged key wins.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `audit.read` has existed since Phase 1 and already carried the ✅ and 🟡-brand rows; a parallel `activity.*` family would have been a second permission for the same capability, which is how two answers to one question get shipped. Deriving the grade from the role KEY instead would make the permission bag a lie: a role in this platform IS its permissions, and a capability that is secretly a hidden role check cannot be reasoned about, overridden, or safely extended by a role added later.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| **D-126** | **A REVIEW CYCLE IS JUDGED BY THE POLICY IT WAS OPENED UNDER.** `approval.policySnapshot` is READ at decision time, not merely written at submit time: `allowSelfApproval` and `clientApprovalEnabled` come from the snapshot, so flipping a brand's policy does not retroactively change what an OPEN cycle permits. A new cycle carries the new policy. **Membership, role and permissions stay CURRENT** — authority somebody has lost is not preserved by the snapshot. The scheduling gate is deliberately excluded and read live, because it governs the calendar rather than this decision.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | The snapshot was being written and then ignored, which made it a decoration: a workspace admin could submit their own content, flip `allowSelfApproval` on, approve it, and the row would still carry a snapshot saying self-approval had not been allowed — a record contradicting the decision it records. The snapshot is what the requester was promised when they asked for review, so it is what the verdict is judged against. Keeping identity current is the other half: a historical policy must never become a way to keep authority a person no longer has.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **D-127** | **AN ASSIGNED REVIEW IS THAT PERSON'S TO DECIDE, AND THE ASSIGNEE IS VALIDATED BEFORE IT IS PERSISTED.** `assignedToUserId` must name an ACTIVE member whose BrandScope covers the brand and who holds approval authority for it (`content.approve`; under D-130 there is no other source). `decide()` refuses anybody else while an assignment stands. An unassigned review may be decided by any eligible reviewer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | The column was written straight from the form and then ignored: any uuid at all became the assignee — a member of another brand, a suspended member, somebody with no review authority — and the verdict path did not consult the field, so the screen said a review was assigned to a person who could never act on it while anybody else could. Recording an intention the system does not honour is worse than not offering it, so assignment is either real or absent. It is real.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **D-128** | **AN APPROVAL IS WRITE-ONCE, AND THE APPLICATION ROLE CANNOT DELETE ONE.** DELETE on `approval` and `approval_policy` is revoked from `brandspace_app`, and a database trigger makes a decided or withdrawn cycle immutable and a cycle's identity — subject, requester, round and policy snapshot — immutable throughout. The legitimate `PENDING` → terminal transition is untouched. The PLATFORM role keeps DELETE, for tenant offboarding and the D-116 retention purge.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | An approval is the record that somebody reviewed something; erasing or rewriting one removes the evidence the module exists to provide. The first migration's own comment said `notification` was "the only one of the three the application may DELETE" and then granted DELETE on all three, and UPDATE was unrestricted — so raw application-role SQL could reopen a decided cycle, change its verdict or replace the snapshot it was judged under. Test fixtures now clean up as the PLATFORM role rather than the production grant being widened to make cleanup convenient, which would have handed every workspace admin the same power.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **D-129** | **A SCREEN MAY ONLY OFFER WHAT THE SERVER WILL HONOUR.** Every affordance that stands for a server-authorized action is computed from the SAME inputs the action enforces — for an approval that means the cycle’s `policySnapshot` (D-126), its assignee (D-127) and the D-122 self rule, resolved through the exported `policyFromSnapshot` rather than re-derived.
