The approvals queue and `reviewSubject().mayDecide` both answer this way. This is a USABILITY AND HONESTY rule, never a security one: the server remains the only authority, and UI that withholds a button is never how an action is denied (CLAUDE.md §2.1, and the standing rule that no endpoint relies on UI hiding).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Correcting D-126 server-side left the screen reading the brand’s LIVE policy: flipping `allowSelfApproval` on rendered an Approve button for a cycle snapshotted under the stricter rule, and pressing it earned a refusal. `reviewSubject()` had the same shape, answering `mayDecide` from the permission and status alone, so it offered the button to the submitter and to a non-assignee. A control that refuses the person who uses it teaches the rule by denial and reads as a bug; worse, it invites the reader to conclude the rule is arbitrary. The divergence is now closed at both surfaces and pinned by tests that fail if either drifts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| **D-130** | **VIEWER (READ-ONLY) IS STRICTLY READ-ONLY IN THE MVP, AND NOTHING CAN LIFT IT. D-121 IS WITHDRAWN; D-62 IS AUTHORITATIVE.** `client_viewer` stays exactly `['workspace.read']` and can never pass `mayApproveForBrand` — which now takes **only** a permission list, so no role key or brand policy has a channel to reach it. `/approvals` and its actions require `content.read` again; `reviewSubject()` requires it too, closing the bypass that existed only for a D-121 Viewer. `clientApprovalEnabled` is unpatchable in the service type, `z.literal(false)` in configuration, and pinned by the `approval_policy_client_approval_withdrawn` CHECK. Existing `true` rows are normalized; `approval.policySnapshot` is deliberately NOT rewritten, because an approval records what actually happened and nothing reads a snapshot for authority. **The capability is DEFERRED, not deleted:** a future **External Review / Guest Approval** actor — narrow, distinct, and NOT a repurposed customer role — is the intended home, and the structure that would serve it (approval cycles and rounds, assignment, policy snapshots, immutable history, subject types, notes, the notification architecture, the reserved column) is kept unchanged. Nothing of that capability is built now.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Product owner, 2026-09-15 (product-architecture correction)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | D-62 already excluded client portals, client hand-off and agency-specific surfaces from the MVP, and an approval right for a "client viewer" is a client-review workflow by another name — so D-121 contradicted an existing product decision rather than extending one. The product has no surface for an external reviewer to act in, which is the real reason: a grant with nowhere to live is a liability, not a feature. Withdrawing it at EVERY layer rather than defaulting one flag off is the same standard the rest of this milestone was held to — a flag left off is a setting, and the point is that there is no setting. Removing the parameters from `mayApproveForBrand` matters more than any single check: a rule that cannot see a role key or a brand setting cannot be talked into honouring one by a later change.                                                                                               |
| **D-131** | **D-112 IS NOW APPLIED PLATFORM-WIDE, AND THE SCHEMA IS MACHINE-CHECKED AGAINST IT.** The cross-phase audit parsed every `@relation` in the schema and found five relationships from Phase 3 and Phase 4 still referencing a tenant-owned parent by id alone — `credit_transaction`, `credit_grant` and `credit_reservation` onto `credit_wallet`, and `ai_usage_ledger` onto `ai_request` and onto itself. All five are now composite on `workspaceId`. **`role` is the documented exception**: its tenant key is NULLABLE, because a system role is shared by every workspace, so a composite key is impossible by construction — `membership` and `invitation` are protected by the `app.role_reference_is_workspace_scoped()` trigger instead, which refuses any role that is neither a system role nor this workspace's own. **The invariant is enforced by the CATALOGUE, not by a list:** `tests/isolation/d112-platform-wide-invariant.test.ts` reads the authoritative tenant-owned model registry the D-29 gate uses, asks PostgreSQL for every single-column foreign key between two tenant-owned tables, and allows exactly two structural exceptions — the `workspace` anchor and `role`, whose exclusion is itself backed by an assertion that the trigger exists. A Phase 6 table adding a plain tenant-parent key fails on the day it is written. **AMENDED BY D-133**, which corrects the trigger's definition of a workspace role and makes the gate's exemptions exact.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15 (Final UI Polish & Cross-Phase Audit)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | D-112 was written as a platform-wide rule while fixing Brand Brain (F-80/F-83) and then applied only there, which is how a rule becomes a local fix. Two of the five were demonstrated before the fix: from inside workspace A, a `credit_transaction` naming workspace B's wallet and an `ai_usage_ledger` row naming workspace B's request were both ACCEPTED. The credit keys are the worse pair — they attach a MONEY LEDGER row to another tenant's wallet. The application never does this, because it resolves the wallet from the workspace and never accepts a wallet id from input, and that is precisely why it survived: §2.1 requires two independent layers and only one was holding. The `role` case is the same shape one layer up — the application refuses it and the database did not, while permissions resolve straight through `membership.roleId`.                                                              |
| **D-132** | **BRANDSCOPE IS A QUERY PREDICATE EVERYWHERE, NEVER A POST-RETRIEVAL FILTER.** A module that returns tenant rows to a scoped caller takes `brandScope` and applies it through `brandIdScopeFilter` inside the `where`. `ContentCalendarService.listSlots`/`monthView` and `ContentLibraryService.listItems`, `countsByStatus` and `getItem` now do; the calendar page no longer filters in JavaScript, and neither does the composer's item read. **An explicit `brandId` and the authorization scope INTERSECT**, through the single helper `brandIdQueryFilter`, which can only emit an `AND`. Empty or absent remains UNRESTRICTED, as `brandInScope()` has meant since Phase 2B. **EXTENDED BY D-134** to the mutation paths, and machine-enforced over the source. Cross-Phase Audit)                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | The calendar fetched the workspace's whole month and dropped out-of-scope brands afterwards. RLS kept another TENANT's rows out so nothing leaked across workspaces, but two things followed. First, the same defect the Activity Log was corrected for: rows the reader may not see were fetched on their behalf, so any count or page boundary over that list is a boundary over invisible rows. Second, and actually a bug a customer could hit: `listItems` applies `limit` IN THE DATABASE, so filtering afterwards filtered a page that had already been truncated — a member scoped to one brand, in a workspace whose newest 200 drafts belong to another, was shown nothing to schedule and told it was empty. Filtering late is not a style preference; it is how a scoped member loses their own data.                                                                                                                      |
| **D-133** | **A WORKSPACE ROLE IS DEFINED BY ITS REALM, NOT BY A NULL WORKSPACE — AND D-112'S GATE IS EXACT.** `app.role_reference_is_workspace_scoped()` refuses any role whose `realm <> 'WORKSPACE'`, **regardless of `workspaceId`**, and only then applies the same-workspace rule to custom roles. `membership` and `invitation` may therefore reference ONLY a WORKSPACE-realm system role or a WORKSPACE-realm custom role of their own workspace. The migration's pre-flight asks the same question and REFUSES rather than repairing. The D-112 catalogue gate is likewise tightened: exemptions are exact `child.constraint -> parent` entries whose continued existence is asserted, not a parent-wide pass for `role`; and a composite key is checked by pairing `conkey` with `confkey` **by ordinal**, so it must map the child `workspaceId` to the PARENT `workspaceId` rather than merely contain a column of that name.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Implementation decision, 2026-09-15 (Final UI Polish & Cross-Phase Audit)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | D-131 read `role."workspaceId" IS NULL` as "system role". It is not: every PLATFORM role carries a NULL workspace too, and `role`'s RLS policy makes all of them VISIBLE inside a tenant context. Customer sessions build `permissionKeys` straight from `membership.role.permissions` with no realm check, so a membership bound to `platform_owner` would have carried the whole platform permission set into a customer session. Demonstrated, not inferred: an invitation naming `platform_owner` was ACCEPTED from inside a tenant context. The gate changes come from the same lesson one level up — a parent-wide exemption pre-authorises the next relationship to that parent, and a name-only composite check passes a key that maps `workspaceId` to something else entirely.                                                                                                                                               |

Assets use `assetBrandScopeFilter`, which emits `OR [brandId IS NULL, brandId IN (…)]` so a workspace-level asset stays visible to every member. An empty scope remains UNRESTRICTED. **The rule is machine-enforced:** `tests/unit/brand-scope-predicate-gate.test.ts` fails if any brand handed to a scope assertion is read off a row the service just fetched, anywhere in `packages/`. `assertBrandInScope` throws a NOT_FOUND by design (F-74) — and was not. Measured on the defective form, a real out-of-scope content id answered `NOT_FOUND: Brand not found` and a fabricated one `NOT_FOUND: Content not found.`: two different messages for what CLAUDE.md §2.1 requires to be one answer, which let a brand-restricted member enumerate which ids were real. Three consecutive reviews found this pattern in code the previous review had just corrected, each time somewhere new, so the enforcement moved from a list of fixed sites to a check over the source.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **D-135** | **PHASE 6 SHIPS AGAINST DETERMINISTIC MOCK PROVIDERS, AND SAYS SO.** Every security property — OAuth state, PKCE, token encryption, RLS, composite keys, idempotency, the approval gate, retry classification — is real and proven against real PostgreSQL. The five platform adapters are mocks. `createConnectorRegistry` REFUSES to return a mock in a PRODUCTION environment, so a deployment with no real connector fails at resolution rather than accepting publish jobs and storing external ids that point at nothing. app review before it issues a production credential (D-18, D-19), an owner-driven process measured in weeks. A milestone that could not be tested until that finished would be a milestone tested by nobody. Shipping an adapter that has never been run against the real API would be worse: it would claim an integration that does not exist. The mocks model rejection, rate limiting, expiry, revocation and an indeterminate timeout, because those are the paths correctness rests on — a mock that only succeeded would test the one case that was never in doubt.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| **D-136** | **THE ENVELOPE-ENCRYPTION PRIMITIVES MOVE TO `@brandspace/vault`, AND CUSTOMER TOKENS GET THEIR OWN KEY DOMAIN.** `crypto.ts` and `key-provider.ts` leave `@brandspace/secrets` for a package holding no table, no service and no policy; `@brandspace/secrets` re-exports them unchanged and its F-07 import restriction is untouched. Customer OAuth tokens are encrypted under `SOCIAL_TOKEN_VAULT_KEK`, a SEPARATE key from `SECRET_VAULT_KEK`. Implementation decision, 2026-09-16 (Phase 6) that service can decrypt platform provider credentials. The alternative was a second implementation of envelope encryption inside the connector package, and a second implementation of a cipher is a second place for it to be wrong. The KEYS are separate for a sharper reason: the worker holds the social KEK and has database access, so a shared key would let it unwrap every platform provider credential in the database — the exact reach F-07 exists to deny it. One key, one blast radius.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **D-137** | **PUBLISH JOBS ARE MATERIALISED WHEN A SLOT FALLS DUE, FROM THE BRAND ACTIVE CONNECTIONS, ONE JOB PER (SLOT, CONNECTION, VARIANT).** Targets are resolved at dispatch rather than pinned when the customer schedules. A connection publishes the variant written for ITS platform, and a slot with no matching variant produces no job. scheduling time would mean a post scheduled on Monday goes to an account disconnected on Tuesday, or fails to reach one reconnected on Wednesday. The customer intent is to publish this brand plan, not to publish to four row ids, so resolving at dispatch keeps the calendar a plan rather than a list of targets. The unit is the TRIPLE rather than the slot so one platform rejecting a caption does not fail the others and a retry retries only what failed. And a variant written for Instagram is never posted to LinkedIn merely because both are connected: the caption, the hashtags and the length were chosen for one platform.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **D-138** | **A SOCIAL CONNECTION BRAND IS NOT NULL.** Every other brand-scoped table in this schema allows a NULL brand to mean that the row belongs to the workspace rather than to a brand. A connection does not. BrandScope has to be expressible as a query predicate (D-132/D-134), and a row visible to every brand is exactly the hole a brand-restricted member would publish through — they would hold a credential for an account belonging to a brand they may not act on. Making it NOT NULL means the ordinary `brandIdQueryFilter` covers connections with no special case, which is the form least likely to be got wrong later.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **D-139** | **YOUTUBE IS NOT IN PHASE 6.** The provider set is Facebook, Instagram, TikTok, LinkedIn and X. YouTube remains a designed platform in `docs/SOCIAL-INTEGRATIONS.md` §3 and is not enumerated, adapted or configurable. among them. It is also the one platform in the design whose publishing model is materially different — a resumable video upload bounded by quota UNITS rather than request counts, with processing time before availability — so adding it as a fifth enum value with the same shape as the others would have declared a capability nobody had designed. It is a deliberate exclusion rather than an oversight, recorded here so a later reader does not restore it by assuming one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **D-140** | **INBOUND WEBHOOKS AND THE DEAD-LETTER/REPLAY SURFACE ARE DEFERRED OUT OF PHASE 6.** A job that exhausts its attempts is `FAILED` with its failure class and a manual retry where the class allows one; there is no dead-letter queue and no Control Center replay, and no webhook endpoint exists. Signature verification written against no signer would be untested code that looks tested — the same objection D-135 makes to a real adapter nobody has run. The dead-letter surface is a Control Center screen rather than a customer one, and it belongs beside the webhook administration it would sit next to. Both are recorded in `docs/ROADMAP.md` Phase 6 as not done, rather than being quietly counted as delivered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **D-141** | **THE OAUTH CALLBACK IS A PUBLIC `GET`, AND THE STATE IS THE IDENTITY.** `GET /v1/social/callback/:provider` carries `code` and `state` in the query string, resolves the workspace from the state row on the platform client, and answers only with a `303` back to `PUBLIC_DASHBOARD_BASE_URL` carrying one coarse word. It reads no session.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | The first version registered `POST /v1/social/callback` expecting a JSON body while handing every provider `/v1/social/callback/<provider>` — two independent reasons the redirect could never land, and nothing exercised it, so CI stayed green. The session cannot be the identity here and never could have been: `__Host-bs_customer_session` forbids a `Domain` attribute, so the browser will not send it to `api-staging.brandspace.cc` and must not — widening it to `.brandspace.cc` would hand every subdomain the session, which is exactly what the prefix prevents. The state already does this job: 32 CSPRNG bytes, stored hashed, single-use by conditional UPDATE, bound at creation to one workspace, one brand and one user. PKCE, exact redirect-URI matching and post-grant scope verification are unchanged, and every refusal — expired, replayed, forged, foreign, a failed exchange — redirects identically. |
| **D-142** | **A GRANT OFFERING SEVERAL TARGETS PAUSES FOR AN EXPLICIT CHOICE.** The exchanged token is sealed onto the authorization row, the offered targets are recorded, a single-use selection secret goes back through the browser, and no connection exists until the customer names a page. The choice requires the session, the permission, the brand scope and the person who started the flow, and must name a target from the recorded list. Meta returns every Page a person administers and LinkedIn every organization they can post as; "the first one" is an ordering accident of somebody else's API, and getting it wrong publishes a customer's scheduled posts, publicly, to the wrong page of their own. BrandSpace does not get to make that choice. A connection row IS a chosen target, so the token is held on the in-flight authorization instead — under the same envelope, AEAD and key domain a stored credential gets, because a pending grant is a live credential.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **D-143** | **THE PUBLISH PATH AND THE VERIFICATION PATH ARE DIFFERENT PATHS, AND A CLAIM HAS A LEASE.** `execute()` claims `QUEUED` only. `verify()` contains no call to `publish()` at all. A `PUBLISHING` job whose claim is older than `dispatch.claimLeaseSeconds` moves to `VERIFICATION_PENDING` and is asked about, never resent; a provider that cannot be asked waits for a person. `retry()` refuses `VERIFICATION_PENDING` and every indeterminate class. second review)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `execute()` claimed `QUEUED` OR `VERIFICATION_PENDING` and then ran into `publish()`, so a duplicate queue delivery re-sent a post whose outcome was deliberately recorded as unknown — the one outcome this pipeline exists to prevent. The Retry button reached the same place: `TIMEOUT` and `PLATFORM_UNAVAILABLE` are both indeterminate AND manually retryable, so two clicks in the product did it. And the opposite gap: the sweep dispatched only `QUEUED`, so a worker that died after moving a job to `PUBLISHING` left that row stalled for ever. Elapsed time is evidence that a worker is gone and evidence of nothing else, so the lease authorises a QUESTION, never a send.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

                                                                                                                                                                                                      | The first version asked `findFirst` and then called `create`. Two sweeps running together — which is the normal deployment, not an edge case — both see no row, both insert, and the unique index correctly refuses the second with `P2002`, aborting the loser's entire workspace pass so every later slot in that batch goes unmaterialised. The data was never wrong; the index was doing all the work and reporting it as a crash. Resolving the conflict inside PostgreSQL makes the counts exact under concurrency, which the `findFirst` answer never was.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **D-145** | **A METRIC IS A ROW, NOT A BLOB, AND ITS IDENTITY IS THE MEASUREMENT.** `metric_observation` holds one row per (connection, subject, metric, granularity, period), keyed by a sha256 `observationKey` unique per workspace. Writes are a single `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` whose conflict clause refuses a reading OLDER than the stored one.                                                                                                   | Implementation decision, 2026-09-16 (Phase 7) is the obvious shape and it makes every later question expensive: "engagement rate for this campaign over these two weeks" becomes a scan and a parse, and a provider revising yesterday's figures becomes a read-modify-write race. Rows make the aggregate a `GROUP BY` and the correction an upsert. The key excludes the run, the adapter version and the value deliberately — all three change between two fetches of the SAME measurement, and a key that included them would make the idempotency ornamental. The `observedAt` guard in the conflict clause is what stops a late redelivery rolling a figure backwards silently.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

                                                                                                                                   | Implementation decision, 2026-09-16 (Phase 7) "0% engagement" is a claim about PERFORMANCE; "we have nothing to divide by" is a claim about DATA. A product that renders the first when the second is true is lying quietly, and the customer acts on it. The six reasons exist because "no data" is not actionable and "this platform does not publish that figure" is. Enforced by the query service returning `MetricValue.absent` rather than by each screen remembering.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

| **D-148** | **EVIDENCE IS PERSISTED SEPARATELY FROM PROSE, AND A FABRICATED CITATION IS STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE.** An insight's numbers live in `insight_evidence` rows carrying the metric, the value, the unit, the window and the source; the UI renders figures from THOSE. The model is handed an ordinal-indexed evidence table and may cite only those ordinals; its prose may contain only numerals that appear in the evidence (Arabic-Indic digits folded). A violation refuses the whole generation — no degraded insight, no warning banner. A CHECK constraint refuses a METRIC evidence row with no measurement, so a citation pointing at nothing cannot exist even if a future call site tried to write one. `insight_evidence` is append-only by trigger and `DELETE` is revoked from the tenant role: a citation that can be rewritten or deleted is not evidence.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

The hash is what binds the confirmation to the plan a person actually read, so changing the plan invalidates the confirmation — and the freeze trigger closes the other door, where the plan changes and the confirmation is KEPT. The denial sink exists because every service here runs inside `withWorkspace`, which is one transaction: a refusal throws, the transaction rolls back, and an audit row written just before the throw goes with it. A replayed token is exactly the shape an attempted replay takes, and it must leave a trace.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

| **D-182** | **A RULE-DERIVED PRODUCER IS A FAIR QUEUE OVER A DURABLE CURSOR, NEVER AN UNORDERED `LIMIT`.** `automation_rule.nextEvaluationAt` (NOT NULL, defaulting to `now()`) orders both producers; each visited rule is parked in the SAME TRANSACTION that writes its event, so the two commit together and a crash simply leaves the rule due. A timed rule parks to `nextTimedEvaluationAt` — its real next occurrence, biased half an hour EARLY so a daylight-saving shift cannot push the wake past the window — capped at one hour so nothing is invisible for longer than the occurrence it waits for. A threshold rule parks to `now`, staying eligible while moving to the back. An OUTBOX ROW RETIRES AND A RULE DOES NOT, which is why `take: batch` was honest pagination in one place and permanent starvation in the other: past `batch` rules the same subset could return for ever, and a `SCHEDULED_TIME` hour nobody looked at is an occurrence missed by design. `NOT NULL` is deliberate — `ORDER BY … ASC` puts NULLs LAST in PostgreSQL, which would have sent every new rule to the back of the queue that exists to reach it. The same correction is applied to `sweepAnalytics`, whose nullable `nextAttemptAt` sorted never-attempted cursors last.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **D-183** | **A FORM DECODER FAILS CLOSED: INVALID INPUT NEVER BROADENS A RULE.** An unrecognised `conditionField` is refused with `VALIDATION_FAILED`; it used to become `[]`, so a stale or tampered request that meant a CONDITIONAL rule created an UNCONDITIONAL one — a rule that fires on everything instead of the narrow set somebody chose, stored, enabled and listed, with nothing saying the condition had been dropped. THREE INPUTS, THREE ANSWERS, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS A CHOICE: the entry PRESENT and exactly empty means "no condition", because that is the value the picker's own option carries and the picker always renders; the entry ABSENT is a request that never went through the screen and is refused — `String(formData.get(…) ?? '')` collapsed the two, which is the identical widening in the last shape left for it; anything else goes through the contract lookup. Whitespace is not folded into the empty string either, or a whitespace payload would inherit the same unconditional rule one character along. The lookup is `Object.hasOwn`, not a truthiness or `undefined` test: `CONDITION_FIELD_CONTRACTS['__proto__']` is `Object.prototype` and `['toString']` is a function, so an `undefined` check ADMITS three names that are not fields. The decoders live in `apps/dashboard/src/server/automation-form.ts` rather than inside the `'use server'` action, because a module that may only export async server actions cannot be asserted without a browser — and every rule here is about a request the screen would never produce.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **D-184** | **A REQUIRED PARAMETER IS REFUSED WHEN IT IS MISSING OR BLANK, NEVER COERCED; A DEFAULT EXISTS ONLY WHERE THE PRODUCT DECLARES ONE.** `Number('')` is `0`, and zero is a valid threshold, hour and platform count — so a blank input did not fail, it became a DIFFERENT RULE from the one being written, and one nobody would think to suspect. Required numbers must be present and finite (`NaN`, `Infinity` and `-Infinity` refused); required text must be present and non-empty; `?? 0` for the hour and `?? 'above'` for the direction are gone, because a control the screen renders is one a real submission always carries. The two genuine defaults stay, and stay in the REGISTRY: an untouched set of weekday checkboxes means every day (`.default([])`), and an ABSENT window means `.default(7)` — reached by omitting the key rather than repeating the number here. A window that is PRESENT and blank is still refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **D-185** | **THE OUTBOX AND THE ENGINE USE ONE IDENTITY FOR ONE EVENT, AND THE EVENT CARRIES IT.** `AutomationEvent.dedupeKey` travels as `EvaluateAutomationPayload.eventKey` → `TriggerEvent.eventKey` → the run key's discriminator, in the order `occurrence ?? eventKey ?? runBucketFor(...)`. THE DEFECT IT CLOSES: the outbox identifies a threshold event by the rule's ARMING CYCLE, and the engine identified the run by `(rule, trigger, refId, 'event')` — and a `MetricObservation` is written `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE`, so a provider revising a figure moves the SAME ROW across the line under the SAME id. Below, revised above (crossing, cycle 0), revised below (re-arm, cycle 1), revised above again (crossing, cycle 1): the outbox correctly wrote two events and the engine collapsed them into one run, dropping a real crossing in silence. THE REFERENCE IS UNCHANGED AND STILL IN THE KEY — it is PROVENANCE, the reading the crossing was seen in, and it is simply no longer asked to be an identity it cannot carry. A timed event still prefers its carried occurrence (P7-R5), and a domain event's key is `<trigger>:<refId>`, both of which the run key already contained, so not one equivalence class moves for either of them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **D-186** | **A CLAIMED FIRE AND ITS EVENT COMMIT TOGETHER OR NOT AT ALL.** `evaluateThresholdRule` resolves the `MetricObservation` it will cite BEFORE the compare-and-swap that claims the crossing, so no step that can decline runs after anything is committed. THE DEFECT IT CLOSES: the lookup ran AFTER the CAS and a miss returned `'no_observation'` — a NORMAL return, not a throw — so `withWorkspace` committed `thresholdBreached = true` and the scheduler's park WITHOUT the matching `AutomationEvent`. The next sweep read a rule already marked breached, computed `steady`, and did nothing: the crossing had been permanently consumed, with no event, no run, no error and no log line. THE RACE IS REACHABLE: analytics retention prunes observations, and `withWorkspace` is one READ COMMITTED transaction rather than a repeatable-read snapshot, so a prune committing between the metric-window read and the provenance lookup is visible to it. THE INVARIANT IS NOW STRUCTURAL rather than handled — between the claim and `recordRuleAutomationEvent` there is no branch, no return and no second query, so the only outcomes are both rows or neither. A miss leaves the rule armed and the cycle unmoved, so the same crossing is evaluated again on the next sweep. The CAS is unchanged and still the arbiter: two schedulers may both read provenance, only one can move the state.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **D-187** | **THE REMAINING MVP DELIVERY IS EXACTLY THREE TOP-LEVEL PHASES: 8 Product Completion, 9 Commerce & Onboarding, 10 Production Launch.** The former "Phase 8 — Billing and Launch" is superseded: its billing half is Phase 9, its public-site and launch-readiness half is Phase 10, and the product coherence work neither covered is Phase 8. No further top-level phase may be invented and no original MVP product scope may be deferred out of these three without an owner decision recorded here. **THERE IS NO PHASE 11.**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

Being unlinked describes the current sidebar, never the scope: both are mandatory Phase 8 deliverables (D-195).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| **D-189** | **BRAND PROFILE IS NOT A PRIMARY SIDEBAR MODULE.** It is a brand's canonical identity and basic configuration, reached contextually from the global Brand Selector and from Settings. It is NOT Brand Brain: Brand Profile is the identity a brand declares, Brand Brain is the knowledge the AI is grounded in. Giving configuration its own top-level rail entry would put a settings page beside the places people actually work.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **D-190** | **ONE SERVER-AUTHORITATIVE BRAND CONTEXT, AND NO PAGE-LEVEL BRAND PICKERS.** The shell carries a global Brand Selector beside the Workspace Selector; brand-scoped pages read the resolved context instead of each answering "which brand?" their own way. Before this, Brand Brain took the workspace's OLDEST brand silently, and Content, Analytics and Strategy each carried their own `?brand=` picker with their own fallback — four answers to one question, one of which was a silent guess.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **D-191** | **BRAND SELECTION PRECEDENCE: URL, THEN COOKIE, THEN NOTHING — NEVER A GUESS.** An explicit `?brand=` wins so a deep link is deterministic, and a URL naming a brand this member may not see resolves to NO SELECTION rather than falling through to the cookie, so the link means the same thing for everyone who opens it. The cookie is read only when the URL is silent. Nothing else is consulted: a brand-required page with no valid selection shows a choose-a-brand state, and a workspace with no brands shows a create-a-brand state. ONE ACCESSIBLE BRAND RESOLVES TO ITSELF: the defect was preferring the oldest OF SEVERAL, invisibly, between real alternatives — where a member can act on exactly one brand there is no choice to make, nothing is preferred over anything, and the rail still names it. From two upwards the product stops deciding. THE COOKIE IS NOT AN AUTHORIZATION CREDENTIAL: it carries `<workspaceId>:<brandId>`, is ignored outright when the workspace half does not match the active session (which is what makes a workspace switch safe without ever asking whether the brand exists elsewhere), and the brand half is re-validated against the member's BrandScope AT QUERY TIME on every request. A deep link does not rewrite the reader's stored selection. THE SAME RULE GOVERNS A CREATION SURFACE: a new object belongs to exactly one brand and cannot be filed under an aggregate, so a composer reading "all brands" starts on the sole accessible brand where there is one, and asks where there are two or more.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **D-192** | **EVERY DASHBOARD ROUTE DECLARES ITS SCOPE IN ONE TABLE** — Workspace, Brand, or Brand-or-All-Brands — rather than deciding again inside each page. "All Brands" means the brands the CURRENT MEMBER may access, never every brand in the workspace, and the scope goes into the QUERY rather than filtering a wider read afterwards (D-132).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **D-193** | **ONE ASSET LIBRARY; BRAND PROFILE REFERENCES IT.** A canonical logo is not a second copy of a file: the bytes live in the Asset Library and `brand.primaryLogoAssetId` / `secondaryLogoAssetId` NAME the asset. The reference is a composite workspace-scoped foreign key (D-112), so a brand can never point at another workspace's asset, and a database trigger refuses an asset belonging to a DIFFERENT brand — a canonical identity asset is either the brand's own or workspace-shared. Deleting the asset nulls only the reference column (D-114). Nothing stamps a logo onto generated visuals; Brand Profile only makes the canonical file findable.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **D-194** | **NO PRODUCT-WIDE COUNTRY, LOCALE, TIMEZONE OR CURRENCY DEFAULT.** `workspace.country/defaultLocale/timezone/currency` and `user.timezone` carried `SA` / `AR` / `Asia/Riyadh` / `SAR`, which made every customer Saudi unless somebody remembered otherwise. The defaults are DROPPED rather than replaced — substituting US/USD/New York would be the same mistake facing the other way. Creation paths must resolve the values explicitly, the type system enforces it, and existing stored values are preserved untouched by the migration. Phase 9 builds the onboarding UX that asks; Phase 8 removes the assumption. **The Arabic dialect default is unchanged and remains MSA**, which is a language decision rather than a country one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **D-195** | **PHASE 8 IS AN UMBRELLA PHASE, DELIVERED IN SIX WORKSTREAMS, AND IS NOT CLOSED BY ITS FIRST PULL REQUEST.** Workstream 1 — product coherence: brand context, Brand Profile, Asset Library context, the country-default removal — is delivered. Workstreams 2–6 remain MANDATORY Phase 8 scope: **Campaigns** (customer UI, create/read/update/archive, objective, brief, dates, channels, status lifecycle, content linkage, campaign content view, performance UX on the existing analytics foundations); **media inside the AI Content Studio** (upload during creation, selection from the ONE Asset Library including shared and brand assets, attachment to content and platform variants, media-aware editing and social previews); **the AI Creative Studio** (on-brand image generation using Brand Profile identity inputs and Brand Brain context, output format and platform selection, adaptation/resizing, generated media saved into the same one library and selectable in the Content Studio); **the media workflow end to end** (media-aware Approvals and Calendar, a media-aware publishing contract, provider abstraction carrying media payloads); and **Marketing Intelligence** with the final navigation integration and Product Completion polish. **Phase 8 closes only when the exit journey runs end to end:** Workspace → Brand → Brand Profile → Brand Brain → Assets → AI Strategy → Campaign → AI Content Studio → media selection/upload → AI Creative Studio → Approval → Calendar → Publish through the provider abstraction → Analytics → Marketing Intelligence → accepted learning back into Brand Brain. MOCK AND DEVELOPMENT ADAPTERS ARE SUFFICIENT to close Phase 8; real providers, real credentials and production deployment are Phase 10. An earlier draft of the Phase 8 roadmap section listed Campaigns, the AI Creative Studio and media publishing as "deliberately not in Phase 8" — that contradicted the approved plan, and is corrected here. |
| **D-196** | **AI CREDITS ARE PREPAID AND ENTITLEMENT-CONTROLLED; THERE IS NO POSTPAID OVERAGE.** This restates and binds D-11 for Phase 9 rather than reopening it. When a workspace's usable credits reach zero, AI execution STOPS. The customer may buy a credit pack or upgrade their plan; nothing runs on credit that has not been bought, and no balance may go negative (CLAUDE.md §2.4). The platform owner pays the upstream AI provider; the customer consumes BrandSpace credits under the Phase 4 gateway and ledger rules (D-25, D-26). Phase 9 therefore builds packs and top-ups and must NOT define an overage product. An earlier Phase 9 scope line read "packs, add-ons, overage"; the last of those was never approved and is removed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **D-197** | **A NAVIGATION ROW IS OFFERED ONLY WHERE THE MEMBER CAN FOLLOW IT, AND THAT IS DEAD-LINK PREVENTION RATHER THAN AUTHORIZATION.** The Settings section navigation is ONE table (`apps/dashboard/src/server/settings-nav.ts`) whose permission column MIRRORS each route's own `requireWorkspace` call — asserted by a test that reads the routes, so the mirror cannot rot. The Brand Selector's Brand Profile row additionally requires `brand.read`. THE DEFECT: Brand Profile requires only `brand.read` and listed workspace Settings — which requires `workspace.update` — unconditionally, so a brand manager who was not a workspace administrator saw a row that answered 404; the selector had the mirror-image bug, offering the profile row to any member with a resolved brand. **No authorization depends on any of this.** Every route still calls `requireWorkspace` and still answers 404 identically to a route that does not exist, so hiding a row removes a broken promise from the screen and nothing else (CLAUDE.md §2.1, docs/SECURITY.md §4).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **D-198** | **MARKETING INTELLIGENCE IS ITS OWN AREA, AND THE THREE AI-REASONING AREAS ANSWER THREE DIFFERENT QUESTIONS.** Analytics is the NUMBERS. Marketing Intelligence is what the numbers and the brand's own declarations MEAN — content gaps, performance explanations, notable changes and recommendations, each rendered from its stored `insight_evidence` rows. AI Strategy is the PLAN, and lists only strategies and monthly plans. `/strategy` previously listed every insight the workspace had ever produced beside its proposals, so one list answered both "what did we learn" and "what shall we do". Content-gap analysis has its own route (`POST /v1/intelligence/content-gap`) rather than a mode of strategy generation, because the distinction the customer needs is exactly the one a shared endpoint would blur. It rests on ABSENCE EVIDENCE — pillars declared in Brand Brain and not published against, accounts connected and not posted on — all of it checkable against this workspace's own data; there is no external market source (D-18, D-19) and the screen says so above every finding. THE LEARNING WRITE-BACK LIVES HERE, gated on `brand_brain.review` rather than `strategy.manage`, and proposes PENDING candidates into the existing Brand Brain queue with their provenance; it writes nothing into the brand (D-150).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **D-199** | **A `uuid[]` COLUMN CANNOT CARRY A COMPOSITE FOREIGN KEY, SO THE PREDICATE IS THE TENANT BOUNDARY — AND THERE IS EXACTLY ONE OF IT.** `content_variant.assetIds` is an array, so D-112's composite key cannot reach it. `publishableAssetWhere()` in `packages/assets/src/publishable.ts` is therefore the boundary, and BOTH callers use it: the Content Studio when an author attaches a picture, and the publish pipeline just before a payload reaches a provider. Two implementations would mean the Studio admitting something publishing later refused (a post that can never go out) or publishing admitting something the Studio refused (a refusal that is theatre). Admissible means: this workspace, this brand or the workspace-SHARED shelf, inside the caller's BrandScope as a query predicate (D-132), READY and CLEAN, and of a kind a post can carry. The scope clause is NOT the ordinary one — restricting `brandId` to the scope would hide the shared shelf, whose `brandId` is null. A refusal is `NOT_FOUND`, identical to a miss.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **D-200** | **MEDIA IS RESOLVED TO BYTES BY THE PIPELINE, NEVER FETCHED BY AN ADAPTER, AND IS NEVER SILENTLY DROPPED.** `PublishRequest.media` carries an identity, a type, a size, dimensions and the BYTES — never a storage key or a signed URL, because an adapter that could reach into the library would be a place tenant isolation could fail. Resolution happens in a PRE-FLIGHT before any adapter is called, and every refusal fails the job with a stable code rather than publishing a post with fewer pictures than its author attached: over the provider's activated ceiling is `preflight.too_many_media` (refused, not truncated), an inadmissible asset is `preflight.media_rejected`, and a caller wired with NO media port is `preflight.media_unavailable` — the absence of the port is load-bearing, because publishing the caption alone would be worse than not publishing. Those codes carry their own localized sentences: resolving a pre-flight refusal from the failure CLASS alone told a customer "the platform rejected this content" when no platform had been called.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **D-201** | **`PARTIALLY_PUBLISHED` IS A POST STATE THE DESIGN SYSTEM CARRIES.** The Calendar read every slot as `SCHEDULED` with a comment deferring the publishing states to a later phase; that phase shipped, and a planner looking at last week saw posts marked scheduled that had already gone out and one that had failed. The Calendar now reads the slot's own status, the latest `Approval` row, the item's campaign and the post's cover picture — joining canonical models and storing nothing. A post that went out on one platform and failed on another is NEITHER published nor failed, so `PostStatus` gains the state rather than the card picking one of the two: a card's accessible name states its status in words (WCAG 1.4.1), so the wrong choice is announced to a screen-reader user as fact.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **D-202** | **A DICTIONARY KEY A PAGE DOES NOT SEND IS A BLANK LABEL NOTHING CATCHES, SO A TEST READS THE LOOKUPS.** A dashboard page builds `Record<string, string>` from an explicit key list and hands it to its client view, because a translator function cannot cross the server/client boundary. A key the list omits comes back `undefined` and the view's `?? ''` renders an empty string: the Content Studio's media picker, its live social preview and one composer control read twenty-seven keys their page never sent, and every one rendered blank with both translations present and neither ever shown. Types cannot catch it — the lookup is always well-typed. `tests/unit/phase8-dictionary-coverage.test.ts` scans every view's `t['…']` literals and requires the key to appear in its own `page.tsx`.
