The cache is now STAMPED with the activated `configuration_version` id, read uncached on every resolve through `activeVersionId` (which documents its own refusal to be cached), and the domain's configuration cache is dropped before each rebuild because `ConfigurationService` memoises payloads for thirty seconds of its own — invalidated in the process that activated, not in this one. **Activation and disable now take effect on the next send, in every process, with no window.** The TTL survives with one job, stated as a contract: rotating a CREDENTIAL does not change the document, so rotation remains bounded by `PROVIDER_TTL_MS` rather than instant. Found by CI, where two `production-email` tests saw the delivery route accept a message and the Resend recorder receive nothing. | Current execution Phase 5 | `apps/api/src/email-provider.ts` |
| **D-258** | **A TEST THAT INHERITS ITS PRECONDITION PASSES FOR A REASON IT DOES NOT STATE.** The boundary test in `production-email.spec.ts` asserts that an accepted link reaches the transport — which only means anything when a real provider is active — and it established no such precondition, depending instead on the journey test above it having activated Resend and left it so. That dependency was invisible for as long as D-257's stale cache stood in for the missing setup step. Fixing the product turned the coincidence into a failure, which is the correct outcome: the test now calls an idempotent `ensureResendActive` of its own. Recorded because the PATTERN is the risk — a test whose precondition is another test's side effect is one reordering away from lying, and the harness change was made only AFTER the product defect underneath it was fixed, not instead of fixing it. | Current execution Phase 5 | `tests/e2e/production-email.spec.ts` |

| **D-259** | **AN UNSTATED QUOTA IS NOT A QUOTA OF ZERO — AND A WORKSPACE WITH NO PLAN COULD NOT CREATE ITS FIRST BRAND.** Reported from staging: creating a workspace and then a brand answered _"This workspace has reached a limit on its plan. Free some space or ask an admin to change the plan."_ — on a workspace that had no plan, had reached no limit, and had nothing to free. Every instruction in that sentence was unactionable. The chain: onboarding writes `planKey: null` unless a trial plan is configured AND has `trialDays > 0` AND is priced in the workspace's currency, so an environment with no configured customer plans leaves every new workspace planless; `limit.brands` then falls past the plan rung to the FEATURE DEFAULT, where the bootstrap projection declares `{ valueType: 'quota', defaultValue: null }`; step 9 applied a BOOLEAN feature's rule — `enabled = defaultValue === true` — to a QUOTA feature and returned `enabled: false`; and `EntitlementService.limit()` turned that into `0`. **That is precisely the collapse `limit()`'s own comment forbids** — _"`null` is unlimited and `0` is none, and a caller that collapses the two locks out exactly the customers who paid for no limit"_ — performed by the engine on its own behalf. A quota default that is ABSENT states no ceiling; it does not state a ceiling of zero. **NO ALLOWANCE IS HARD-CODED BY THE FIX**, which the owner ruled out explicitly: the ceiling remains whatever configuration states at a rung above — plan entitlement, workspace override, or a declared numeric default — and only the case where configuration states NOTHING moves. **THE FIRST ATTEMPT WAS TOO WIDE AND THE EXISTING SUITE CAUGHT IT**, which is why the narrowing is the decision rather than a footnote: reading every absent quota default as unlimited also granted it to a workspace ON a plan that simply omits the dimension — destroying the distinction that makes a plan writing `limitValue: null` mean _negotiated, unlimited_ rather than _not mentioned_ — and, far worse, to a workspace whose subscription had been CANCELLED or EXPIRED, for which D-246's terminal-status rule sets `planKey: null` **so that quotas become none**. That would have made cancelling a subscription an upgrade. `planKey: null` cannot tell "never had a plan" from "had one that ended", so `WorkspaceEntitlementContext` now carries `planEnded` and only a workspace that has NEVER had a plan reaches the new reading. `unknown_feature` is a different rung and still fails closed, so a typo still grants nothing. **A SECOND, SEPARATE DEFECT SURFACED WHILE PROVING CASE 4**: two genuinely simultaneous submits of the SAME brand under a ceiling of one produced one brand and one alarming `QUOTA_EXCEEDED` — the loser blocks on the counter row, wakes to find the slot taken, and throws from inside the transaction BEFORE reaching the unique idempotency key that the existing duplicate-key path catches. The sequential replay of that key does not refuse; the concurrent one now agrees with it, by the same `sameUsageRequest` comparison, while a different request wearing the key is still a CONFLICT and a genuine refusal with no record still throws. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-03b) | `packages/entitlements/src/{precedence,service,usage}.ts`, `tests/unit/phase6-quota-default.test.ts`, `tests/isolation/phase6-brand-quota.test.ts` |
| **D-260** | **THE PHASE 6 PROTOTYPE IS UX AUTHORITY AND THE SHIPPED DESIGN SYSTEM IS VISUAL AUTHORITY, AND NEITHER BORROWS THE OTHER'S.** Phase 6 arrives with an HTML prototype, and the failure mode it invites is the one Phase 5A already cost us in the other direction: a prototype that looks like a design gets treated as one. The split is recorded so no commit has to re-derive it — the prototype decides what screens exist, what is on them, how they are grouped and how work flows through them; `packages/ui`, the approved routes and `docs/UI-FIDELITY-CONTRACT.md` decide every pixel, and a Phase 6 change may move, group, name or complete a thing but may not restyle one. The prototype file is NOT vendored, so its IA is transcribed into `docs/CURRENT-EXECUTION-PHASE-6.md` §3–§4 to give the work a checkable in-repo source. Where Phase 6 needs a screen with no approved reference, CLAUDE.md §4.2 governs unchanged. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-01) | `docs/CURRENT-EXECUTION-PHASE-6.md` |

| **D-261** | **THE CUSTOMER PASSWORD MINIMUM IS 8, AND IT IS CONFIGURATION — WHICH IT WAS NOT.** The owner asked for the floor to come down from 12 while the hashing and the server-side validation stayed exactly as strong. What made this more than editing a literal is that the literal appeared FIVE times and was configuration in only ONE: `packages/auth/src/password.ts` refused below 12, both password-setting server actions refused below 12, and the reset and invitation screens wrote `minLength={12}` — while the sign-up page alone read `onboarding.signup.minPasswordLength`. So an operator lowering the configured value changed one of five screens, and lowering it below the domain's own hard-coded floor produced a form that ACCEPTED a password the domain then refused to hash, which reads to the customer as the site being broken. `ABSOLUTE_MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH` (8) and `ABSOLUTE_MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH` (128) now live in `@brandspace/shared`, are what the configuration schema bounds itself by, and are the only floor the domain enforces; the POLICY in force is resolved from configuration at every boundary through one accessor, `signupPolicy()`. **THE CEILING IS NEW AND IS NOT COSMETIC**: Argon2id hashes whatever it is given, so an unbounded password on an unauthenticated endpoint is a cheap way to spend the server's memory budget. **WHY EIGHT IS NOT A WEAKENING.** NIST SP 800-63B puts the minimum at eight and spends its remaining advice on what actually resists guessing — length over composition, no forced rotation, no symbol classes. A floor of twelve rejected a great many passphrases people would actually remember, and the observable effect of that is not stronger passwords but more reuse of one already memorised elsewhere. Argon2id at m=19456/t=2/p=1 is unchanged, every server action still validates independently, and the F-19 abuse ceilings are untouched. **NOTHING HERE TOUCHES PLATFORM ADMIN**, whose mandatory MFA (D-27) and rules are separate and are asserted so by a test. **THE UI IS ONE COMPONENT, NOT FOUR COPIES**: `PasswordField` carries the reveal toggle (a real button whose accessible NAME changes with the state, plus `aria-pressed`, because whether a password is legible on a shared screen is exactly what a screen-reader user needs to know), the confirmation, and a rules list stated BEFORE anything is typed rather than discovered by failing. **THE CONFIRMATION IS THE CLIENT'S COURTESY AND THE SERVER'S NOTHING.** It is deliberately unnamed so it is not even submitted, no action reads it, and a test fails if one starts to: a client-side match is the caller checking their own input. No password value is logged, measured or placed in a `data-` attribute. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-03a) | `packages/shared/src/password-policy.ts`, `packages/auth/src/password.ts`, `packages/config/src/domains.ts`, `packages/ui/src/password-field.tsx`, `apps/dashboard/src/server/signup-policy.ts` |
| **D-262** | **A SOURCE-SCANNING GUARD MUST BLANK COMMENTS, OR IT REPORTS PROSE AS CODE.** Three guards in this phase read source text to assert a property of every call site — no bare `<button>`, no password length compared against a literal, every control carrying `.bs-control`. Two of them reported a DOC COMMENT as a violation: the comment explaining the billing fix contains the words `<button>`, and `password-field.tsx` explains the defect it fixes by quoting `<input type="password">`. The first was caught by the guard failing on its own file; the second by the pre-existing `design-system.test.ts`, which had the same flaw and had simply never met a file that described a control in prose. Each now replaces every comment with spaces of the SAME LENGTH before scanning, so line numbers stay exact and the failure still points at the line somebody has to open. Recorded because the pattern will recur: a guard that greps source is measuring text, and text includes the explanation of why the guard exists. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-02, P6-03a) | `tests/unit/phase6-control-consistency.test.ts`, `tests/unit/phase6-password-policy.test.ts`, `tests/unit/design-system.test.ts` |

| **F-89** | **THE BREACHED-PASSWORD CHECK IN `docs/SECURITY.md` §3 HAS NEVER EXISTED.** The requirements table has listed "breached-password check" beside the length policy since the document was written, and nothing in the repository implements one — no k-anonymity range query, no local corpus, no call site, no test. Found while lowering the length floor (D-261), where it matters more than it did before: NIST's advice is that a shorter minimum is safe BECAUSE candidates are screened against known-breached corpora, so the two halves were always meant to ship together and only one did. NOT FIXED IN PHASE 6 and not silently dropped from the requirement either — the row now says it is not implemented, so the document stops claiming a control the product does not have. Proposed fix for the owner to schedule: a k-anonymity range query against a breach corpus at sign-up, password reset and invitation acceptance, failing OPEN on a provider outage (refusing a customer's password because a third party is down is an outage of our own), with the provider and its endpoint as configuration rather than code. | Current execution Phase 6, found while lowering the password floor | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED.** The length floor moving to 8 is the owner's instruction and stands on its own; this is the complementary control that was documented and never built. |

| **D-263** | **A ONE-OFF OPERATOR COMMAND REACHES A RESTRICTED PACKAGE AT THE POINT OF USE, NOT AT THE MODULE EDGE.** `bootstrap-staging-owner.ts` arrived on the branch importing `@brandspace/secrets` and `@brandspace/auth` at the top of the file, which `packages/database` may not do (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md §4.1, and F-07 for `secrets` specifically) — seven lint errors, and CI red on the shared branch. The resolution is the convention its production sibling already established and documents: `await import()` at the point of use, so the dependency is a runtime fact of one command rather than a module edge the rest of the package could follow. A bootstrap is not an architectural exception; it is a command that happens to live beside the schema. Its three `console.log` calls and its one `new Date()` are the other half: the printing IS the command's interface, so the file joins `bootstrap-production-owner.ts` in the noisier-files list, and the timestamp is read once into a named value because a bootstrap has no injected clock to use instead. Fixed rather than reported because a red lint job blocks every other thing on this branch. | Current execution Phase 6, after merging the owner's staging work | `packages/database/prisma/bootstrap-staging-owner.ts`, `eslint.config.mjs` |

| **F-90** | **`phase7-round6` AND `phase7-round7` FAIL ON A LONG-LIVED LOCAL TEST DATABASE, AND THE REASON IS THE SUITES' OWN LITTER.** Nine tests failed locally on an isolation database that has accumulated **7312 `automation_rule` rows** across many runs. Both files sweep with `BATCH = 500`, under a comment stating it is "a ceiling well above the number of rules any suite leaves behind" — an assumption that was true when written and has quietly stopped being true, because every isolation run creates automation rules and none of them removes its own. The fair-work cursor (R4-2) then orders the enumeration past the rule the test just created, the sweep produces no event, and the assertion reads `expected 1, received 0`. **PROVEN, NOT INFERRED**: raising `BATCH` above the accumulated row count makes all six pass unchanged, and the same commit's CI job "Tenant isolation and RLS (real PostgreSQL)" is GREEN, because CI migrates a fresh database from empty every run. **NOT A PRODUCT DEFECT AND NOT CAUSED BY ANY PHASE 6 CHANGE** — nothing in this phase touches automation. It is the F-62 pattern in a new place: a batch-bounded sweep starved by rows nobody cleans up, and F-62's own resolution was to make the offending suite delete what it creates. Proposed fix for the owner to schedule: have both suites remove the automation rules, runs and events for the workspaces they create, so the pile stops growing; a `BATCH` raised to outrun the litter would be treating the symptom and would drift again. Until then the local failure is expected on an old database and CI is the authority. | Current execution Phase 6, found running the full local isolation suite | **RECORDED, NOT FIXED.** Out of Phase 6's scope, and fixing a test's cleanup in an unrelated area would widen this PR for something CI does not see. |

| **D-264** | **THE CUSTOMER RAIL IS GROUPED, USING THE MECHANISM IT ALREADY HAD.** `AppShell` has taken `readonly ShellNavSection[]` with an optional per-section `title` since Phase 2C, renders those titles as the demo's `.nav-group-title`, and already swaps a heading for a divider when the rail collapses — the Control Center has used all of it since it shipped. The customer dashboard passed all TWENTY-TWO entries as one unnamed section, so a member arriving at a workspace met an undifferentiated list and had to already know the product to find anything in it. Phase 6 groups them into CORE / PLAN / CREATE / PUBLISH / IMPROVE / AUTOMATE / WORKSPACE, in the order of the work — know the brand, plan it, make it, publish it, learn from it, automate it, and administer the workspace last because administering it is not the work. **NO COMPONENT, TOKEN, GEOMETRY OR ICON CHANGED**, which is what makes this compatible with the brief's locked visual system: the grouping is a data table of hrefs, deliberately separate from `NAV` so that every entry keeps the comment explaining its permission gate, its icon reuse and the phase it arrived in. **TWO INVARIANTS ARE ENFORCED IN CODE RATHER THAN BY REVIEW**: an entry placed in no group throws at build of the section list, because a route that silently vanishes from the rail is the inverse of a dead link and much harder to notice; and a group whose entries are all permission-filtered renders nothing at all, because a titled heading over nothing is dead navigation wearing a label — a Viewer (D-62, D-130) empties most of them. **THE COUNT WAS TWENTY-TWO, NOT TWENTY-ONE**: the Phase 6 audit counted by eye and was wrong, and the test that counts disagreed on its first run. The four entries the brief's list does not name — `/permissions`, `/notifications`, `/plan`, `/onboarding` — are real screens and are placed rather than dropped; matching a list by hiding working product would have been the wrong repair. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-04) | `apps/dashboard/src/components/workspace-shell.tsx`, `apps/dashboard/src/i18n/messages.ts` |
| **D-265** | **THE HOME SCREEN ANSWERS "WHAT SHOULD I DO", BEFORE IT ANSWERS "HOW ARE THINGS GOING".** The Overview already carried real figures with honest unavailable states, and it was still the wrong first screen: a member signed in, read four correct numbers and had to go looking for the work. An ATTENTION ITEM is a piece of work, not a statistic — it names something a person can act on, deep-links to where they act, and exists only when it is genuinely true. **THERE IS NO ZERO ROW AND NO PLACEHOLDER.** A workspace with nothing outstanding gets one sentence saying so, because that is a good state and should read like one; a "0 items need attention" card is clutter that never leaves. Five sources ship — failed and half-failed publishes, overdue schedules, connections needing re-authorisation, content in review, and brands with no knowledge at all. **EVERY SOURCE IS PERMISSION-GATED BEFORE IT RUNS**, because an attention item is a link and offering somebody a route that will answer 404 is a dead link delivered as a to-do. **EVERY SOURCE IS ALSO INDEPENDENT**: one that throws contributes nothing rather than failing the page, since no single count should be able to stop a member reaching their workspace. **WHAT IS ABSENT IS ABSENT, NOT ZERO** — assigned notes and mentions do not exist until P6-05, so there is no source for them rather than a source returning 0, which would be a fabricated metric wearing a number. **NO READINESS PERCENTAGE**: "how complete is this brand's knowledge" has no defensible denominator, so the brand signal is empty-or-not, which is a fact. The whole surface is a new isolation boundary — five queries over four tables on the screen every member lands on — and is covered accordingly: cross-workspace, brand-scope and permission leaks each fail a test. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-04) | `apps/dashboard/src/server/command-center.ts`, `apps/dashboard/src/app/[locale]/overview/page.tsx` |

| **D-266** | **CONTEXTUAL COLLABORATION IS A NEW TENANT-OWNED CAPABILITY, AND NOTES ARE STRUCTURALLY NOT BRAND BRAIN.** Three tables — `note_thread`, `note`, `note_mention` — each carrying `workspaceId` DIRECTLY rather than reaching it through a parent, because "scoped by its parent" holds right up until somebody counts mentions for one person. All three take ENABLE + FORCE RLS, a tenant policy on `app.current_workspace_id()`, a platform policy and explicit grants. **THE APPLICATION ROLE MAY NOT DELETE A NOTE OR A THREAD** — a conversation is evidence of who asked for what, notes are soft-deleted so the thread keeps its shape, and only a MENTION may be deleted (an edited note drops the people it no longer names); the platform role keeps DELETE for offboarding, exactly as D-128 sets it for approvals. **THE SEPARATION FROM BRAND BRAIN IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN A CONVENTION**: a separate package with no dependency on `@brandspace/brand-brain`, no foreign key into `brand_knowledge_item`, no method that writes there — and an isolation test that writes a thread saying "treat that as settled" and asserts the knowledge count is unchanged. The failure mode being prevented is silent: a passing remark becoming governed memory and then becoming what the Copilot believes about the brand, while the four-layer model's HUMAN > DOCUMENT > AI_INFERRED precedence describes a guarantee the product no longer has. **A MENTION NAMES AN ACTIVE MEMBER OF THIS WORKSPACE OR NOBODY**, and an unresolvable id is DROPPED rather than rejected: rejecting would tell the mentioner that the id is real but not here, which is an account-enumeration oracle. Assignment refuses instead, because silently assigning to nobody leaves the thread looking owned to the person who assigned it. **A THREAD IS NEVER CREATED EMPTY** and replying to a resolved thread REOPENS it — somebody typing into a closed conversation is saying it is not closed, and leaving it resolved hides the reply from every "what is open" view. Audit events record what changed and never the body: duplicating a private remark into the audit trail puts the conversation somewhere its own permissions do not reach. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-05) | `packages/collaboration/`, `packages/database/prisma/migrations/20260923120000_phase_6_notes_mentions/` |
| **D-267** | **A SCOPE CHECK THAT REWRITES WHAT IT WAS ASKED ABOUT IS WORSE THAN NO CHECK.** Resolving a BRAND-subject thread needs the brand's own column, `id`, and both shared helpers (`brandIdScopeFilter`, `brandIdQueryFilter`) emit `brandId` — right for every model that REFERENCES a brand, wrong for the brand itself. Writing it by hand reintroduced the exact defect `brandIdQueryFilter` was created to prevent, one comment away from the comment explaining it: spreading `{ id: { in: scope } }` over `{ id: subject.brandId }` means the LATER KEY WINS, so the requested brand was discarded and the query returned whichever brand happened to be in scope. The thread was then attached to the WRONG BRAND and the caller got a SUCCESS — a scope violation that presents as working software. Found by the isolation test rather than by review, which is the only reason it is a footnote instead of an incident. The fix is an explicit `AND`, which cannot express the mistake because both clauses are present and both must hold. **THE GENERAL LESSON, worth more than the fix**: a shared helper that does not fit is a signal to write the same DISCIPLINE by hand, not to drop it — and "later key wins" is now the second distinct defect this repository has recorded from that one JavaScript rule. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-05) | `packages/collaboration/src/notes.ts`, `tests/isolation/phase6-notes-isolation.test.ts` |
| **D-268** | **PULSE IS THE COMMAND CENTER'S ATTENTION LIST, EXTENDED — NOT A SECOND FEED (P6-11).** The brief asks Pulse to surface pending learnings, performance patterns, campaign and calendar gaps, connection expiry, publishing failures and credit forecasts, coherently across Home, context, Notifications and Copilot, with no banner spam. A separate "insights feed" beside the P6-04 attention list would have been two ranked answers to "what should I do next" that can disagree. So Pulse is the SAME list, with six more sources under the same three rules: an item exists only when its condition is true against real rows; it links to where it is acted on and is gated on that route's permission (the credit forecast needs BOTH `credits.read` and `billing.read`, so `SOURCES` now takes a permission LIST); nothing is a score. The sources: `learnings-pending` (PENDING candidates, `brand_brain.review`), `insights-new` (NEW, unexpired intelligence findings), `connection-expiring` (ACTIVE tokens inside `EXPIRING_SOON_MS`, now exported so Home, `/integrations` and publish readiness share one window — nothing refreshes a token automatically, so this is a real task), `campaign-empty` (ACTIVE, not ended, no content), `calendar-gap` (a brand with an ACTIVE connection and nothing planned/scheduled/publishing in the next 7 days), `credits-forecast` (net usage over 28 days from the LEDGER, divided by the whole window so a young workspace is never extrapolated from one afternoon, raised only when the run-out lands before `nextResetAt` — no renewal date, no usage or nothing spendable means no claim), plus `performance-above`/`below` from `detectAnomalies` over Home's own 28-day series, raised only for a shift in the last 7 days. The 7- and 28-day horizons are PRESENTATION horizons — which question Home asks — not policy: they set no limit and change no behaviour, and the unit suite fails if the copy stops stating them. The two "repeated" signals the brief lists are NOT built, because no honest signal for them exists yet (F-92). | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-11) | `apps/dashboard/src/server/command-center.ts`, `apps/dashboard/src/server/performance-patterns.ts`, `tests/isolation/phase6-pulse.test.ts`, `tests/unit/phase6-pulse.test.ts` |
| **D-269** | **THE P6-04 COMMAND CENTER WAS EMPTY FOR EVERY BRAND-SCOPED MEMBER — A SECOND INSTANCE OF D-267.** `brandsWithNoKnowledge` filtered the BRAND table with `brandIdScopeFilter`, which emits `brandId`; the brand table has only `id`. For a member with a non-empty BrandScope the query failed Prisma validation, and because every source is dispatched in the same tick the failure rejected ALL of them — `Promise.allSettled` then dropped every item, so the member was told "nothing is waiting" whatever was. Invisible to P6-04's suite, which never ran a scoped member holding `brand_brain.read`. Found by the P6-11 isolation suite and fixed with `brandScopeFilter` (by `id`), which is what D-267 already prescribes for the brand table. Pinned by a scoped-member test that fails with the old helper restored (defect plant 1). | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-11) | `apps/dashboard/src/server/command-center.ts`, `tests/isolation/phase6-pulse.test.ts` |
| **F-91** | **`prisma-config.test.ts` RACED THE SHARED GENERATED CLIENT.** It ran `prisma generate` into the shared `.prisma/client` while Vitest ran other suites in parallel, which intermittently removed `.prisma/client/default` under them. Fixed in `0cfa776` by generating into a throwaway directory and asserting the shared client's mtime does not change. Recorded here so the fix is not regressed by a later "simplification". | Current execution Phase 6 (CI) | `tests/unit/prisma-config.test.ts` |
| **F-92** | **TWO PULSE SIGNALS THE BRIEF NAMES HAVE NO HONEST SOURCE YET: REPEATED WORKFLOWS AND REPEATED EDIT PREFERENCES.** Nothing records a workflow as a sequence, and a reviewer's edits exist only as `reviewedTitle`/`reviewedBody` on individual candidates with no comparison across them. Either could be approximated — "you did X three times" from audit rows — but an approximation presented as a detected habit is the fabricated insight CLAUDE.md §2.2 forbids. Likewise `analytics.anomaly_detected` stays declared and unsent: anomalies are computed on read and never persisted, so there is no event to notify ON without a scheduled detector, which is new background work rather than integration. Open until a real signal exists. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-11) — OPEN | `docs/CURRENT-EXECUTION-PHASE-6.md` §8 |
| **D-270** | **THE COPILOT IS OPENED IN CONTEXT, INSPECTS WITHOUT CEREMONY, AND MAY COMPOSE — NEVER ENABLE — AN AUTOMATION (P6-12).** Five gaps closed without changing the confirmation contract. (1) CONTEXT: `copilot_session.surface` was stored and never read; it is now a CLOSED list (`COPILOT_SURFACES`) the session route enforces, and the prompt carries the repository's own description of that screen — never caller text, because that line of the prompt is not fenced. Home's Pulse, Analytics, Intelligence and Automations open the Copilot with `?from=`. (2) BRAND: the screen took the rail's brand instead of its own `brands[0]` picker (D-190). (3) INSPECT: a READ_ONLY plan is created CONFIRMED with no token, and `/confirm` requires one — so read tools were offered and never ran. `/turn` now executes a read-only plan through the same `execute` (live authorization, per-step permission/brand/entitlement, audit) and returns the tool results, which are metadata by construction. (4) DECLINE: `CopilotPlanService.cancel` had no route; "Cancel" only forgot the plan in the browser, which kept it open against the open-plan ceiling. `/v1/copilot/cancel` now closes it server-side. (5) COMPOSE: `automation.create_rule` (INTERNAL_REVERSIBLE, `automation.manage`, confirmation required) writes through `AutomationEngine.createRule`, always with `enabled: false` typed as a literal on the port; the trigger/action pairing and the caller's hold on the ACTION's own permission are checked at plan build by an injected `AutomationRuleCheck` — `packages/copilot` may not import `@brandspace/automation` (§4.1), and an unwired check fails CLOSED. Undo removes the rule only while it is still disabled and unedited (`rule_enabled_since` otherwise). No tool enables, pays, disconnects or deletes a workspace. Also: the credit estimate now quotes only steps that spend credits, under the real workspace id. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-12) | `packages/copilot/src/{surfaces,tools,executors,undo,orchestrator,plans}.ts`, `apps/api/src/routes/copilot{,-automation}.ts`, `tests/isolation/phase6-copilot-automation.test.ts`, `tests/unit/phase6-copilot.test.ts` |
| **D-271** | **`AutomationEngine.listRules` DISCARDED THE BRAND IT WAS ASKED FOR — THE D-267 SHAPE, THIRD INSTANCE.** It spread `{ brandId }` and then `{ brandId: { in: scope } }` into one object, so for any brand-scoped member the later key won and every in-scope brand's rules came back. Not a cross-tenant leak (the scope still bounded it) but the screen could not show one brand. Both `listRules` and `listRuns` now use `brandIdQueryFilter`, which ANDs the two, and the Automations screen finally filters by the rail's brand. Also: a NOTIFY rule now sends its own `automation.notice` template instead of `automation.confirmation_required`, the external-publish confirm shows the rule and the content title it would publish and is offered only to a member holding `publishing.manage`, rule deletion asks first, and run failure codes are shown. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-12) | `packages/automation/src/engine.ts`, `apps/dashboard/src/app/[locale]/automations/*` |
| **F-93** | **API ROUTE RATE LIMITS ARE DECLARED AND NOT ENFORCED.** Every `apps/api` route declares `rateLimit` (and some `confirmation`/`idempotent`) in its contract, and `route-contract.ts` records them and enforces only scope and permission; no limiter exists in `apps/api`. Pre-existing, found by the P6-12 audit, not introduced by Phase 6. AI routes remain bounded by the credit ledger and customer sign-in has its own F-19 ceilings, so this is a hardening gap rather than a Phase 6 blocker — recorded rather than fixed here because a limiter is new infrastructure (shared Redis counters, per-route budgets as configuration) outside this phase's scope. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-12) — OPEN | `apps/api/src/route-contract.ts` |
| **D-272** | **BRAND ACCESS IS NOW MANAGED — AND AN INVITATION COULD NO LONGER WIDEN IT (P6-13).** BrandScope decides which brands a member sees, and two things were wrong. (1) ESCALATION, pre-existing: `InvitationService.create` stored `brandScope` unvalidated and the Team screen never sent one, so every invitation granted EVERY brand — including one issued by a member restricted to a single brand; `resend` re-issued whatever was stored under the resender's authority. (2) Nothing could change an existing member's scope. One rule, `resolveGrantableBrandScope` in `@brandspace/auth`, now governs both grant paths and the new `MembershipService.changeBrandAccess`: every requested brand must be a live brand of THIS workspace (a foreign id and an invented id are refused identically — no oracle); a restricted actor may grant only a non-empty subset of their own brands, never "all brands"; a platform operator (`actorBrandScope: null`) is not ranked by it. `changeBrandAccess` also needs `member.assign_role`, follows the role ladder, never restricts the workspace owner, locks the workspace row, and audits `workspace.member.brand_access_changed` with before/after. The member inviter's `brandScope` is a REQUIRED field, so a call site that forgets it fails to compile rather than granting everything. No schema, migration or RLS change: `membership.brand_scope` and `invitation.brand_scope` already existed. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-13) | `packages/auth/src/{brand-access,invitations,memberships}.ts`, `apps/dashboard/src/app/[locale]/members/*` |
| **D-273** | **ACTIVITY, SETTINGS AND PLAN SAY ONLY WHAT THE BACKEND KNOWS (P6-13).** (1) ACTIVITY printed raw audit keys (`content.review_requested`) as titles and filter options. `activityActionLabel` resolves exact key → family → generic, in both languages; a unit test scans every literal `action:` the code writes and fails if any falls through to the generic label, so a new audit action without a label is caught. Resource types are translated or omitted, never printed raw. (2) SETTINGS gains Connected accounts (the existing `/integrations` route, `integrations.read`) and Data controls (`/settings/data`, `workspace.update`) — a page that names every data control that exists and links to where it lives, and lists self-serve workspace export and deletion as NOT AVAILABLE rather than hiding them; no control is invented. Brand Profile stays contextual — reached from the brand switcher's Manage link and inside Settings, never as a main-sidebar item. (3) PLAN shows brands and social accounts from `TOTAL_RESOURCE_DIMENSIONS` — the populations the quotas enforce — against the ceiling the entitlement engine resolved: a stated limit, a disabled quota as 0, and an enabled quota with no value as "no ceiling stated" (D-259), never a number made up by the page. Seats stay uncapped on screen because nothing enforces `limit.seats` (D-233). Plan names come from the activated catalogue, falling back to the key itself. (4) BILLING actions that failed reloaded silently; they now show a failure notice. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-13) | `apps/dashboard/src/server/{activity-labels,plan-usage,settings-nav}.ts`, `apps/dashboard/src/app/[locale]/{activity,settings/data,plan,billing}/*` |
| **D-274** | **ARABIC, RTL, RESPONSIVE AND ACCESSIBILITY ARE NOW SWEPT ACROSS EVERY CUSTOMER SCREEN, NOT PROVEN SCREEN BY SCREEN (P6-14).** A new E2E sweep visits all 26 signed-in customer routes in English and Arabic in both the desktop and phone projects and asserts `lang`/`dir`, zero inline-end overhang (F-26 measurement, so RTL's left edge is seen), an Arabic heading on Arabic screens, no raw dictionary key or `undefined` in the copy, and ZERO axe violations at WCAG 2.2 AA — not merely no serious ones. Its first run found six real defects, all pre-existing: the Permissions screen described every permission in English on the Arabic page (54 descriptions now in the dictionary, with a unit test pinning the English to the catalogue so they cannot drift); the Brand Brain add-knowledge form's accessible names were English literals; Brand Profile's locale checkboxes and the new brand-access checkboxes failed WCAG 2.5.8 target size on a phone; Plan's and Brand Brain's scrolling regions could not be reached by keyboard (`scrollable-region-focusable`); and Plan's table headings measured 4.24:1 where the translucent card sits over the ambient glow. That last one is fixed by giving the table wrappers the opaque surface `DataTable` already has, not by restyling the card. Plan's credit history also printed ISO dates and system-written English reasons; it now shows a localized date and the entry's kind in the reader's language, keeping the stored reason only where a person wrote it. Static guards widen with it: the Arabic-copy check now covers the whole dictionary (was Brand Brain only), placeholders must survive translation, the physical-property scan covers the whole dashboard (was `packages/ui` only), and literal English accessible names are refused. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-14) | `tests/e2e/phase6-locale-sweep.spec.ts`, `tests/unit/phase6-locale-guards.test.ts` |
| **D-275** | **A RAW DATABASE STATE ON AN ARABIC SCREEN IS A DEFECT, AND THE SWEEP NOW SAYS SO (P6-15).** The final owner-review screenshot of the Arabic Team screen showed `ACTIVE` — member and invitation statuses were printed as their enum values. The sweep had not caught it because it looked for dictionary keys, not enum words, so it now also refuses a raw enum state (an `UPPER_SNAKE` token or a known status word) on every Arabic screen. Its next run found two more: Home's workspace status badge and Plan's credit-allocation source. All three now render from the dictionary (`members.memberStatus.*`, `members.inviteStatus.*`, `overview.workspaceStatus.*`, `plan.grantSource.*`), and a unit test fails if any value of `MembershipStatus`, `InvitationStatus`, `WorkspaceStatus` or `CreditGrantSource` lacks a label in either language. Allocation expiry dates are locale-formatted like the ledger's. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-15) | `apps/dashboard/src/app/[locale]/{members,overview,plan}/page.tsx`, `tests/e2e/phase6-locale-sweep.spec.ts` |
| **D-276** | **THE CUSTOMER TOP BAR IS REVIEW · NOTES · NOTIFICATIONS · COPILOT · CREATE, EACH A REAL DESTINATION — AND SEARCH IS REMOVED RATHER THAN FAKED (P6-16).** Staging acceptance found the top bar still rendering `TopbarActions`: search, notifications and create opened a "not connected yet" sheet and the bell carried a purple dot on every page regardless of data. The customer shell now composes the bar from `TopbarLink` and `TopbarCreateMenu` (`packages/ui`), driven by the pure `topbarModel` (`apps/dashboard/src/server/topbar.ts`). **Review** → `/approvals`, on `content.read` exactly as the route; its dot is the pending-approval count in the member's brands, shown only to a member holding `content.approve`. **Notes** → a new `/notes` surface, a READING of the existing collaboration domain (`NotesService.inbox`) — threads stay attached to their content item, campaign or brand, each row links back to that subject, and nothing is written from the page; its dot is `unreadMentionCount`. **Notifications** → `/notifications`; its dot is `NotificationService.unreadCount`. **Copilot** → `/copilot?from=<surface>` via `copilotSurfaceForPath`, so the P6-12 context travels from every screen; `copilot.use`. **Create** → a menu of the flows the member can complete, each requiring BOTH its page's gate and the creating permission (content, campaign, AI creative, library upload — the last via `/assets?upload=1`, which opens the library's own dialog); with none, no button. A dot is drawn only from a non-zero count, and the count is in the link's accessible name. **Search is removed** from the customer bar: no search domain exists behind it, and an unconnected control is the placeholder this replaces. Two defects fixed on the way: `NotesService.unreadMentionCount` ignored brand scope and permission (it counted mentions in threads the member could no longer open) and now matches the Command Center's count, which in turn now excludes deleted notes and deleted subjects; Home's two notes attention items now lead to `/notes` instead of back to Home. `DropdownMenu` gains the menu-button arrow keys (ArrowDown/ArrowUp on the trigger open onto the first/last item). The Control Center's `TopbarActions` is unchanged — out of scope, and its own search and notifications are not built. | Current execution Phase 6 (P6-16) | `packages/ui/src/{topbar-actions,overlays,icons}.tsx`, `packages/collaboration/src/notes.ts`, `apps/dashboard/src/{components/workspace-shell.tsx,server/topbar*.ts,app/[locale]/notes/page.tsx}` |
| **D-277** | **OWNER DECISION — THE PHASE 6 FINAL UX CONTRACT IS THE PRODUCT / UX / INFORMATION-ARCHITECTURE AUTHORITY; THE DESIGN SYSTEM REMAINS THE VISUAL AUTHORITY.** Recorded in `docs/PHASE-6-FINAL-UX-CONTRACT.md` and tracked in `docs/PHASE-6-FINAL-EXECUTION.md`. It supersedes every earlier reading of Phase 6 in which "preserve the current design" meant "preserve the current page composition or workflow". Where `docs/UI-FIDELITY-CONTRACT.md` calls an affected screen a mechanical port whose composition may not be repositioned, simplified or reorganised, that restriction is SUPERSEDED for every surface the contract redesigns — Home, the sidebar IA, the Setup Wizard, Brand Brain, Strategy, Campaigns, Content Library, Create Post, Assets, Calendar, Publishing, Analytics, Intelligence, the global Copilot, Notifications and Settings. What is NOT superseded: the visual language (identity, colours, typography, tokens, component primitives), accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA), responsive foundations, RTL, and CLAUDE.md §2 in full (tenant isolation, configuration over code, secrets, credits, external side effects, delivery). No second visual system; no recreation of an old composition because the demo used it. Also decided by the same owner specification: English is the default CUSTOMER interface language (revises the D-03 Arabic-default reading for the customer app; interface language and content language are separate), Search is not part of this correction, and the out-of-scope list of §52 is binding. | Owner, 2026-09-24 | `docs/PHASE-6-FINAL-UX-CONTRACT.md`, `docs/UI-FIDELITY-CONTRACT.md`, `docs/PHASE-6-FINAL-EXECUTION.md` |
| **D-278** | **THE FIRST-RUN SETUP WIZARD IS DERIVED FROM REAL DATA, REUSES THE PRODUCT'S OWN ACTIONS, AND STORES THE FIRST GOAL IN THE BRAND'S STRATEGY MEMORY (D-277 §6).** The config-driven onboarding checklist (`onboarding.steps` in the activated `onboarding` policy) no longer drives the customer screen: the owner's contract fixes the journey — Workspace → Brand → Learn → Review → Connect → First goal → Ready — so the step list is product flow, not configuration. The API's `/v1/onboarding` state endpoint and the policy are unchanged. (1) NO STORED PROGRESS: every step is a question asked of rows (`setup-wizard-state.ts`); "Skip for now" is a link; the wizard ENDS at "You're ready to start" and never re-opens itself. "Choose a plan" is no longer a setup step — the trial and the plans live in Settings > Plan / Billing. (2) ONE BRAND-CREATION PATH: `server/brand-creation.ts` (idempotent on the name, brand quota, and — newly — an audited `brand.created`), used by Brand Brain and the wizard; the wizard asks only name, website, industry, languages, colours and an optional logo, decoded by Brand Profile's own rules. The logo goes through the ordinary asset pipeline and becomes the brand's logo only when READY and CLEAN. (3) REUSED ACTIONS: document upload and candidate review are the Brand Brain's actions, connecting is the Connections OAuth action, each returning through a CLOSED-SET `returnTo`. The OAuth callback still lands on Connections; while the wizard is unfinished, a banner there offers the way back. (4) THE FIRST GOAL is a HUMAN knowledge item, area STRATEGY, key `goal.primary`, written through `BrandKnowledgeService` (versioned, audited, editable in Brand Brain) — not an onboarding field. Its options are `CampaignObjective` values; "Build authority" is NOT offered because no objective means it; "I'm not sure" stores nothing. The strategy engine's content-gap check skips `goal.` keys, which are not pillars. (5) `/onboarding` is `brand`-scoped. | Phase 6 final C4 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-279** | **HOME ANSWERS "WHAT NEEDS ME" FIRST AND SHOWS FIGURES LAST (D-277 §7/§8).** Home's order is fixed by the owner: greeting (workspace-clock morning/afternoon/evening, first name only) and selected brand; A — What needs you: the existing Command Center attention sources, each with ONE verb (`ATTENTION_ACTIONS`) and a link; publishing failures and account health now link to Publishing (Failed / Accounts) and additionally require `publishing.read`, so no row is a link to a 404; B — Recommended by BrandSpace: at most three NEW/SEEN, unexpired `RECOMMENDATION`/`OPPORTUNITY`/`CONTENT_GAP` insights in scope, each with its evidence count and basis, View evidence (`/intelligence?insight=`), Give to Copilot and Dismiss (the existing review action, returning through a closed set); C — Notes from your team: the top three `NotesService.inbox` conversations with author, excerpt, subject, relative time, Open and Resolve; D — Coming up this week: live slots for seven days grouped by the workspace's calendar day; E — Performance snapshot: engagement (28 days), published (28 days), scheduled (7 days), in review with pending approvals. ATTENTION and INSIGHT stay distinct sections (§8 A/B); PREFERENCE and WORKFLOW (§8 C/D) arrive with §9/§10. Plan, credits, members, the activity log and the notification count left Home (Settings / top bar). Home invites the reader back into setup only with no brand, or a brand with nothing done after it. | Phase 6 final C5 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-280** | **THE COPILOT IS GLOBAL AND KNOWS WHAT THE PERSON IS LOOKING AT (D-277 §37).** The top bar's Copilot control opens the SAME conversation component (`CopilotView`, with its plan / confirm / undo ceremony unchanged) in the demo's `.side-drawer` over the current screen; it remains a link to the full Copilot for a modified click or no script, and the full screen is one click away inside the drawer. Context: workspace (session), brand (the rail's), surface (the route), and — new — a SUBJECT: the campaign (`/campaigns/:id`), content item (`/content/compose?item=`) or insight (`/intelligence?insight=`) the address names. `copilot_session` gains `subjectType` / `subjectId` (migration `20260924120000_phase_6_copilot_subject`, both-or-neither CHECK limited to the three kinds; the table's FORCE RLS covers them). No foreign key — the subject is one of three tables — so the orchestrator ADMITS it at open against the session's own brand (another workspace's, another brand's, a fabricated or wrong-kind id and a brand-less session are all refused with the same 404, before any row), and re-reads it every turn: only the KIND reaches the unfenced instruction; the title travels in a fenced `CURRENT SUBJECT` block; a subject deleted since is simply absent. The drawer shows "looking at “…”" from a read under the reader's scope. The tool registry is unchanged — no new Copilot powers. | Phase 6 final C6 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-281** | **NOTES ARE CONVERSATIONS ABOUT FOUR SUBJECTS, WITH A DUE DATE AND AN IMPORTANT FLAG — AND NOTHING MORE (D-277 §28).** `NoteSubjectType` gains `ASSET`; `note_thread` gains `assetId` (composite FK to `asset`, ON DELETE CASCADE), `dueAt` (optional) and `importance` (`NORMAL` / `IMPORTANT`) in migration `20260924130000_phase_6_notes_asset_due_importance`, which rewrites the exactly-one-subject CHECK for four subjects (compared as text, because a new enum value cannot be used in the transaction that adds it) and adds the `note_thread_asset_scope` trigger (not SECURITY DEFINER): a thread about a brand's asset must be in that brand; a workspace-shared asset is discussed within a brand the reader names and may act on. The service reads the brand from the asset and refuses foreign, out-of-scope, mismatched, deleted and fabricated assets with one 404. `setDue` / `setImportance` are audited (`customer.note.due_set`, `customer.note.importance_set`). Mentions are a real typeahead (`@Sa` → Sara) over the same active-member population the service accepts; the native select remains for no-script. Assignment (already in the domain) is exposed. Deep links (`noteThreadHref`) open the exact post / campaign / asset / brand with `?thread=` highlighting and `#thread-` scrolling, from the Notes inbox and Home; the "changes requested" notification now opens the post in the composer. NOT added: task statuses, subtasks, Kanban, estimates, dependencies, reminders. | Phase 6 final C7 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-282** | **THE CONTENT LIBRARY IS MEDIA-FIRST, COUNTS REAL STATES AND FILTERS IN THE QUERY (D-277 §15).** `/content` stops being the demo port (`postsPage()` gradient cards — superseded under D-277) and becomes a design-system extension: `LinkTabs` status tabs for every lifecycle state that has content (Drafts always), each badge a real `groupBy` count; a GET filter form (search, brand, campaign, platform, format, language) whose values narrow `ContentLibraryService.listItems` itself (new `contentType` / `platformKey` / `locale` predicates) so nothing is filtered after truncation; Grid and List views as addresses. A card shows the post's REAL first image through an expiring download grant (ready + clean + inline-previewable only), a video tile for a video, and the caption on a neutral surface for a text-only post — never invented art. Cards carry format, platforms, campaign, language, status, relative update time, owner and open-note count. Quick actions: Edit / Open, Request approval (the existing submit action, returning to the library through a closed set), Schedule (opens the calendar's own scheduling dialog with the post preselected, `?item=`), Duplicate (a NEW draft through `createManualItem` — same checks and audit, no lifecycle copied, campaign kept only for `campaigns.manage`, per-render idempotency token). "Ideas worth making" appear only when grounded: a planned/active campaign with no content, an open CONTENT_GAP insight. | Phase 6 final C8 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-283** | **CREATE POST ASKS HOW YOU WANT TO START, OFFERS ONLY FORMATS SOMETHING CAN CARRY, AND KEEPS THE GOAL IN THE BRIEF (D-277 §17-§19).** `/content/compose` without a draft and without `?mode=` is the Create entry: Generate with AI, Write it yourself, Start from an idea, Repurpose a post — each an address (`?mode=ai|write|idea|repurpose`) that carries `?campaign=`. `write` makes Save draft the primary action and labels the field "Your post"; `ai` keeps Generate primary; both verbs stay. IDEAS are grounded only: the brand's first goal (D-278), up to three ACTIVE STRATEGY pillars (not `goal.` keys), up to three DRAFT/PLANNED/ACTIVE campaigns with no content, open CONTENT_GAP insights — each gated on the permission that reads it, with an honest empty state. REPURPOSE lists the member's posts through `ContentLibraryService.listItems` (BrandScope), and the chosen post is read with `getItem(id, brandScope)`; its title and words become explicit, fenced, length-bounded source text in the brief (`repurposeBrief`) and the original is never modified. FORMAT comes first and is filtered by the publishing capability registry (`publishing.providers.<key>.postKinds`): a format is offered only if some enabled provider declares a kind it needs (`FORMAT_POST_KINDS`), and a channel that cannot carry the chosen format is DISABLED with a reason; a platform with no enabled provider can be drafted for as a plain post only. The post GOAL (Educate, Engage, Promote, Build awareness, Generate leads, Launch) is offered only in AI mode, recommended from the brand's first goal and labelled as such, and travels as one sentence appended to the GENERATION brief (inside the same length ceiling, and inside the generation idempotency key) — never stored, never added to a post written by hand. A campaign chosen before AI generation is filed afterwards through the existing audited `setContentCampaignAction` (`campaigns.manage`); the gateway still neither takes nor stores one. No schema change. | Phase 6 final C9 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-284** | **THE DRAFT EDITOR IS CONTEXT / EDITOR / LIVE PREVIEW, SAYS WHAT IS WRONG IN WORDS, AND NEVER SAVES BEHIND THE PERSON'S BACK (D-277 §20-§22, §27).** Once a post exists `/content/compose?item=` renders three tracks: CONTEXT (the lifecycle as a path Draft → In review → Approved → Scheduled → Published, the format and dialect, a quiet "Using {Brand} Brand Brain" that opens to the sources retrieval actually returned, the insufficient-knowledge notice with Add knowledge / Open Brand Brain, the campaign and the upload form); the EDITOR (one tab per platform variant with arrow-key navigation, caption, hashtags, a FIRST COMMENT only where the configured platform `allowsFirstComment`, media, inline AI actions — Shorten, Rewrite, Make friendlier, More professional, Add detail, Generate hashtags, Translate — each one of the content service's own tools, friendly validation computed live in graphemes against the configured limits with the fix beside it: "Your Instagram caption is 128 characters over the limit" + Shorten with AI, "This Reel needs a vertical video" + Choose media); and the LIVE PREVIEW (the approved `SocialPostPreview`, in the post's own format — a Reel drawn as a 9:16 reel — following every keystroke, with Compare previews). A new `hashtags` tool proposes tags and is forbidden from changing the caption (the model's body is discarded; tags are cut to the platform ceiling). AN AI EDIT NOW REVOKES AN APPROVAL exactly as a human edit does (`revokeApprovalOnEdit`, previously private to `editVariant`): before this an inline tool could rewrite an APPROVED caption and leave it saying Approved. An APPROVED post shows "Editing its content or media will return it to Draft and require review again" BEFORE Save. NO BACKGROUND AUTOSAVE, deliberately: every save is audited and a save of an approved post revokes its approval, which must never happen because somebody paused; instead the editor shows Unsaved changes / "Saved just now", guards navigation with `beforeunload`, disables AI edits and Send for review until the words are saved. Saving a caption no longer erases a stored first comment (absent means leave it). The tool idempotency key now includes the variant version and the tone argument, so the same tool after an edit is a new request. No schema change. | Phase 6 final C10 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-285** | **MEDIA IS ORDERED SLIDES, ADDED IN A DRAWER, AND A REEL'S COVER IS A DURABLE COLUMN (D-277 §22-§26).** Each platform version's media is shown as ordered slides ("Slide 01…" for a carousel) that can be dragged or moved with buttons, removed, or replaced; the order IS `content_variant.assetIds`, submitted in order and saved as before. "Add media" opens a `SideSheet` (a new generic overlay in `packages/ui`, the Copilot drawer's geometry and `Dialog`'s focus behaviour) with three ways in: the ONE Asset Library (brand + shared, READY and CLEAN — the server-narrowed list; vertical media first for a Reel, Story or video; aspect and duration shown), UPLOAD through the one pipeline (`uploadComposerMediaAction`, pending its scan), and GENERATE WITH AI through the Creative Studio's own `/api/creative/quote` and `/generate` (brand-grounded, quoted before spending, idempotent on the prompt's fingerprint); a generated image is an ordinary AI_GENERATED asset and the editor re-reads the page until it is usable, then puts it on the slides — never attached before its scan. `content_variant.coverAssetId` (migration `20260924140000_phase_6_variant_cover`: nullable UUID, composite FK to `asset` `ON DELETE SET NULL ("coverAssetId")`, trigger `content_variant_cover_scope` requiring the variant's brand or the shared shelf, not SECURITY DEFINER, FORCE RLS asserted) holds a Reel's or video's cover; `editVariant` accepts it only as an IMAGE the brand may use (the same resolver as media), undefined leaves it, null clears it, and it is audited. The preview draws the cover as the video's poster, shows the measured duration, and pages a carousel (arrows, dots, "Slide 2 of 5") through new optional `slides` / `slideLabel` in `SocialPostPreview`. The Creative Studio's "Use in a post" opens Create Post with `?asset=`; the server admits it only when READY, CLEAN, not deleted and this brand's or shared, and it is placed on the new draft's slides UNSAVED (`?attach=`) — the ordinary save re-resolves it. AI carousel outlines are NOT built (no per-slide text model exists; §23 says not to fake one). | Phase 6 final C11 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-286** | **EXPIRED RIGHTS MAY NOT BE ATTACHED OR PUBLISHED — ENFORCED IN THE ONE PUBLISHABILITY PREDICATE (D-277 §30).** `asset.rightsExpiryAt` was stored and shown but decided nothing. It now means "the licence to use this file ends here": `publishableAssetWhere` (shared by `ContentMediaResolver` in the composer and `PublishMediaResolver` in the worker) requires `rightsExpiryAt IS NULL OR rightsExpiryAt > now`, with `now` from each caller's injected clock — so a post scheduled before the date and dispatched after it is refused at dispatch, not merely warned about. The refusal is the same NOT_FOUND a missing or quarantined file gets (one answer, no oracle). A file with no recorded date is unaffected. Isolation tests pin both callers either side of the date. The Asset Library's rights badges and the composer's explanation of a lapsed slide follow in C12. | Phase 6 final C12 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-287** | **THE ASSET LIBRARY GAINS VIEWS, A DETAIL DRAWER WITH "USED IN", LICENCE AND RIGHTS EDITING, AND BULK ACTIONS — ALL OVER THE EXISTING DOMAIN (D-277 §30).** VIEWS (All, Images, Videos, Documents, Recent = last 7 days, AI generated, Uploaded, Shared, Unused) are `LinkTabs` over new `browse` predicates (`sources`, `createdAfter`, `unusedOnly`) — filters, never stored collections, and named "views". "Unused" excludes every file referenced by a live post's `assetIds`, a `coverAssetId` or a brand logo, via one reviewed raw query with an explicit workspace predicate on every table (RLS applies too). TILES carry dimensions, "Used in N posts" (`usageCounts`, one query per page) and small badges only where meaningful: AI generated, Shared, Rights expiring (≤30 days), Rights expired. The DETAIL is a `SideSheet` drawer (closing navigates back to the same view): large preview, Use in a post (Create Post with `?asset=`, D-285), Download (an attachment grant), Brand/shared, Source, Added by (named through the workspace membership), Licence, Rights end, Folder, Versions and USED IN — `AssetLibraryService.usage` from real `assetIds` / `coverAssetId` references, listing only posts inside the reader's BrandScope and never a deleted post. The edit form adds licence, rights end (the last day covered, closing at 23:59:59.999Z) and folder, each changed only when on the form. BULK Tag / Move / Archive loops the same single-file service calls with the same permission, scope and per-file audit; files the member may not change are skipped and reported. The composer no longer offers a lapsed file and explains an attached one ("The licence has ended for N of this post's files"). BRAND KIT (§31): a card above the views for the selected brand — primary/secondary logos from the Brand Profile's own asset references (inline grants), palette and typography from the profile, and a link to Brand settings to edit; a view, nothing copied. | Phase 6 final C12 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-288** | **APPROVALS FEEL LIKE ONE FLOW — THE SAME APPROVAL RECORD, NO SECOND WORKFLOW (D-277 §29).** The composer's next step follows the brand's own `requireApprovalBeforeScheduling`: a brand without it shows Schedule as the primary action (the calendar's own dialog, preselected with `?item=`, under `content.schedule`) and keeps Send for review available; a brand with it makes Send for review primary, says "This brand needs approval before a post is scheduled", and offers no Schedule until the post is APPROVED. When changes were requested, the composer shows "{reviewer} requested changes" and the reviewer's reason (the approval's `decisionNote`), and one action — Resolve and send for review again — posts the author's optional answer on the thread the decision opened, resolves it, and calls the SAME `approvals.submit` (next cycle, same permission, policy and audit; the answer becomes the request note). The thread is derived, not stored: open threads on the post whose note is the reviewer's own `decisionNote`, created after the decision. The reviewer's screen now shows the post as it will look (the composer's `VariantPreview`, in the post's format — `contentType` added to the review subject) and the post's Notes panel beside the verdict. No multi-step chains, no guest approval; `/approvals` stays for queue access and is not a sidebar module. | Phase 6 final C13 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-289** | **A CAMPAIGN IS A PROJECT ROOM OVER THE EXISTING DOMAIN (D-277 §14).** `/campaigns/[id]` gains a header (status, goal, dates, channels, owner via the workspace membership, brief) and `LinkTabs` addresses: OVERVIEW (progress "N of M posts published" with per-status counts, approvals waiting with a link to the filtered content, next scheduled slot, the performance snapshot, recent activity, the campaign's Notes panel — notes stay contextual, never a tab — and the edit/archive form folded into Details for `campaigns.manage`); CONTENT (status filter over the real lifecycle, each row with first picture, format, platforms, language, status and open-note count; Write a post carries `?campaign=`); CALENDAR (this campaign's calendar slots in time order, and Open in the calendar with `?campaign=`, which the calendar honours from C15); ASSETS (files the campaign's posts use — `assetIds` and covers — from the one library; no Campaign↔Asset association was invented, because "prepared but unused" is not needed yet); PERFORMANCE (What changed = the analytics summary against the previous 30 days; What contributed = `topPosts` by engagements in the campaign scope; What you can try = open insights linked to the campaign, each opening Intelligence where the Copilot drawer picks it up; then the metrics); ACTIVITY (the one `ActivityLogService`, narrowed by a new `resourceIds` filter to the campaign and its posts — it narrows the viewer's scope, never widens it). No schema change. | Phase 6 final C14 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-290** | **THE CALENDAR PLANS FROM WHAT IS WAITING, AND SAYS ONLY WHAT IT HAS MEASURED (D-277 §32).** FILTERS brand / platform / campaign / status are a GET form (addresses, kept across month navigation) that NARROW the month already read under RLS and BrandScope. THE UNSCHEDULED TRAY ("Unscheduled · N") lists DRAFT and APPROVED posts with a variant and NO live slot (a slot not CANCELLED / PUBLISHED / FAILED), both conditions in the query (`listItems({ unscheduledOnly })`) so the 200-row page is never spent on posts the tray would drop — the same set the scheduling service accepts, narrowed by the same filters; eight rows show before a Show all toggle so the grid stays in reach; each row is DRAGGABLE onto a day of the month grid (`ContentCalendar.onDropDay`, desktop only because the grid is desktop only) and has a SCHEDULE BUTTON — the keyboard and touch path, never an afterthought. A drop only opens the existing schedule dialog with the post and the day filled in: the time is confirmed by a person and the one `scheduleContentAction` runs, so moving a card never publishes anything by itself. THE WEEK VIEW shows the week containing today. THE POST DRAWER replaces the modal with the shared `SideSheet`: the live `VariantPreview` (format, media, first variant), when, platforms, campaign, status, approval, open notes, readiness, then Reschedule / Cancel / Request approval (a DRAFT, `content.submit`, the one `submitForReviewAction` returning to the same month) / Open the full post as permissions allow. NO BEST-TIME RECOMMENDATION: nothing in the analytics domain measures performance by posting hour per brand with enough evidence, so none is shown rather than a guess. ONE QUIET SUGGESTION: "Tuesday has been empty for 5 weeks." — a weekday with no non-cancelled slot in the five whole weeks before this one, said only when the window holds at least five slots and at most two weekdays are empty (a brand that does not post daily is not told so), with Give to Copilot for members with `copilot.use`; a line, never a popup. The readiness banner's fix now points at Publishing › Accounts instead of the technical Integrations page. No schema change. | Phase 6 final C15 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-291** | **PUBLISHING SAYS WHETHER EACH POST CAN GO OUT, AND A RECONNECTED ACCOUNT LEADS BACK TO A REAL RETRY (D-277 §33).** QUEUE rows show the post's first picture (cover, else first media; a neutral text tile for a text post), the platform, brand and time, READINESS from the job's own account (`Ready to publish` when ACTIVE, `Account needs reconnecting` otherwise), the post's latest review, and the account name only for `integrations.read`. TWO DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED. (1) RECONNECT COULD NOT SUCCEED: reconnecting an account whose connection was still live (NEEDS_REAUTH, or ACTIVE near expiry) created a second row, which `social_connection_one_live_per_account` refused. `SocialOAuthService#connect` now RENEWS the existing row in place for the same brand, provider and external account — old credential retired, a new version stored (as `refresh` does), status ACTIVE (or NEEDS_REAUTH on a partial grant), no second plan slot, audited `social.connection.reconnected`. (2) NO HONEST RETRY AFTER A FIX: `retry()` correctly refuses `AUTH_REVOKED` / `INSUFFICIENT_SCOPE` while the account is broken, and nothing said when it no longer was. `PublishPipelineService.retryOnReconnectedAccount` re-queues a FAILED job whose class is an account failure (`AUTH_EXPIRED`, `AUTH_REVOKED`, `INSUFFICIENT_SCOPE`; never an indeterminate one) only when the SAME account is ACTIVE with a credential renewed AFTER the failure — the job's own connection, or after a disconnect a new row for the same external account, to which the job is RE-BOUND with the key `materialiseSlot` would have written (one job per slot and account, so the lifecycle stays honest). It only queues; `#preflight` re-checks approval, cancellation, content and rights before anything is sent; audited with `via: reconnected_account`. `reconnectedRetryable` gives the screen the same rule, so the Failed tab shows "Account reconnected. You can retry now." with Retry exactly when it would be accepted, and Reconnect otherwise. After the OAuth round trip (which always lands on Connections, D-141) a banner counts the failed posts now retryable and links to Publishing › Failed. Nothing retries on its own. No schema change. | Phase 6 final C16 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-292** | **STRATEGY IS THE BRAND'S PLAN, READ FROM THE ACCEPTED STRATEGY; AI PROPOSALS SIT APART (D-277 §13).** `/strategy` reads top to bottom as Current objective (the brand's first goal `goal.primary` from the Setup Wizard, and the accepted strategy's summary, basis and date) · Audience and Key messages (Brand Brain's approved AUDIENCE, and OFFERS + PROOF_POINTS items — the strategy engine does not generate these, so nothing is invented; an empty section says so and links to Add knowledge) · Content pillars and Channel mix (shares as bars with the number, rationale and the evidence ordinals they rest on; declared Brand Brain pillars shown when no strategy weights them) · Monthly plan (each week: theme, posts planned, rationale, and Create campaign / Send to Content / Give to Copilot) · Evidence and source (stored `insight_evidence` rows) · Suggested changes (NEW/SEEN proposals marked "AI proposal", with Accept — which now says it may influence future suggestions — Dismiss and Propose learnings; the request form, pre-filled from the first goal so the question is not asked twice). The body is parsed defensively by a pure `server/strategy-view.ts` (malformed entries dropped, never guessed). A week's actions are ADDRESSES: `/campaigns/new` now accepts `name`, `brief`, `objective` (closed set) and `channels` (configured platforms only) as a prefill, and Create Post takes the theme as its `brief` — nothing is created until the person saves. Copilot gains a `strategy` surface (both closed lists and its label). EVIDENCE LABELS: rows without a metric printed their stored key (`content.pillar_unpublished`) on Strategy and Intelligence; `evidenceLabel()` now renders a sentence in EN/AR for every key the engines emit, and a neutral "Evidence" for an unknown one. No schema change. | Phase 6 final C17 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-293** | **ANALYTICS OPENS WITH THE STORY, THEN THE NUMBERS; INTELLIGENCE STATES PERIOD, SCOPE AND CONFIDENCE ONLY WHERE REAL; THE LEARNING LOOP IS SHOWN (D-277 §34-§36).** `/analytics` now reads: What changed? (MEASURED lines — each headline metric that moved at least 5% against the previous period, largest first, as a template around the stored change; the detected shift with its baseline and threshold; the period's top post — then the stored explanation's cited `notableChanges`) · Why might this matter? (the latest non-dismissed ANALYTICS_EXPLANATION's summary and cited claims, with "This describes what happened together over the measured period. It is not proof of what caused it."; without one, the explain action and its spend hint) · What can we try? (the explanation's cited recommendations, each with View evidence → `/intelligence?insight=`, the measured next steps, and Give to Copilot) — then totals, trend, platforms, top posts, findings, and the CSV export last as a quiet link. The explanation's claims were already checked against their evidence when stored; `server/analytics-story.ts` reads the body defensively. `/intelligence` headers add the PERIOD (`periodStart – periodEnd`), the SCOPE (brand, or the campaign by name), and a confidence ONLY when `confidenceMilli` is set (no insight writer sets one today, so none is shown); each finding gets Give to Copilot; a five-step line states the loop — noticed → evidence stored → proposed as a learning → a person accepts, edits or dismisses → accepted learnings join Brand Brain — beside the existing per-finding pending/accepted counts. No schema change. | Phase 6 final C18 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-294** | **BRAND BRAIN NAMES THE BRAND AND SAYS WHAT IT KNOWS IN COUNTS; EVERY VALUE HAS PROVENANCE; "ASK" AND "GIVE TO COPILOT" OPEN THE ONE COPILOT OVER THE SCREEN (D-277 §12, §36, §37).** The demo's generic headline is replaced by the selected brand's name and "BrandSpace understands this brand from N approved facts across K of 10 areas and S processed sources" ("doesn't know much yet" when there are none) — counts, never a readiness score. The existing completion figure stays: it is the documented coverage rule over approved knowledge (`packages/brand-brain/src/completion.ts`), not an invented score. A new "What BrandSpace remembers" strip names the engine's four memories for people — Brand knowledge (CANONICAL), Strategy, Content memory, Learnings — with ACTIVE counts, and says how many measured learnings wait for review rather than counting them. Knowledge gaps (areas with no approved knowledge) are buttons that open that area to add to it. Each value in an area now shows "Updated {date} · by {member} · from {document}" from `updatedAt`, `createdByUserId` (a member of this workspace) and `sourceDocumentId` (a document of this brand). The learnings card says where each learning came from, shows the measured evidence sentence, shows a confidence ONLY for measured learnings (computed from deviation and observation count; a document extract's area-match score is not shown as confidence in the fact), and says accepting may influence future strategy and content suggestions. "Ask about this brand" opens the GLOBAL Copilot drawer on the `brand_brain` surface — the one assistant, with the `brand.context` grounded tool — via a new `CopilotLink`: still the full Copilot screen's link without script, and with script a request the shell's drawer answers. Every in-page "Give to Copilot" (Home, Strategy, Analytics, Intelligence, Automations, Calendar) now uses it. The orb's in-page grounded chat (approved demo interaction) remains, reading the same approved knowledge with citations. No schema change. | Phase 6 final C19 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-295** | **A PREFERENCE IS NOTICED FROM A PERSON'S OWN AUDITED EDITS, SHOWN AS SUCH, AND SHAPES THEIR DRAFTS ONLY AFTER THEY CHOOSE IT (D-277 §8-§9).** OBSERVATIONS ARE NOT STORED: they are derived each time from the immutable audit trail — `content.variant.shorten` and `content.variant.tone` events, which now also record the tone chosen (a closed key, never free text) and `afterGeneration` (whether the edit reworked words BrandSpace had generated; editing your own words says nothing about the generator). Two closed shapes: `shorter:<platform>` and `tone:<friendly|professional>:<platform>`, per member and per brand, only past configured thresholds (new `content.learning` configuration: 4 edits across 3 different posts inside 90 days; "Not now" lasts 30 days) — operator configuration, not code. The ONE new model, `member_suggestion` (migration 20260924150000, FORCE RLS, composite brand key, user FK, checks: a snooze always has its date, only a PREFERENCE can be ACCEPTED, keys are closed forms), stores only what the person DECIDED: ACCEPTED ("Make this my default"), DISMISSED ("Don't suggest this again" / "Stop using") or SNOOZED ("Not now" until a date), with the evidence count and source; every decision is audited (`suggestion.preference.<status>`). ACCEPT is refused unless the preference is noticed NOW, so a crafted form cannot manufacture a default. An accepted preference reaches the generator as an instruction the service builds from the closed key for the platforms being written (never from user text), and nothing else — it never enters Brand Brain. Home shows "BrandSpace noticed a preference" (a PREFERENCE, apart from evidence-backed insights) with the sentence, the evidence (N edits across M posts) and the three choices; the AI composer lists "Your defaults for this brand" with Stop using. | Phase 6 final C20 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-296** | **A RECURRING WORKFLOW IS ONE KNOWN SHAPE READ FROM REAL ROWS, OFFERED TO THE COPILOT AS A REQUEST THE PERSON SENDS; AUTOMATIONS LEAD WITH ASKING (D-277 §10, §39).** No process mining. The one shape: this member made a `<language>` `<platform>` post on `<weekday>` (audited `content.item.generated` / `content.item.authored`) and it went onto the calendar for `<weekday>` (a non-cancelled slot), both read in the workspace's zone and counted in DISTINCT WEEKS, past `content.learning.workflowMinRepeats` (4) inside the window; key `weekly:<made>:<platform>:<language>:<planned>`. Home shows it as a "Recurring workflow" (its own kind, apart from insights and preferences) with Give to Copilot, Not now and Don't suggest this again (`member_suggestion` WORKFLOW rows; a workflow can never be ACCEPTED — the database refuses it). Give to Copilot opens the global drawer with the request WRITTEN OUT in the box and nothing sent (`CopilotLink request` → drawer `initialRequest`; without script the full screen's `?ask=`, bounded to 1,000 characters); the person presses Propose, and the existing plan / confirm ceremony applies — any rule comes from the closed automation registry and starts DISABLED, and a proposed publish still asks at run time. The Automations screen now opens with "Automate by asking": the same Copilot path with an example request, stating that Copilot-prepared rules start off, that publishing proposals ask first, and that Home offers recurring workflows; the trigger / condition / action form stays below for building by hand. No schema change beyond D-295's table. | Phase 6 final C21-C22 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-297** | **THE BELL OPENS A FEED OVER THE CURRENT SCREEN, READ FROM TWO REAL SOURCES, WITH THREE TABS (D-277 §40-§41).** The top bar's bell stays the LINK to `/notifications` (progressive: modified click or no script follows it); a plain click opens a side sheet and reads the feed then, not on every render (`loadNotificationFeed` server action). Sources, never copied: the member's notifications (`NotificationService.list`, newest 15) and the Note threads that MENTION them (the Notes inbox the top bar's Notes count reads, newest 10). Each row is who (mentions: the note's author, by membership), what (the template sentence in the reader's language, or "<name> mentioned you"), the object (payload `itemTitle` or the thread's subject / brand), a relative time and an Open link to the exact place (`linkPath`, `noteThreadHref`). A mention shows at most 140 characters of the member's own workspace note; never a caption or document. Tabs: All, Mentions, Approvals (`approval.*` templates) — volume justifies these three; no tab added for symmetry. Unread is a word and an inline-start edge, never colour alone. In-app only: no email / SMS / push preferences exist, so none are offered. No schema change. | Phase 6 final C23 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-298** | **SETTINGS READ AS ONE PLACE: BILLING & USAGE IS ONE SECTION, TEAM LEADS WITH THE PERSON, A POST CARRIES ITS OWN HISTORY, AND BRAND BRAIN REACHES THE BRAND PROFILE (D-277 §11, §41, §44-§47).** (1) Plan and Billing were two Settings rows; they are one row, "Billing & usage", whose two tabs (`LinkTabs`, testId `billing-tabs`) are the existing routes — Plan & payments (`/billing`: subscription, plans, credit packs, invoices, accounting export) and Usage & limits (`/plan`: plan, trial, usage, quotas, allocations, credit history, features). Each route keeps its own `billing.read` check; `/plan` is a `SETTINGS_SUBPATHS` entry so the sidebar still marks Settings current. Generation surfaces keep the existing quote routes (composer, media drawer, Creative) so credits show before spend; no model, provider or token price is shown. (2) Team's first column is the MEMBER: name where the person gave one, the address, and "Joined <date>" from the membership's `acceptedAt`; initials from the name. Role, brand access, status, invite and every change stay on the existing RBAC and BrandScope rules. (3) Contextual timelines share one helper (`activityTimeline`) over `ActivityLogService` with its viewer grading and a resource-id narrowing — no second activity store: the campaign room (campaign + its posts) and, new, the composer's "This post's history" (the post, its variants, review cycles, calendar slots and publish jobs; newest 8). Each row: the event in words (`activityActionLabel`, never the key), who ("You", a member's name, or the translated actor type) and a relative time with the exact moment on `time`. (4) Brand Profile is reached from the brand selector (`manageHref`), Settings > Brand, the Asset Library and now the Brand Brain head ("Brand profile", with `brand.read`). Logos remain Asset Library references; Creative already receives palette, typography and approved knowledge (D-193). No schema change. | Phase 6 final C23 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-299** | **EVERY EMPTY STATE SAYS WHAT TO DO NOW; CUSTOMER COPY SAYS POST, VERSION AND AUTOMATION; A FAILED OR MISSING SCREEN IS ONE LOCALIZED PAGE (D-277 §41-§43, §48-§51).** (1) Empty states pass `StateMessage`'s existing `action` (a new `EmptyAction` link, gated on the destination's own permission): Campaigns → Create your first campaign; a workspace with NO brand (every no-brand state — Campaigns, New campaign, Analytics, Intelligence, Strategy, Creative, Copilot, Brand profile) → Create brand (`/brand-brain`, with `brand.manage`) — the Campaigns list had said "Choose a brand" when none existed; Calendar's empty agenda → Schedule a draft / Create a post (a new optional `emptyAction` on the UI calendar); Assets → Upload / Generate a visual; Content → Create your first post; Publishing queue and published → Open the calendar, accounts → Connect an account ("no failed posts" is good news and offers nothing); Approvals "sent by me" → Open your drafts; campaign room assets → Add media to a post; an empty Brand Brain → Upload brand documents beside its understanding line. Automations, runs, Intelligence, Insights, Notes and Home's week now say what the surface is for.
