(2) Microcopy: "variant" → "version", "Content items" → "Posts", approval notifications name "your post", "automation rule sent its notice" → "An automation sent you a notice", and platform/configuration/payload/job vocabulary is gone from customer copy ("Automation rule" remains on the advanced Automations screen and in the Activity log's resource names). (3) `[locale]/error.tsx` and `[locale]/not-found.tsx`: "This screen could not load — nothing was changed" with Try again / Back to Home and only Next's opaque `digest` as a reference; "We couldn't find that page" with Back to Home — ONE page for a missing record, another workspace's record and a screen the role may not open, still a 404. No `loading.tsx`: pages render the shell themselves, so a route-level loading boundary would replace the whole shell with a bare placeholder; navigation keeps the current screen until the next is ready, and in-page async work already carries its own status (composer, bell, Copilot). (4) Mobile / RTL / a11y: the P6-14 locale sweep (every route × EN/AR × desktop/phone, zero axe violations, inline-end overhang, raw keys/states) plus a new overlay sweep — the bell's feed and the Copilot drawer open against the inline END (left in Arabic), fit a phone, pass axe and close on Escape — and the not-found page in both languages. No schema change. | Phase 6 final C24 | Approved under D-277 |
| **D-300** | **AN AI CAROUSEL STARTS AS A SLIDE OUTLINE IN THE CAPTION, AND A GENERATION IS QUOTED ON EXACTLY THE PROMPT IT WILL SEND (D-277 §23, §46; AC-11.1).** For `contentType = CAROUSEL` the generation prompt adds a fixed instruction (`carouselOutlineInstruction`): begin each body with one line per slide — "Slide 1 — <hook>", the points, a last "Slide N — <call to action>", 3 to 7 slides — then the caption; words only, no described images. The outline is the caption's own editable text, previewed with the rest; there is NO per-slide text model, so none is faked (§23). Visuals are then chosen or generated per slide with the existing slide controls (D-285). The composer says so under the format selector when Carousel is chosen. The quote and the generation now build the prompt through one private helper (`#generationPrompt`: format, language and dialect, the member's accepted defaults) — the quote request carries optional `contentType` and `locale`, the API passes the caller — because the quote had been pricing a shorter prompt than the one reserved (language, D-295 defaults), which rounding had hidden until the carousel instruction exposed it. | Phase 6 final C24 | `tests/isolation/content-studio-lifecycle.test.ts` |
| **D-301** | **CREATIVE STUDIO SAYS WHAT AN IMAGE WILL DRAW ON, AND THE BRAND'S TYPE ACTUALLY REACHES IT (D-277 §26).** The standalone studio keeps its brief, format, quote-before-spend, generate, adapt to another format, regenerate, save to the library and Use in a post (the same generation stays available inside Create Post, D-285). Above it, a card "What <brand> images draw on": the Brand Profile palette (swatches, the brand's own colours as data), its heading and body faces, and the count of approved IDENTITY / TONE_OF_VOICE notes Brand Brain contributes — the same selection the generation route reads (ACTIVE, at most six) — or a plain statement that there are none and the image follows the brief alone; "Edit brand profile" with `brand.read`. Counted, never scored. DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED: Brand Profile stores typography as `{ heading, body }`, but the generation route read it as a string array, so no image had ever been sent the brand's typography; `brandTypography()` in `@brandspace/creative` reads both shapes and the route and the card share it. No logo is composited (unchanged). No design editor, no video. | Phase 6 final C24 | `tests/unit/phase6-creative-identity.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/phase6-creative.spec.ts` |
| **D-302** | **THE CUSTOMER RUNS A BUSINESS AND A BRAND; THE WORKSPACE STAYS UNDER THE FLOOR (owner-approved final correction §1–4, §25–27).** The backend is unchanged: Platform → Workspace → Brand, `workspaceId` on every tenant row, FORCE RLS, BrandScope, memberships, permissions and the multi-brand data model all stay exactly as they are. What changes is only what the customer is shown. (1) No workspace switcher in the shell; the rail shows the BRAND. (2) With exactly ONE reachable brand the rail shows a static `BrandCard` ("Active brand", linking to the brand profile with `brand.read`) and no selector, and `brand-selection.ts` resolves the sole brand itself on `brand-or-all` routes, so nothing ever asks a single-brand customer to choose. With two or more, the existing `BrandSwitcher` appears unchanged. (3) A person who belongs to several businesses gets one "Switch business" item in the profile menu, and only then. (4) Copy says "business" where it said "workspace" (Business settings, Your business, business account, business timezone …) in both languages; the role catalogue's "Workspace Owner/Admin" is SAID "Owner/Admin" to the customer by `customerRoleName()` without renaming a role. (5) The gate on visible multi-brand is the existing entitlement: the plan quota `brands` (`limit.brands`) decides how many brands a business may create, so a Standard plan (1 brand) never sees a selector; nothing new was invented (§27 audit: no separate "multi-brand" flag exists or was needed). FUTURE MODE, documented not built: Standard = one hidden workspace + one brand; an Agency / multi-brand plan = one workspace holding several client brands, surfaced by the same selector and the same quota — which is why nothing below the shell was removed. | Phase 6 final acceptance A1 | `tests/e2e/brand-context.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/customer-app.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/design-system.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/phase8-*.spec.ts` |
| **D-303** | **SETUP IS A FIVE-STEP WIZARD THAT LOOKS LIKE ONE (§5–7).** The workspace step is gone from the journey (it is still done by construction, so the state machine is unchanged). Steps: Your brand · Teach BrandSpace · Review what we learned · Connect socials · Your first goal, then Ready. A `SetupProgress` line ("Step 2 of 5 · Teach BrandSpace", a progressbar with the same value, an "Exit setup" link) sits above the stepper; the shell is FOCUSED (`WorkspaceShell focus`: no navigation sections, no top-bar links, no Create menu). Brand step: name prefilled from the business name, logo and colours folded into an optional disclosure, content language separate from interface language. Teach copy: "Give BrandSpace the information it needs to understand your brand." Review: grouped by area in disclosures with a count each and a one-line summary; Accept / Reject / Edit, then accept are unchanged. Connect: optional, only providers the integration registry really offers, OAuth only. Goals add CONSISTENCY and AUTHORITY to Awareness, Engagement, Leads, Traffic, Launch, Retention and "I'm not sure"; a goal reaches a campaign only through `campaignObjectiveFor()` (no invented objective) and a post only through `goalForObjective()` (Consistency → Engage, Authority → Educate). Ready: "BrandSpace understands the starting point for {brand}" with Plan with Copilot, Create first post, Go to Home. | Phase 6 final acceptance A2 | `tests/unit/phase6-setup-wizard.test.ts`, `tests/unit/phase6-create-post.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/onboarding-first-brand.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/phase9-commerce.spec.ts` |
| **D-304** | **COPILOT IS ONE LABELLED CONTROL AND ONE CONVERSATION (§8–10).** The top bar's Copilot control carries its word ("✦ Copilot" / "✦ المساعد") on the lavender-strong surface with the pressed-purple label, on desktop and on the phone (the action row wraps on the narrowest widths rather than overhanging), so it is discoverable without hovering. The full Copilot page no longer stacks a separate request card above the conversation: `CopilotBody` takes a live `composer` (controlled textarea, Enter sends, one send button), the context line is the header, the history sits between. "Give to Copilot" still opens the same drawer with the same context. | Phase 6 final acceptance A3 | `tests/e2e/phase6-topbar.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/design-system.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/analytics-copilot.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/phase6-workflows.spec.ts` |
| **D-305** | **FOLDERS ARE PLACES, AND LONG LIBRARIES PAGE (§11–17).** The Media Library shows a breadcrumb ("Media Library / Campaigns / National Day") and the current folder's SUBFOLDERS as folder cards (`FolderIcon`), then its files. New folder defaults its parent to the folder the reader is in (the real `parentFolderId`; the existing depth limit is unchanged) and returns there. Tags stay a separate filter (capped at twelve), never a folder. The per-brand scope chips ("Flow 54f8b0" clutter) are gone: a single-brand business sees no scope control and no "Shared" view; several brands get ONE compact brand select. The Asset Library keeps its cursor paging with a "Next page" / "First page" pair; the Content Library pages by 48 with Previous / Next (`listItems` gains a bounded `offset`, stable order `updatedAt desc, id asc`). | Phase 6 final acceptance A4 | `tests/e2e/phase6-media-folders.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/assets.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/assets-pagination.spec.ts` |
| **D-306** | **THE REMAINING VISUAL FINDINGS (§18–24, §28).** (1) Create Post has one title: the shell says "New post" before a draft exists and "Edit post" once one is open, and the inner heading is the draft's own title. (2) "Brand knowledge coverage", never "completion". (3) The calendar opens on the Agenda below 768px. (4) Intelligence, Strategy and Analytics cite evidence in words ("evidence 1"), never "e1"; E2E fixtures no longer put run tags in text a reader sees. (5) A text-only post is a designed card (lavender surface, "Text post" with the note glyph, the post's words) and the compact list tile keeps only the glyph. (6) Campaign Activity is a footnote: its latest entries are in the Overview with an "All activity" link; `?tab=activity` still works and shows as a tab only while open. (7) An image whose grant does not resolve steps aside (`MediaImage`), so a tile shows its neutral bed, never the browser's broken-image glyph. (8) The calendar's unscheduled tray is an even grid; the wizard clears the sticky header's fade. | Phase 6 final acceptance A5 | `tests/e2e/phase6-campaign-room.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/phase6-analytics-story.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/phase6-strategy.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/phase6-calendar.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/phase6-final.screenshots.spec.ts` |
| **D-307** | **SIMPLE AND ADVANCED ARE TWO PRESENTATIONS OF ONE CONTROL CENTER, AND THE MODE IS NEVER AN ACCESS CONTROL.** The owner reads Simple (the default); technical operations read Advanced, which is every existing screen unchanged. The mode is a `__Host-bs_console_mode` cookie holding `simple` or `advanced`, set by a server action (so the framework's origin check applies) that requires a signed-in operator and redirects only to a validated path inside the same locale's console. No page guard, server action or service reads it: every page resolves its permission first and only then chooses markup, which a unit test asserts. Where a route already existed, both presentations share the URL, so every existing action's redirect lands on the right screen and switching keeps the reader in place; new routes exist only where no screen did (`/console/ai`, `/ai/connect`, `/ai/profile`, `/console/usage`). A cookie rather than a database column because it is a per-browser display preference with no audit meaning, like the sidebar collapse; switching writes no audit event. | Owner Control Center Simple + Advanced Mode contract | `apps/admin/src/server/console-mode*.ts`, `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/console/mode/actions.ts`, every console `page.tsx` |
| **D-308** | **TWO NAVIGATIONS; EACH CARRIES THE OTHER'S ROUTES HIDDEN.** Simple lists Home, Customers, Plans & Pricing, Features, AI, Integrations, Usage & Billing and System, each behind the same permission as the screen it opens. Support and the raw audit log are Advanced tools. Advanced keeps the existing sections, labels, order, permissions and `nav-nav.*` test hooks exactly. `ShellNavItem.hidden` (additive, unused by the customer dashboard) lets a mode know the other mode's routes for the top-bar title without drawing them. An Advanced screen opened in Simple mode renders normally with a note offering the switch, and is never redirected or 404d, because the mode does not decide access. | Owner Control Center Simple + Advanced Mode contract | `apps/admin/src/components/console-nav.ts`, `packages/ui/src/app-shell.tsx` |
| **D-309** | **THE CONTROL CENTER'S TOP BAR HAS NO PLACEHOLDER CONTROLS.** It carried the customer product's search box, bell and "+ Create", each opening a "not connected yet" panel. The Control Center has no search index, notification feed or single thing to create, so they are removed and the top bar carries the mode switch and the language switch. `TopbarActions` is deleted from `packages/ui` because nothing renders it (the customer bar moved to `TopbarLink`/`TopbarCreateMenu` in P6-16). The language switch now points at the same screen in the other language instead of the console home. | Owner Control Center Simple + Advanced Mode contract | `apps/admin/src/components/admin-shell.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/topbar-actions.tsx` |
| **D-310** | **THE CONTROL CENTER OPENS IN ENGLISH (owner decision, contract §21).** `CONTROL_CENTER_DEFAULT_LOCALE = 'en'` in `@brandspace/ui` and the admin middleware redirects a locale-less path to it. Arabic remains full RTL at `/ar`. `DEFAULT_LOCALE` stays Arabic for the public website (D-03 unchanged). Interface language only. | Owner Control Center Simple + Advanced Mode contract | `packages/ui/src/direction.ts`, `apps/admin/src/middleware.ts` |
| **D-311** | **"IS BRANDSPACE READY FOR CUSTOMERS?" IS DERIVED ON EVERY REQUEST, NEVER STORED.** Six areas: email, file storage, AI, payments, plans (required) and social apps (optional, per the registry's own `requiredInProduction`). An integration is **Connected** only when a provider is active, selectable in this environment, fully configured AND its newest recorded connection check passed; a saved but untested key is "Needs attention: not tested". A development stand-in is **Test stand-in** and never counts as ready, however well it works. Plans are ready when one is active, public and priced. A role that cannot see an area is told so, and the verdict is then "cannot be confirmed" rather than a guess. No onboarding table: visiting a form completes nothing. The attention list is built from real sources (the health verdict, the billing inbox, readiness gaps, past-due and trial counts, stuck AI requests, open configuration drafts), and each item links to the screen that resolves it. | Owner Control Center Simple + Advanced Mode contract | `apps/admin/src/server/owner-readiness.ts`, `apps/admin/src/server/owner-overview.ts` |
| **D-312** | **A WHOLE-SETTING CHANGE IN SIMPLE MODE IS ONE PASS THROUGH THE EXISTING LIFECYCLE.** `proposeAndActivate` makes the same four `ConfigurationService` calls the Configuration page makes, in order (create draft, validate, preview, activate), with the owner's reason and an explicit confirmation passed as the high-impact acknowledgement (never defaulted true). It refuses, before writing, when an unfinished draft of the same setting is open (`DRAFT_OPEN`: activating over it would supersede its ground, and folding into it would activate edits nobody reviewed here) or when the change is a no-op (`UNCHANGED`), and it discards its own draft if validation or activation refuses. It backs the AI profile change, which had no action at all, and the feature access change. **An open draft is shown before it blocks anything**: Features and AI profile name the unfinished change (setting, when it was saved, its reason) and withhold their controls while it is open. They offer the two ways forward: review it in Advanced, or discard it deliberately (`discardOpenDraftsAction`, a reason and confirmation, `ConfigurationService.discardDraft`, audited as `config.draft.discarded`). The discard is limited to `feature-flags`, `entitlements` and `ai.capability-routing`. Before this, the Advanced screens offered no way to discard a draft in those domains, only to finish it. Both require `platform.configuration.manage` AND `.activate`, and the service re-checks each. | Owner Control Center Simple + Advanced Mode contract | `apps/admin/src/server/simple-config.ts`, `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/console/ai/actions.ts` |
| **D-313** | **PLANS ARE EDITED IN MAJOR UNITS AND DESCRIBED IN PLAIN WORDS, THROUGH THE SAME PLAN ACTIONS.** The Simple editor posts to the existing `savePlanAction` with `priceUnit=major`. The Advanced form does not send it and still posts minor units. Conversion uses each currency's own ISO 4217 minor digits (JPY 0, KWD 3) and REFUSES extra decimals, signs or separators rather than rounding, because a rounded price is a different price. Pending changes are described plan by plan from the active and draft catalogues ("Fixture Starter: price (USD, monthly) $26.00 → $27.50"), beside the service's own validation and over-limit preview and the count of customers on each plan. They go live only through the existing validate, activate (dual control and acknowledgement enforced by the service) and discard actions. Rollback and version history stay in Advanced. | Owner Control Center Simple + Advanced Mode contract | `apps/admin/src/server/money.ts`, `apps/admin/src/server/plan-diff.ts`, `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/console/plans/actions.ts` |
| **D-314** | **"WHO GETS A FEATURE" IS THE ENGINE'S OWN ANSWER, AND SIMPLE MODE ONLY OFFERS WHAT IT CAN ENFORCE.** Everyone and Nobody set the flag's `globalEnabled`. Selected plans sets each plan's grant in `entitlements` (keeping any limit, period and option, and adding a row only where the answer differs from the feature's default) and clears the global setting so the grants decide. This is offered only for a boolean feature whose flag has no kill switch and no advanced targeting (workspace lists, plan list, cohorts, countries, dates, percentage). Anything else is shown as "Turned off in an emergency", "Custom targeting" or "Set per plan" and changed in Advanced, so nothing set there is overwritten by a three-way switch. The action re-checks every precondition against the active documents. Customer exceptions are counted and shown as still applying. **Validated against seeded data**: `tests/e2e/seed-features.ts` (development-only, refuses production and non-local databases) registers four documented product features no code path gates on yet: `approvals.workflow` (granted to the Growth fixture plan, as the product matrix has it), `intelligence.market`, `governance.advanced` and `security.sso`. It uses the same draft, validate and activate an operator would. The owner suite switches Team approvals on for everyone, off for everyone, and back to its plan grant, and asserts each state. Selected plans on a feature that currently has a global setting takes two activations (grants first) and is not atomic across the two domains (F-94). **One narrow Customer Dashboard change followed from the seeded data**: the customer Plan screen (`/plan`) printed every feature by its KEY, in both the features table and the upgrade list. It went unnoticed while the registry was empty, because only the synthesised `limit.*` dimensions were listed. With real registered features, a customer would read `approvals.workflow`, and the P6-14 locale sweep correctly refused it. The screen now shows the feature's registered name in the reader's language, falling back to the key only where the registry has no name (a quota dimension). This is the same rule P6-13 applied to plan names (`featureDisplayName` beside `planDisplayName` in `apps/dashboard/src/server/plan-usage.ts`). `FeatureDefinition` gains the optional `name` the `entitlements` schema already requires. Nothing resolves on it, and no other customer screen changed. | Owner Control Center Simple + Advanced Mode contract | `apps/admin/src/server/feature-access.ts`, `apps/admin/src/app/[locale]/console/features/simple-actions.ts` |
| **D-315** | **ACTIONS THAT SPEND CREDITS ALSO NEED `copilot.use` (Q18).** Every AI or credit-spending action a person starts keeps its own feature permission and additionally requires `copilot.use`. Members without it see neither credit-spending buttons nor the credit balance. System and background execution keep their existing authorization. | Owner, 2026-09-25 (`docs/PROTOTYPE-V76-ALIGNMENT.md` §5.0) | One simple rule for who may spend AI credits, without replacing the per-feature keys. |
| **D-316** | **BRAND BRAIN COMPLETENESS IS KEY QUESTIONS PER AREA, NEVER A SCORE (Q19).** Each area shows "answered n of m" key questions from configuration (Offers questions depend on the industry); no percentage and no overall score. "What's missing" lists the top unanswered questions. | Owner, 2026-09-25 (`docs/PROTOTYPE-V76-ALIGNMENT.md` §5.0) | Keeps the existing no-score rule while telling the customer what to add next. |
| **D-317** | **ALL AI WRITING IS GROUNDED IN APPROVED, NON-EXPIRED FACTS ONLY (Q20, extends Q14).** Captions, strategy, Copilot and Brand Brain chat retrieve only approved facts that have not expired. Uploaded documents and websites propose pending facts; their raw chunks are never retrieved into prompts. | Owner, 2026-09-25 (`docs/PROTOTYPE-V76-ALIGNMENT.md` §5.0) | Makes "Used N facts" and "Fix it" accurate and keeps unreviewed text out of writing. |
| **D-318** | **ATTACHING A CAMPAIGN TO A POST THAT HAS NONE NEEDS `content.create`; MOVING OR REMOVING NEEDS `campaigns.manage` (Q21, clarifies Q11 / D-224 / D-232).** The same rule applies in the Posts menu and in the Studio. | Owner, 2026-09-25 (`docs/PROTOTYPE-V76-ALIGNMENT.md` §5.0) | Choosing a first campaign is part of creating the post; changing one changes what a campaign reports. |
| **D-319** | **A TIME-ZONE CHANGE KEEPS SCHEDULED POSTS AT THEIR LOCAL CLOCK TIME (Q22).** Every future `PLANNED`/`SCHEDULED` slot is re-computed in the new zone in one transaction; slots that are `PUBLISHING` or later are untouched. The settings warning says so, and the change is an audit event. | Owner, 2026-09-25 (`docs/PROTOTYPE-V76-ALIGNMENT.md` §5.0) | 09:00 should stay 09:00 for the customer who planned it. |
| **D-320** | **TWO-STEP VERIFICATION IS AUTHENTICATOR (TOTP) ONLY, WITH 10 BACKUP CODES (Q23).** No SMS. Setup: QR code or typed key, a 6-digit check, then the codes. Turning it off needs the password or a current code; "New phone" re-enrols with a current code. The owner can require it for the workspace: members without it enrol at their next sign-in and cannot turn it off while it is required. | Owner, 2026-09-25 (`docs/PROTOTYPE-V76-ALIGNMENT.md` §5.0) | Matches the existing MFA implementation and adds an owner requirement. |
| **D-321** | **"VIEW AS" IS A READ-ONLY PERMISSION PREVIEW ON THE SUPPORT-MODE PATTERN (Q3, re-confirmed).** No session switch; every change action is refused with "Preview only"; each preview start is audited; offered only for members who have joined, not pending invites. It is not the impersonation D-28 prohibits. | Owner, 2026-09-25 (`docs/PROTOTYPE-V76-ALIGNMENT.md` §5.0) | Lets an owner check what a teammate sees without acting as them. |
| **D-322** | **"NO ACCESS TO THIS PAGE" FOR THE KNOWN NAVIGATION LIST ONLY (Q5, E2).** A page listed in the sidebar, the Settings list or the top bar (`KNOWN_PAGE_PERMISSIONS`) that the member's role does not open renders "No access to this page" inside the normal shell, answered 200, with the E6 denial (who lacks which permission, and who can change the role). Every other URL, and every record inside those pages — another brand's post, an unknown id — keeps the identical 404. Amends the page half of D-299 and docs/SECURITY.md §20.3. | Owner, 2026-09-25 (`docs/PROTOTYPE-V76-ALIGNMENT.md` Q5; Phase 2A approval) | Every member can already see these routes listed, so naming them leaks nothing; a 404 for a page the member knows exists read as a broken product. CLAUDE.md §2.1 is kept because nothing off the list, and no record, changes shape. |
| **D-323** | **THE VIEWER READS CONTENT AND MAY ONLY COMMENT — ROLLED OUT IN TWO RELEASES (Q12; amends D-62 and D-130 prospectively).** Release 1 (prototype v90 Phase 2A) adds `notes.manage` — resolving, reopening, assigning, due date, importance, assigning a new thread and replying to a resolved one — granted by an idempotent data migration (`20260926090000_notes_manage_permission`) and by the catalogue definitions to every role that held `content.read`, the Analyst included through a named exemption in the read-only boundary test, so no role loses anything. It also builds the Viewer's read-only surfaces: Approvals without decision controls, the Home "waiting for your feedback" section linking to the calendar (E7), and no Copilot tools without `copilot.use`. `client_viewer` keeps exactly `['workspace.read']` in release 1. Release 2 is a separate small PR that grants `client_viewer` `content.read` in a migration, the seed and the bootstrap together; it is deployed after release 1 and never together with it, on staging and on production. The Viewer still never creates, submits, approves, schedules, publishes or spends credits. | Owner, 2026-09-25 (`docs/PROTOTYPE-V76-ALIGNMENT.md` Q12; Phase 2A approval) | The code that protects triage must be live before any grant that would expose it, so no deployment window lets a Viewer resolve or assign a thread. |

## 6. Risk Register

| Risk                                              | Likelihood | Impact       | Mitigation                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ---------- | ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Social platform app review delays launch          | **High**   | High         | Start reviews in Phase 1–2 (D-19); design so launch can proceed with a subset of platforms                                                                          |
| Arabic AI output quality below expectation        | Medium     | **High**     | Evaluate models on Arabic marketing copy before Phase 4 (D-17); Brand Brain grounding; multi-provider routing so a better model can be swapped in without a release |
| AI provider price increase erodes margin          | Medium     | High         | Credit abstraction insulates customers; margin monitoring and alerts; routing can move traffic to cheaper models instantly                                          |
| Tenant isolation defect                           | Low        | **Critical** | Two enforcement layers, mandatory isolation tests, CI coverage gate, penetration test before launch                                                                 |
| Credit accounting bug (over/under charging)       | Medium     | High         | Immutable ledger, reserve/settle, concurrency tests, nightly reconciliation with alerting                                                                           |
| Duplicate social posts damaging customer trust    | Medium     | High         | Idempotency keys, verify-don't-retry on uncertain outcomes, per-target job identity                                                                                 |
| Scope creep delaying the MVP                      | **High**   | Medium       | Phase gates with explicit exit criteria; the vertical slice is deliberately narrow                                                                                  |
| Secret leakage                                    | Low        | **Critical** | Vault abstraction, no-reveal UI, redaction layer, CI secret scanning, bundle scanning                                                                               |
| Social platform API breaking changes              | High       | Medium       | Independent connectors, contract tests with recorded fixtures, per-platform disable switch                                                                          |
| Small team, broad scope                           | **High**   | High         | Modular monolith, shared language, heavy reuse of managed services, strict phase discipline                                                                         |
| Payment provider unavailable in the target market | Medium     | High         | Provider abstraction and the option of a second regional provider (D-21)                                                                                            |
| Prompt injection via uploaded brand documents     | Medium     | High         | Untrusted-data delimiting, server-side per-call authorization, allowlisted tools, output validation                                                                 |

---

Three independent blockers point the same way: the configuration domains are unreadable to `brandspace_app`, `asPlatform()` requires a platform actor with verified MFA and is not a path a customer action can take, and the gateway's own transactions would run with `app.current_workspace_id()` NULL. | Phase 5A (W5)                      | **Resolved:** customer-initiated AI executes in `apps/api`, the designated platform surface. | F-09 | ~~**The KMS key provider is an interface, not an implementation.**~~ **RESOLVED — AWS KMS, ALL THREE KEY DOMAINS.** `KmsKeyProvider` wraps and unwraps the data key through AWS KMS `Encrypt`/`Decrypt`, binding the caller's context as a KMS `EncryptionContext` and pinning `KeyId` on decrypt so a ciphertext from another key is refused rather than silently accepted. The envelope is unchanged: the payload is still AES-256-GCM locally and KMS only ever sees the 32-byte data key. **ONE IMPLEMENTATION, THREE DOMAINS.** The first cut made only the PLATFORM domain production-capable, which would have left the publish worker unable to unseal a customer's OAuth token and the login surface unable to resolve a customer's MFA seed — discovered on the day the first customer connected an account or enabled MFA, not at boot. `SOCIAL_TOKEN_VAULT_KMS_KEY_ARN` and `CUSTOMER_MFA_VAULT_KMS_KEY_ARN` complete it through the same class and the same `KeyDomain` abstraction; a domain is a pair of variable names, never a third copy of a cipher. `createKeyProvider()` still refuses the local development provider when `NODE_ENV=production` — that reason (the key sits beside the data it protects) did not change; what changed is that the refusal now names a configuration an operator can apply. **AND THE KEK IS NO LONGER REQUIRED IN PRODUCTION**, nor a fallback there: requiring it forced every deployment to carry three keys it could not legally use. | Phase 2A, resolved in `packages/vault/src/key-provider.ts` and `packages/shared/src/env.ts` | **Why it mattered:** until this landed, a production deployment could not seal ANY secret in ANY domain, because the only provider permitted in production threw from both methods. The vault existed and could not hold a secret. **The blast radius is now enforced rather than described:** each service refuses to start holding a key domain it may not reach, in either form, and each has its OWN IAM principal scoped to exactly its keys — so the separation survives a leaked credential pair. **Operator action:** three KMS keys per environment and four IAM users; docs/RAILWAY-DEPLOYMENT.md §25. | **Resolved.** AWS KMS chosen by the owner, 2026-09-19; extended to all three domains at the owner's direction the same day. |
| F-10 **FIXED IN THIS PHASE** | **`platform_user` was readable by the tenant application role.** The identity migration created the table and the RLS migration then granted `brandspace_app` every privilege on ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public. No policy was ever added, so `SELECT "passwordHash", "mfaSecretRef" FROM platform_user` succeeded as the tenant role — any SQL-injection defect in a customer request path would have exposed the Platform Owner's Argon2id hash. | Phase 2A (found while writing `tests/isolation/platform-auth.test.ts`) | Fixed by migration `20260901210500_platform_user_isolation`: RLS enabled and forced, a policy naming `brandspace_platform` only, and every privilege revoked from `brandspace_app`. The class of bug is closed by the isolation gate now requiring EVERY model to be classified — a model nobody classified fails the build instead of silently defaulting to "not tenant data". | **Done** |
| F-11 | **Several drafts can exist per configuration domain at once**, and the console edits the newest. | Phase 2A (`packages/config/src/service.ts`) | Not a defect — two administrators drafting in parallel is legitimate — but the console shows only one of them, which is confusing. Either surface all open drafts, or require one draft per domain and say so. | Phase 2B, with the per-domain form editors (D-30). |
| F-12 | **Configuration cache invalidation is per-process.** `InMemoryConfigCache` has a 30-second TTL; activating on one instance leaves other instances serving the previous payload until their entries expire. | Phase 2A (`packages/config/src/service.ts`) | Acceptable at one instance and bounded at 30 seconds beyond that. Before running multiple instances, publish activation on Redis pub/sub and invalidate on receipt. | Phase 6 (deployment), or sooner if a second instance is added. |
| **R-01** ~~**RESOLVED**~~ | **CRITICAL — MFA lockout could be bypassed.** `verifyMfa()` never read `lockedUntil`. Failed MFA attempts set the lock, but nothing consulted it, so a session created before the lock could keep submitting codes — and a CORRECT code still signed the attacker in. The counter was also read-modify-write, so parallel attempts overwrote each other and the threshold was never reached. | Independent security review of PR #4 | Enforce the lock inside `verifyMfa()`, revoke pre-MFA sessions when the lock fires, and make the counter a single atomic UPDATE. Never swallow a failure to persist it. | **Done.** 15 regression tests in `tests/isolation/platform-lockout.test.ts`; 9 of them failed against the vulnerable code. |
| **R-02** ~~**RESOLVED**~~ | **CRITICAL — read-only roles could modify secrets and configuration.** Every secret and configuration action was gated on `platform.workspace.read` ("View any workspace"), held by `support_agent`, `billing_manager` and `operations_viewer`. The services checked only that an actor existed and had MFA, so calling them directly bypassed RBAC entirely. | Independent security review of PR #4 | Five new least-privilege permissions (D-35), enforced at page, action AND service level (D-36). | **Done.** 42 table-driven tests in `tests/isolation/platform-rbac.test.ts` plus 14 in `tests/unit/rbac-matrix.test.ts`; 29 failed against the vulnerable code. |
| **R-03** ~~**RESOLVED**~~ | **HIGH — one recovery code could succeed twice concurrently.** `findMany` then `update` by id let two requests read the same unused code and both win. | Independent security review of PR #4 | Consume with `UPDATE ... WHERE usedAt IS NULL` and require exactly one affected row (D-39). | **Done.** Concurrency tests in `tests/isolation/platform-lockout.test.ts`; the two-request test returned `['ok','ok']` before the fix. |
| **R-04** ~~**RESOLVED**~~ | **HIGH — a hard-coded development owner password.** `seed.ts` fell back to a literal committed value. | Independent security review of PR #4 | Remove the fallback; require an explicit, strength-checked variable; fail closed (D-38). | **Done.** 17 tests in `tests/unit/seed-password.test.ts`. |
| **R-05** ~~**RESOLVED**~~ | **MEDIUM — raw internal errors were placed in redirect URLs.** `safeMessage()` returned `error.message`, which then travelled through the address bar, browser history, access logs and `Referer` headers. | Independent security review of PR #4 | Emit only fixed codes plus an opaque correlation id (D-37). | **Done.** 21 tests in `tests/unit/public-error.test.ts` and 22 in `tests/unit/error-boundary.test.ts`. |
| **R-05a** ~~**RESOLVED**~~ | Two adjacent gaps the R-05 boundary tests exposed: `redact()` did not catch a connection string with embedded credentials inside an error MESSAGE (only under a sensitive key), and `sanitizeAttributes` did not drop `error.detail`-style span attributes. | Found while writing the R-05 regression tests | Add a URL-with-credentials value pattern to the shared redaction layer, and treat error detail/stack as forbidden span attributes. | **Done.** |
| F-13 | **Recovery codes are shown once, at enrolment, and cannot be regenerated from the Control Center.** Lockout itself is implemented (10 failed attempts across either factor locks the account for 15 minutes; the refusal is indistinguishable from a wrong password), but an operator who exhausts their codes has no self-service route back. | Phase 2A | Add a recovery-code regeneration screen behind a step-up check, and an operator-visible list of locked accounts. | Phase 2B. |
| F-14 | **Conditional cells in the role matrices are ungranted, not implemented.** docs/SECURITY.md §4.3–§4.4 mark several authorities as 🟡 (Marketing Manager invites "non-admin roles"; Support Agent resends invitations and grants goodwill credits "within cap"; Billing Manager suspends "for non-payment"). The condition — the cap, the role filter — does not exist yet, so the plain permission is NOT granted. | Phase 2B implementation | An ungranted capability is recoverable; an ungated one is not. Implement the conditions, then grant. Asserted as ungranted in tests/unit/phase2b-boundaries.test.ts. |
| F-15 | **`workspace_admin` was defined as "every workspace permission except two", so it silently inherited `billing.manage` the moment that permission was added** — an authority docs/SECURITY.md §4.3 reserves for the Owner. Found by the new RBAC matrix test before it shipped; the grant is now an explicit deny list. Any future blanket grant has the same defect. | Phase 2B implementation | Fixed. Recorded because the PATTERN is the risk, not the single instance. |
| F-16 | **Support Mode has no elevated write grant.** The Blueprint allows one (docs/SECURITY.md §8, "write actions require a separate elevated grant"); Phase 2B issues none, so `assertMayWrite()` always refuses and audits. | Phase 2B implementation | Deliberate: read-only is the safe default, and a write path with no reviewer is worse than none. Needs an owner decision on which writes support may perform. |
| ~~F-17~~ **STALE — implemented in Phase 9, surfaced in Phase 4 (D-254)** | **Customer MFA is optional and not yet implemented.** docs/SECURITY.md §3 makes it optional for customers and mandatory for platform roles; only the platform half exists. | Phase 2B implementation | The customer identity model already carries `mfaEnabled`/`mfaSecretRef`, so this is additive. |
| ~~F-18~~ **STALE — self-serve signup shipped in Phase 9 and creates the account PENDING, which `signIn` refuses; verified by `phase9-account`** | **Email verification is not enforced before a customer's first sign-in.** docs/SECURITY.md §3 requires it. | ~~F-19~~ **RESOLVED (D-250)** | **Rate limiting is per-account, not per-IP.** Both the platform and customer realms lock an account after 10 failed attempts; neither throttles by source address, so an attacker can lock a known account (bounded denial of service) and can spread attempts across many accounts. | Phase 2B implementation | docs/SECURITY.md §10 specifies per-IP limits in Redis. **Closed by current execution Phase 4 in PostgreSQL rather than Redis, for the reasons D-250 gives.** |
| F-20 | **A session-token parameter was passed a user id, and the failure was SILENT.** The workspace picker called `listWorkspaces(customer.userId)` where a session token belongs. Every authenticated end-to-end test failed for a reason that read like missing data. | Phase 2B implementation (found by the E2E suite) | Fixed at the call site AND at the API: an unknown token now raises `UNAUTHENTICATED` rather than returning an empty list (D-45), and the dashboard redirects to sign-in. The residual risk is the PATTERN — two unrelated `string` parameters that are silently interchangeable. A branded `SessionToken` type across the auth package would close it structurally. | Branded token types: next auth change. |
| F-21 | **A whole feature was tested only through the WRONG database role.** Every invitation redemption test ran on the PLATFORM client, which is governed by `platform_access`. The customer application connects as the tenant role, where no policy permitted the read — so `peek()` and `accept()` returned nothing and every invitation link in the product was dead while the suite stayed green. | Phase 2B implementation (found by the E2E suite, not by the isolation suite) | Fixed by D-46, and the redemption path is now asserted on the tenant role. The residual risk is the PATTERN: a service tested on a more privileged client proves nothing about the role that will run it. Worth a sweep of every service the customer application calls, checking each has at least one test on `appRoleClient()`. | Sweep: before the next phase's PR. |
| F-22 | **`INSERT ... RETURNING` is subject to the SELECT policy, and reports the failure as a WRITE error.** A workspace-less outbox row is legitimately writable with no context and deliberately not readable, so Prisma's `create()` failed with "new row violates row-level security policy" for a write that policy actually allowed. | Phase 2B implementation | Fixed by generating the id and using `createMany` (no RETURNING), as the audit path already does. The residual risk is the PATTERN: any table whose `WITH CHECK` is wider than its `USING` will do this. Worth a check of every write-only widening before the next phase. | Sweep: with the F-21 sweep. |
| F-23 | **Isolation suites must BOOTSTRAP what they read, never assume a seed.** CI runs migrations only, so the Phase 2B suites — which read `workspace_admin`, `analyst` and the workspace permission catalogue — passed on a seeded developer machine and failed on a fresh database. | F-24 | **Two style systems had already drifted.** `console-ui.tsx` and `workspace-shell.tsx` implemented the same eight primitives twice, with different input widths, different table borders, one `Field` component, and one table keyboard-scrollable and the other not. The console's `h1` also still used the marketing identity blue on a screen the owner had approved as purple. | Phase 2C-A audit | Both files are now thin adapters over `@brandspace/ui`. The adapters are a MIGRATION SURFACE with a scheduled end, not an API: Phase 2C-B replaces the call sites and deletes them. | Phase 2C-B. |
| F-25 | **A pure function exported from a `'use client'` module cannot be called by a server component.** React treats every export of a client module as a client reference, so `menuItemStyle()` failed at runtime and every authenticated page returned a 500. The residual risk is the PATTERN: nothing yet fails the build when a helper is added to a `'use client'` module. A lint rule would close it. | **Done** (Phase 2C-A visual revision): `tests/unit/design-system.test.ts` reads the first line of every module in `packages/ui/src` and fails any `'use client'` module that exports anything but a PascalCase component, a `use…` hook or a type. A structural test rather than a lint rule: the property is about a module's first line and its export names together, which is awkward to express in ESLint and impossible to forget when it fails the suite. |
| F-26 | **`scrollWidth − clientWidth` is not a reliable overflow measurement.** It counts content parked at negative offsets — Next.js positions its route announcer at `left: -10px` — and reported a 10px overflow on pages that had none, while also masking which element was responsible. | Phase 2C-A implementation | **Done** (Phase 2C-A visual revision). The measurement moved to `tests/e2e/overflow.ts` and ALL FOUR suites now use it — design-system, customer-app, admin-console and responsive. Writing it down also surfaced the worse half of the defect: `scrollWidth` is **blind to the direction that matters in Arabic**, because content pushed past the LEFT edge produces a negative offset rather than a larger scroll width. The proxy could not see the regression it existed to catch. | **Done.** |
| F-27 | **Forty-three form controls were rendered INVISIBLE by the token change, and nothing failed.** Under the borderless model a control's fill, hover, disabled state and focus ring all come from the `.bs-control` class; `inputStyle()` supplies only geometry. Forty-three inputs and selects across the two consoles were styled without that class, so they became transparent rectangles on a white page — legible only once focused. Typecheck, lint and every existing test passed. | Phase 2C-A visual revision (found by auditing the controls, not by a test) | All forty-three now carry the class, and `tests/unit/design-system.test.ts` scans every `<input>`, `<select>` and `<textarea>` in both applications and in `packages/ui`, failing any that is styled with a control helper and missing the class. The residual risk is the PATTERN: a styling system where a missing class means "invisible" rather than "slightly off" needs the guard to stay. | **Done.** |
| F-28 | **A container `opacity` is a silent contrast failure.** `opacity: 0.85` on a feature card to make it read as "quieter" blended its state badge from `#175CD3` toward the page to an effective `#3a74da` on `#f1f9ff` — 4.2:1, an AA failure — with no colour changed anywhere in the source. The same device on the calendar's out-of-month cells would have done it again. | Phase 2C-A visual revision (found by axe on the showcase) | Both removed. Quieting is done with a softer surface and a muted-but-measured text colour. The residual risk is that `tests/unit/contrast.test.ts` measures TOKENS and cannot see a composite opacity, so only the axe run catches this class; it is documented in `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` §2 as a rule rather than left to memory. | Rule documented; axe covers it. |
| F-29 | **An ARIA role brings required structure with it, and a CSS grid does not supply it.** The calendar's `role="grid"` parented `columnheader` and `gridcell` elements directly, because CSS grid needs no row elements — axe reported `aria-required-children` and `aria-required-parent` across 36 nodes. Separately, a tab strip used as a filter left every `aria-controls` pointing at a panel that was never rendered. | Phase 2C-A visual revision (found by axe on the showcase) | Weeks are now wrapped in `role="row"` with `display: contents`, which adds the semantics without adding a box; the posts library renders a real `TabPanel` per status and genuinely filters the records. The residual risk is the PATTERN: a role added for semantics without its companions. | **Done.** |
| F-30 | **The same min-width bug returned as soon as the sidebar widened.** A grid item defaults to `min-width: auto`, so a card holding a table with a `min-inline-size` refuses to shrink and pushes the page sideways — 204px at 768px in the first draft, fixed in `Stack`; 244px again in this revision once the sidebar went from 16rem to 17rem. | Phase 2C-A visual revision (found by the design-system suite) | The floor now lives in `cardStyle()` (`min-inline-size: 0`) rather than in each caller, so a card cannot be laid out without it. | **Done.** |
| F-31 | **No application had a favicon, and the browser's automatic `/favicon.ico` probe was a 404.** Invisible under `chrome-headless-shell`, which does not make the request; the smoke suite's "no browser errors" assertion caught it the moment a full Chromium build was used. | Phase 2C-A visual revision | All three applications now ship `src/app/icon.svg`, so Next.js emits a `<link rel="icon">` and the probe never happens. | F-32 | **A text token measured against ONE ground is not measured.** `textMuted` is 4.61:1 on white — the only surface it had ever been asserted against — and 4.21:1 on `surfaceLavenderStrong`, where it shipped onto the composer's selected account chip. The revision made supporting surfaces the primary structural device, so body text now lands on eight grounds instead of one, and every existing pairing assertion covered only the first. | Phase 2C-A visual revision (found by axe on the showcase) | The chip uses `textSecondary`. `tests/unit/contrast.test.ts` now asserts the whole matrix — every text token against every supporting surface — and names the exactly two surfaces `textMuted` may not sit on, failing if a palette change moves one across that line. | **Done.** |
| F-33 | **A Playwright project documented as "not run by default" ran on every CI push.** The comment beside the `visual-review` project said `pnpm e2e:screenshots` invokes it; Playwright runs every project in the array unless `--project` is given, so it ran in `pnpm test:e2e` too — regenerating 55 screenshots on a CI runner that discards them when it is torn down. Not a correctness failure: the run on `a57e3de` passed all seven checks in about six minutes. But the comment asserted the opposite of what the code did, and a capture project that writes files into the repository has no business in a behavioural test run. | Phase 2C-A visual revision | The intent is now implemented rather than described: the project is registered only when `BRANDSPACE_VISUAL_REVIEW=1`, which `pnpm e2e:screenshots` sets. `pnpm test:e2e` runs 188 behavioural tests; producing the evidence is a separate, local command. Every job also gained a `timeout-minutes` ceiling — ordinary hygiene, so a future hang fails in bounded time with its report uploaded rather than inheriting the six-hour default. | **Done.** |
| F-34 | **GitHub's check-run and job endpoints reported a COMPLETED job as `in_progress` for over two hours.** Run 17 finished successfully at 02:51:31; the job endpoint was still returning `in_progress`, with frozen step timestamps, long afterwards. Acting on that reading, I diagnosed a hang that had not happened and wrote it up as a defect. | Phase 2C-A visual revision (my own misreading, recorded so the next person does not repeat it) | **The run-level `conclusion` is the authority, not the per-job `status`.** When judging whether CI is stuck, read `GET /actions/runs/{id}` and compare `run_started_at` with `updated_at`; a run whose `updated_at` has advanced past its start has progressed regardless of what the job endpoint claims. The changes F-33 describes stand on their own merits — they were not needed to fix a hang, because there was no hang. | **Recorded.** |
| F-35 | **A page-level `overflow-x: hidden` blinds the overflow suite.** Copied from the approved reference so the ambient orbs could not widen a phone, it made `inlineEndOverhang` return 0 for a deliberately planted 900px element — the meta-assertion that exists to prove the measurement can fail was the only thing that caught it. A page-level clip does not fix overflow, it hides it from the reader and from every automated check at the same time, which is the F-28 shape exactly. The clip belongs on the orbs' own fixed container, and the helper now refuses to treat `html` or `body` as a legitimate clipping ancestor. | Phase 2C-A demo alignment | Removed. `tests/e2e/overflow.ts` hardened, and it immediately reported a real 11px overhang in Arabic that the clip had been hiding (F-36). |
| F-36 | **A metric value set its card's width.** `MetricCard.value` is a formatted string and is not always a number — a plan key and an environment name render there too. Rare in a browser and rare in a test, which is what makes it the worst kind of bug to leave in a control. | Phase 2C-A demo alignment | An explicit choice now always wins over the stored one. Surfaced as a single non-reproducing suite failure and fixed rather than re-run. |
