Six controls across three pages — including all three fields on the customer's workspace settings page — were transparent rectangles on a white card while the test passed. | Phase 2C-A demo alignment | The rule is now the simple one it should always have been: every control carries `.bs-control`, exempting only the input types a browser draws itself. Sixteen hard-coded font sizes were found the same way, and now have a guard of their own. |
| F-39 | **A flex item defaults to `flex-shrink: 1`, and that is enough to obscure a link.** The sidebar foot took three wrong answers before the right one: `margin-block-start: auto` did nothing while the rail took its height from its content; a fixed height with an internal scroll put "Sign out" below the fold of a column nobody thinks to scroll; a sticky foot brought it back and then overlapped the last navigation item. Every symptom had the same cause — a ~840px navigation inside a 760px column was being squeezed while its content overflowed through everything below it. | Phase 2C-A demo alignment | The rail refuses to shrink and the page scrolls, which is what the reference does. axe named it precisely both times ("partially obscured, smallest space is 244.9px by 8.4px", "safe clickable space has a diameter of 10.8px"); the lesson is that a WCAG 2.5.8 target-size failure is usually a layout bug, not a sizing one. |
| F-40 | **Every Control Center screen showed "Overview" as the current page.** `AdminShell` marked the active nav item with `(activePath ?? '') === item.href`, and the console LAYOUT — which renders the shell — never passed `activePath`, so the empty string matched the console root's empty href on all sixteen routes. A Next.js layout cannot read the pathname on the server. | Phase 2C-A fidelity pass | The shell is a client component and resolves the active item from `usePathname()` by longest match, so `/console/workspaces/<id>` belongs to the workspaces item and `/console` cannot swallow it. A caller that sets `item.active` explicitly still wins. The same resolution supplies the top-bar title for routes whose layout never sees their heading. |
| F-41 | **Hiding a control's only text takes its accessible name with it.** Collapsing the rail to 78px hid the workspace switcher's and the sign-out button's labels; each was then a button whose only remaining child was an `aria-hidden` icon. axe reported `button-name` immediately. The related trap is subtler: the reference collapses this copy with `opacity: 0; width: 0`, which leaves it in the accessibility tree — announced but unreachable, the worst of both. | Phase 2C-A fidelity pass | `display: none` rather than a zero-width element, and both controls carry an explicit `aria-label` so the workspace and the exit are still named when their text is not shown. |
| F-42 | **The overflow suite went blind again — from the other side.** Reproducing the demo's shell (`.app-shell { overflow: hidden }`, for its 34px corners) and its scrolling panel (`.main-panel { overflow: auto }`) gave the page two ancestors that FIT the viewport and clip their own content. `inlineEndOverhang` is defined to forgive exactly that, so a planted 900px element measured 0 and the meta-assertion failed. Same shape as F-35: geometry that is correct for the design silently removes the check. | Phase 2C-A complete fidelity pass | The clip stays — it is the demo's, and it is what makes the corners real — and a SECOND measurement was added rather than the first being loosened. `clippedInlineOverflow` reports `scrollWidth - clientWidth` for a named container, and the per-width loop now asserts it is zero for both `.bs-shell` and `main` at every breakpoint in both directions. The self-check plants twice: once inside the panel, to prove the new measurement catches what the old one cannot, and once on `body`, to prove the old one still reports and names an offender. |
| F-43 | **Moving a control into a menu breaks every test that clicked it directly.** Sign-out moved into the profile card's `•••` menu, which is where the demo puts it. Both suites clicked `[data-testid="sign-out"]` with no menu open and timed out. | Phase 2C-A complete fidelity pass | The tests open the menu first. The assertion is unchanged — signing out must end the session — because the contract did not change, only the route to the control; a test rewritten to assert less would have been the wrong fix. |
| F-44 | **A test hook can be the only thing naming who is signed in.** The Control Center's rail carried a standalone `actor-identity` row; replacing it with the demo's profile card removed the element, and "shows who is signed in" failed with the operator's identity plainly visible on screen. | Phase 2C-A complete fidelity pass | `ProfileCard` takes a `nameTestId`, so the hook points at the one element that actually answers the question. The alternative — re-adding hidden markup for the test — would have made the assertion true of something the reader never sees. |
| F-45 | **A translucent stage lets the ambient orbs set the contrast.** With the auth page's card floating directly on the orbs, the "forgot password" link measured 4.12:1 (`#7935FE` on a `#F9D9A5` blend) and axe reported it serious. The demo does not have this problem because its `.auth-stage` is an OPAQUE wash and its footnote sits INSIDE the card — ours had the footnote outside it, on the orbs. | Phase 2C-A complete fidelity pass | Both fixed toward the demo rather than away from it: the stage takes the demo's opaque gradient, the card takes its `rgba(255,255,255,.9)` on top of it, and the footnote moves inside the card where `.auth-card > small` lives. A contrast failure whose fix is also the more faithful reproduction is worth noticing — it usually means the departure caused it. |
| F-46 | **This container's Playwright browser is not where Playwright looks for it.** `pnpm test:e2e` failed 195 tests in 2ms each with "Executable doesn't exist at `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium_headless_shell-1234/...`". Nothing was wrong with the code: the image ships `chromium-1194` and a `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium` symlink, and the config only honours it when `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE` is set. | Phase 2C-A complete fidelity pass | Run with `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`. Recorded because the failure mode — every test failing instantly with an identical error — reads like a catastrophic regression and is not one. |
| F-47 | **A `'use server'` module may export nothing but async functions, and violating it does not fail the build.** `plans/actions.ts` re-exported a synchronous helper. Next.js accepted the build and then could not resolve any action id from that module, so every form on the plans page posted into a void. The symptom was a client error boundary with no server-side stack — the least diagnosable shape a failure can take. | Phase 3 implementation (found by the E2E suite) | The re-export is gone. The residual risk is the PATTERN: nothing yet fails the build when a non-async export is added to an action module. A lint rule, or a structural test of the kind F-25 produced, would close it. |
| F-48 | **`??` does not catch the empty string an untouched text input actually posts.** The change-reason fallback used `String(form.get('reason') ?? 'default')`, so a blank field passed `""` to a Configuration Service that requires eight characters — and an operator who left an optional-looking field alone got "that change was rejected. Check the values and try again." The same operator error appeared twice more: a matrix cell rendered EMPTY when a grant carried `enumValue: ""`. | Phase 3 implementation | A blank-aware `reasonFrom()` helper, and a named `describeGrant()` that cannot return an empty string. The field now states the eight-character requirement rather than springing it. Worth generalising: for form input, `??` is almost never the operator you want — the absent case and the blank case are the same case. |
| F-49 | **Validating a raw configuration document is not the same as validating the parsed one.** Semantic checks ran against the payload as submitted, where a field the author omitted is `undefined` rather than its schema default. "A boolean feature takes no limit" therefore fired on every plan that had never mentioned a limit at all. | Phase 3 implementation (found by a unit test) | `validateConfiguration` now parses first and runs the semantic stage against the parsed document, which removes the whole class rather than the one instance. |
| F-50 | **A CHECK constraint caught an ordering bug that no amount of reading would have.** Settlement decremented a bucket's remaining balance before releasing its hold, so a bucket whose entire remainder was both held and spent transiently violated `reserved <= remaining` and PostgreSQL refused the write. | Phase 3 implementation (found by the credit-protocol suite) | Both movements are now one update, which is what they always logically were. Recorded because the instinct on seeing a constraint violation is to question the constraint: here the constraint was right and the code was wrong, and relaxing it would have allowed a bucket to be over-drawn under exactly the interleaving it exists to prevent. |
| F-51 | **A `data-testid` prefix selector catches the form as well as the table.** `[data-testid^="grant-"]` matched the matrix cells AND the editor's own `grant-plan`, `grant-limit` and `grant-enabled` controls, so an assertion about rendered cells was measuring an empty number input. | Phase 3 implementation | Scoped to `[data-testid^="matrix-"] [data-testid^="grant-"]`. A prefix that reads naturally in one place is a namespace collision in another; scope the selector rather than renaming every hook. |
| F-52 | **A `.env` reconstructed field-by-field is a different environment.** Building a fresh-database env by deriving `DATABASE_URL` from `DATABASE_MIGRATION_URL` ran the whole isolation suite as the table OWNER rather than the application role, producing 78 failures that looked like a Phase 3 regression and were not. | Phase 3 verification (my own error, recorded so the next person does not repeat it) | Derive the fresh env by substituting only the DATABASE NAME in the real one (`sed 's#/brandspace_test#/brandspace_fresh#g'`), never by reassembling it from parts. The suite then passed 469/469 on a migrations-only, unseeded database — which is what that verification was supposed to measure. |
| F-53 | **An unbounded admin listing plus test suites that never clean up degrades until it times out.** The secrets E2E test began failing at its ten-second ceiling. The action was working — the secret was written every time — but `listSecrets` has no `take`, and the local test database had accumulated **853 secret records** across repeated local runs (each isolation run adds ~36 probe secrets; each E2E run adds one). Rendering and axe-scanning that table is what exceeded the timeout; the query itself was 3.8ms. I first recorded this as a flake and re-ran it, which was wrong: it failed a second time, and a second failure is not a flake. | Phase 3 verification (found by root-causing rather than re-running) | Clearing the residue restored the suite to 72/72 in that project. **Two real issues remain and are NOT fixed here, deliberately.** (1) `SecretService.listSecrets` is unbounded — a genuine defect that will reach production, but the correct fix is pagination with a "showing N of M" affordance, and that is a redesign of a Phase 2A page this phase was told not to undertake. Adding a silent cap would be worse than the current behaviour: an operator would stop seeing secrets with no indication. (2) Neither the isolation nor the E2E suite cleans up what it creates, so any long-lived database drifts toward this. CI is unaffected — it builds a fresh database per run — which is exactly why it went unnoticed. **Recommended as the first item of Phase 4 housekeeping.** **CLOSED in the pre-Phase-4 hardening pass.** Both halves are fixed and proven. (1) `listSecrets` returns one PAGE — two bounded queries, a total order with an `id` tie-breaker, server-side search and filtering, URL-held state, an out-of-range page that recovers to the last page, and `Showing N–M of Total` in both languages. The page-size cap bounds one REQUEST, not what an operator may see: `total` always reports the true count and every record stays reachable, which is the distinction that made a silent cap unacceptable. Contract in `docs/ADMIN-CONTROL-CENTER.md` §7.1. (2) Every suite that writes secrets now carries a per-run token and removes exactly its own rows; strategy in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §3.9. Proven by two consecutive full Playwright runs and two consecutive isolation runs leaving the `secret_record` count unchanged at 46, where each run previously added rows. No product-facing secret deletion path was added and no privilege was granted — cleanup uses the `DELETE` the platform role already held. |
| F-54 | **A fixture that fabricates an impossible state produces a failure in the code that is right.** The isolation fixtures created a `CreditReservation` claiming an allocation of 1000 against a `CreditGrant` whose `reservedMilliCredits` was 0 — a state the product cannot produce, because reserving always increments the bucket. The sweeper then could not release it: the decrement would take `reserved` below zero and the CHECK constraint refused, aborting the whole batch. Surfaced only because rows accumulate across runs and eventually pass their deadline, so the suite passed for a while and then did not. | Phase 3 verification (found by the full isolation run) | The fixture now records the hold. Separately, `sweepAbandonedReservations` no longer lets one unreleasable row stop the sweep: it reports the bad ids and continues, because reservation leaks are a metric that must stay at zero and a sweeper that dies on the first bad row stops releasing every valid reservation behind it. The constraint was NOT relaxed — it was correct, and relaxing it would have allowed a bucket to be over-drawn under exactly the interleaving it exists to prevent. |
| F-55 | **Catching a unique violation inside a PostgreSQL transaction does not work.** A failed statement ABORTS the surrounding transaction, so a `try/catch` around a racing `INSERT` leaves every later statement failing with "current transaction is aborted" — the catch runs and achieves nothing. | Phase 3 adversarial review | Wallet creation on first credit movement uses `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, which does not abort. Asserted by five concurrent first movements against a workspace with no wallet. |
| F-56 | **Prisma reports WHICH constraint a `P2002` violated in a different place depending on the driver.** The idempotency recovery matched `meta.target`, which the pg adapter does not populate — it nests the constraint under `meta.driverAdapterError.cause.constraint.index`. So the recovery never fired, and a raced retry surfaced a 500 for work that had actually succeeded. | Phase 3 adversarial review (found by writing the test for the fix) | Both shapes are checked, structurally and never on a message. Worth generalising: a `catch` written against an error shape nobody has printed is a `catch` that has not been tested. Twenty concurrent calls with one idempotency key now assert that none is rejected, not merely that the counter reads 1 — the weaker assertion passed throughout. |
| F-57 | **A chevron named for a logical direction still pointed at a physical one.** `ChevronStartIcon` and `ChevronEndIcon` are drawn as left- and right-pointing paths, and three components — the pagination, the sidebar collapse toggle, the calendar's month stepper — each carried a comment saying the glyph "flips with direction rather than pointing the wrong way in Arabic". Nothing flipped it. An Arabic reader saw "previous" pointing forwards. | Pre-Phase-4 hardening (found by looking at the rendered Arabic page, not at the code) | The two logical chevrons now carry `.bs-chevron-logical`, mirrored under `html[dir='rtl']` in `tokens.css`; only those two opt in, so an arrow that means something physical still points where it points. Asserted three ways in `tests/unit/design-system.test.ts` (both icons opt in, the rule exists, nothing else opts in) and once in the browser against the COMPUTED transform — a class with no rule behind it passes a source check and still renders backwards. Recorded because the comment was the defect's best camouflage: it had been read several times as evidence the behaviour was correct. |
| F-58 | **Replacing a per-test unique token with a per-RUN one collides with itself.** Wiring cleanup into `platform-services.test.ts` replaced nine `provider: \`p${Date.now()}\`` values with one shared token, and four tests that had been creating distinct refs started creating the same one — "a secret with ref … already exists. Rotate it instead." | Pre-Phase-4 hardening (found by running the whole isolation suite rather than the new files) | The token is a suffix on the original per-test letter, not a replacement for it: `${TEST_SECRET_PROVIDER}-p`, `-e`, `-r` and so on. Cleanup matches on the token as a substring, so ownership is unaffected. The lesson is narrower than it looks: a fixture identifier usually encodes two things — which RUN owns it and which TEST made it — and a change that only preserves the first breaks the second silently until the ref happens to be reused. |
| F-59 | **Pre-fix residue is not cleaned by the fix.** The local test database still holds 44 secret records created before this pass, plus the 2 the seed legitimately creates for MFA. Cleanup deletes only rows carrying a run token, and refuses everything else by design, so it cannot and must not remove them. | Pre-Phase-4 hardening | Left in place deliberately, and harmless now that the listing is paginated — they are what the "no accumulation" measurements are taken against. A developer who wants a clean local database should reset it (`pnpm db:reset`), which is an operator action, not something a test helper may do on its own initiative. CI is unaffected: it builds a fresh database per run. |
| F-60 | **The secrets page was not the only unbounded or silently-capped listing — it was the one that got large enough to fail.** Confirmed while fixing F-53, and left unfixed here deliberately, since this pass was scoped to F-53 alone. `WorkspaceService.list`takes 200 rows with no total and no navigation, which is precisely the *silent display cap* F-53 rejected: an operator with 201 customers simply stops seeing one, with nothing on screen to say so.`InvitationService`caps at 200 the same way.`ConfigurationService.#workspaceUsage`loads every workspace with a plan, unbounded, and grows with the customer base. The audit log's`take: 100`is honest by comparison — the page says "the 100 most recent events" — but still offers no way to reach an older one. | Pre-Phase-4 hardening (found by auditing every`findMany`while fixing F-53) | **Not fixed here.** The pagination contract in`docs/ADMIN-CONTROL-CENTER.md`§7.1 is deliberately written to be reusable, and`Pagination`in`packages/ui`is now correct in both directions, so applying it to the workspaces, invitations and audit listings is mechanical rather than a redesign. Sized as a small housekeeping item, to be scheduled by the owner rather than folded into Phase 4's scope. The risk is real but slow: unlike secrets, none of these grows from test runs. |
| F-61 | **Secrets were the accumulating rows that hurt; they are not the only accumulating rows.** Measured on a fresh migrations-only database: two consecutive full isolation runs leave **0 secrets** (the F-53 fix) and **20 credit reservations per run**, plus `platform_user`, `workspace`, `audit_event`and`configuration_version`rows. The reservations come from`createIsolationFixtures`, which gives each fixture workspace an OPEN reservation expiring in one hour; an hour later every one of them is an abandoned reservation, which is the supply line feeding F-62. | Pre-Phase-4 hardening (measured, not estimated) | Out of scope for a pass told to fix F-53 and its test-data problem: cleaning the fixture's reservations means a teardown in every suite that calls `createIsolationFixtures`, not one hook. `credit-protocol.test.ts` — the suite whose own residue was breaking it — does now delete the reservations for the workspaces it creates. For the rest, the token-and-`afterAll`pattern in`tests/support/secret-fixtures.ts` generalises directly; the guard that refuses a malformed token is the part to copy, not the part to skip. |
| F-62 | **`sweepAbandonedReservations` can be starved indefinitely by rows it cannot release.** It reads a bounded window (`take: 200`) of OPEN reservations past their deadline, with **no ordering**, and a row whose bucket does not record the hold it claims can never be released — the decrement would take `reserved`below zero and a CHECK constraint refuses it, correctly. Those rows are reported in`failed`and left OPEN, so they come back in the next window, and the next. Once as many as`limit`of them exist, the sweep releases **nothing**, for ever: every valid reservation behind them leaks, and`docs/BILLING-AND-CREDITS.md`§10.3 makes reservation leaks a metric that must stay at zero. Observed directly — the local test database holds 259 abandoned reservations of which the first 200 are all unreleasable, and one sweep pass swept 0. The supply is F-61: every fixture workspace gets an OPEN reservation expiring in an hour, so each run adds twenty rows that become abandoned by themselves. The absent`orderBy`also makes which rows a sweep touches arbitrary. | Pre-Phase-4 hardening (found by root-causing an intermittent sweeper test failure rather than re-running it — the failure was a real signal about the sweeper, not about the test) | **FIXED in the post-Phase-3 audit remediation — see A-7.** It was correctly out of scope for the F-53 pass, which is why it was recorded rather than carried silently. The suite's own contribution IS fixed:`credit-protocol.test.ts`now deletes the reservations for the workspaces it created, so it stops adding to the pile. Proposed fix, for the owner to schedule: bound the sweep by reservations RELEASED rather than rows EXAMINED — page past a window of failures instead of stopping inside it — and give the query a deterministic`orderBy: { expiresAt: 'asc' }`so a leak is cleared oldest-first. Recorded, not silently carried: unlike F-53's residue, this one can reach production, where the unreleasable rows come from real interleavings rather than a test fixture. |
| F-63 | **The gateway does not persist model output by default, so an idempotent replay can return nothing.** docs/AI-GATEWAY.md §7.4 guarantee 2 says a duplicate key returns the ORIGINAL result; §11 says prompts and responses are not persisted by default. Both cannot hold for a client that lost its response unless the result was kept somewhere. | Phase 4 (W6) | Resolved as an opt-in: `ai.routing`gains`parameters.persistOutput`, default **false**, writing to a nullable `ai_request.outputPayload`. A caller that stores its own artifact leaves it off. **RESOLVED 2026-09-13 by D-78**, which approved the default-off rule and required a defined retention and deletion policy for anything persisted. `outputRetentionDays`is now mandatory alongside`persistOutput`(validation refuses them apart) and`purgeExpiredOutputs()`clears expired payloads while retaining the usage, cost, credit, idempotency and audit metadata the policy requires be kept. **Remaining, and scheduled:** the customer-facing *notice* §11 describes is a dashboard surface and belongs with the Phase 5 feature that first turns persistence on. |
| F-64 | **Provider cost was typed as an integer count of MINOR units and would have recorded zero for every text request.** A model at $0.15 per million input tokens costs 0.015 of a cent per thousand tokens. In whole cents that is 0, so`providerCostMinor`would have been 0 on essentially every request and the margin reporting those columns feed would have shown infinite margin on every row — the one number the D-15 margin floor exists to catch. | Phase 4 (W5), found while writing the pricing tests | Fixed forward in migration`20260913090000`: both columns renamed to `providerCostMicroMinor`and widened to BIGINT, existing values multiplied so nothing changed worth. docs/AI-GATEWAY.md §4 and §7.2 corrected. The field name in the original document was the source of the mistake. |
| F-65 | **A stuck AI request whose reservation row was already gone could never leave`RUNNING`.** The release threw, the failure was never recorded, and the sweeper handed the same unfixable row back on every pass — the F-62 starvation shape, in which the reservation-leak metric of §12 would never return to zero. | Phase 4 (W7), found by the sweeper's own tests | A genuine `NOT_FOUND`from the release is tolerated and the request still reaches a terminal status; every other error still propagates. Nothing in production deletes a reservation row, so this is a belt-and-braces path, but an unfixable row in a bounded sweep is not a hypothetical cost. |
| F-66 | **Platform-wide provider spend caps (docs/AI-GATEWAY.md §8, last row) are not enforced.** Per-request, per-workspace and per-plan ceilings are. | Phase 4 (W8) | Deliberately deferred. Enforcing a platform-wide cap needs a rollup table, not a cross-tenant aggregate scan on the path of every request, and there is no real provider to spend against until D-13 is answered. **Scheduled** for the phase that adds the first real adapter. |
| F-67 | **Mid-trial plan changes grant no additional credits.** Carried forward from the post-Phase-3 audit at the owner's instruction. | Post-Phase-3 audit; owner decision recorded 2026-09-09 | **KEEP the current behaviour.** Phase 4 exposed no correctness problem with it: the gateway reserves and settles against whatever the wallet holds, and never assumes a grant accompanied a plan change. Recorded as an **owner-review item, not a Phase 4 blocker**. |
| F-84 | **Two replay lookups match on`workspaceId + idempotencyKey`alone, outside Phase 7's surface:`AssetUploadService` (`packages/assets/src/upload.ts`) and Brand Brain document ingestion (`packages/brand-brain/src/ingestion.ts`).** The key is client-supplied at both, so a member who guesses or observes another member's key is handed that member's row. | Phase 7 remediation, found while sweeping for the P7-R2 defect class | The same defect the review named in three Phase 7 services. The five reachable from the Copilot's own tools — the Copilot's plans, analytics explanations, strategies, Content Studio drafts and Brand Brain chat — are FIXED in that change and bound to the actor, the brand and the live scope. These two are Phase 5 upload paths the assistant cannot reach, and fixing them inside a Phase 7 remediation would widen it into a Phase 5 audit with its own verification surface. **Recorded rather than done, deliberately**, and small: the binding is `createdByUserId`plus`brandIdQueryFilter`, exactly as the five carry. |
| ~~F-85~~ **RESOLVED** | **Two P7-R6 regression tests read a scheduling ceiling they never created, so they passed on a developer database and failed on CI's fresh one.** The fixture workspace carries NO PLAN, so `limit.scheduled_posts`resolves through D-10 precedence to "feature disabled", which`EntitlementService.limit`reports as ZERO — the correct fail-closed answer. Every`consume()`was therefore refused before it could write to the ledger, and the assertion that the real quota COUNTS failed for a reason unrelated to the defect it guards. On a database some earlier run had left with a plan assigned, the same tests passed. | Phase 7 remediation, found by CI run #78 against a fresh database | **Done.** The same class as F-23, in a new suite: the tests now BUILD the ceiling they read, through the platform-override path D-10 already resolves and that the neighbouring ceiling test already used, and they count headroom FROM WHAT IS ALREADY USED so the three tests sharing one monthly window cannot depend on the order they run in. No production code changed and no assertion was relaxed — both tests gained an assertion that`consume()`actually SUCCEEDED, which is what made the silent refusal visible in the first place. Verified by dropping and recreating the local test database and running the whole suite against it, not by trusting one that had been seeded at some point. | **Done** |
| ~~F-86~~ **RESOLVED** | **An automation's proposed external action could not be confirmed by anybody, and no screen offered to.**`AutomationEngine`mints a single-use token for an`AWAITING_CONFIRMATION`run, stores only its digest, and returns the raw value to its caller — the WORKER, which logs a status and drops it. The notification that tells a permitted person to come and look carries no payload, deliberately. So`PROPOSE_PUBLISH`was authorable, storable and listable, and the credential to confirm it existed nowhere;`confirmAutomationRunAction`read a`token`form field nothing ever filled, under a comment claiming it arrived from the notification, and no component called that action at all. | Phase 7 remediation round 2, found sweeping for R2-B's defect class | **Done.** D-175: the credential is minted when an authorized person asks, after the same permission and BrandScope checks`confirmRun` applies, rotating the digest under a compare-and-swap so asking twice leaves one live token. The dashboard obtains it server-side and spends it in the same action, so it never reaches a browser, and the runs list now renders a confirm control for a run that is awaiting one. | **Done** |
| A-1 | **A password reset reactivated any account that held a token.**`completePasswordReset`wrote`status: 'ACTIVE'`unconditionally, so a SUSPENDED user — or a DELETED one holding a token minted before the deletion — could resurrect their own account from a link in their inbox. It was also four separate statements: anything failing after consumption left the token spent and the password unchanged, and an invalid password was rejected AFTER the token was burned. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit | Only PENDING advances; SUSPENDED and DELETED are refused with the same message as an unknown token, and no token is minted for them. The whole reset is one transaction, and the password is hashed before anything is consumed. Six of nine new assertions fail against the previous implementation. **Fixed.** |
| A-2 | **A genuinely new invitee could not get in at all.**`accept`requires an authenticated identity and there is no sign-up route, so an invited address with no account reached the page, was told to sign in, and had nothing to sign in with. The journey only worked if somebody created the`User`row by hand — which the E2E seed did, with a comment saying the invitee "needs an account to accept with". That pre-creation is what hid the dead end for two phases. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit |`acceptAsNewUser`: the invitee sets a password, the identity is created from the address the INVITATION names, and the membership is granted in one transaction. The token is the proof of address control — it was delivered to that mailbox and the database holds only its hash. Uniform failures, token hashing, tenant isolation and single-use acceptance are all preserved, and both entry points now share one implementation of the conditional UPDATE so the guarantee cannot drift. The E2E test drives the journey for an address with only an invitation seeded. **Fixed.** |
| A-3 | **Assigning a plan wrote a string.** `assignPlan`set`workspace.planKey`and an audit event and stopped. No`WorkspaceSubscription`, so no status, no billing period, no trial, no pinned price and none of the credits the plan promises. The Plan & Usage page already read the subscription and there was never one to read; the cycle worker had nothing to advance. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit | Everything the assignment implies now happens in one transaction: plan key, subscription, price and configuration version pinned from the catalogue (AC-04.7), the trial when the plan offers one and the workspace has never had one, and the matching credit grant. Idempotency is derived from the subscription's PERIOD, never the clock — the first attempt keyed it on a freshly computed period end, which differs on every call. Changing plan mid-trial grants nothing, or an operator could mint credits by toggling. Unassigning CANCELS rather than deletes: the row is the evidence of what was sold. **Fixed.** |
| A-4 | **Three pieces of entitlement configuration looked live and were inert.** (1) `enabledForPlans`was declared on`FlagRule`, carried through the schema and the Control Center, and read by NOTHING — an operator could enable a feature for a plan, see it saved, and change nobody's experience. (2) `valueType: 'enum'`existed and no enum could ever resolve: the decision carried`enabled`and`limitValue`only, so an enum feature resolved to`defaultValue === true`, false for any string. (3) `dependsOn`was consulted only when an override was WRITTEN, so a dependency turned off afterwards left the dependent resolving to enabled for ever. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit |`enabledForPlans`resolves as an allow list (a non-match falls through rather than deciding against, or targeting one plan would be an outage for the rest). The decision carries`enumValue`, plans and overrides supply one, and `enumOptions`makes it validatable; overrides gained a nullable column. The dependency graph is walked on EVERY resolve, an override included, with cycles terminating closed. Twelve of sixteen new assertions fail against the previous implementation. **Fixed.** |
| A-5 | **Two owners could be removed at once, leaving a workspace with none.** The last-owner invariant is a COUNT, and a count takes no locks. Two concurrent demotions write DIFFERENT membership rows so nothing conflicts, and under READ COMMITTED each still sees the other owner as active. Both counted one, both passed, both committed. Textbook write skew, invisible to every sequential test. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit | The rows being written are not the rows being read, so row locks and stricter counts do not help.`changeRole`and`remove`take`FOR UPDATE`on the WORKSPACE row first — a mutex over the set — always in that order, so two queue rather than interleave and cannot deadlock. Per workspace, with a test asserting two unrelated workspaces still demote concurrently. **Fixed.** |
| A-6 | **The cycle boundary was four transactions and could double-charge on retry.** Expiry, forfeiture, the grant and the timestamps each committed separately; a crash between any two left a workspace half-reset. Retrying was worse than doing nothing: the grant was keyed on the cycle and no-opped, but the forfeiture was keyed per BUCKET, so a second pass ran FIFO against the survivors and forfeited again. Separately, the rollover cap was computed against a balance INCLUDING reserved credits and taken from`remaining − reserved`; `allocateFifo`returned a shortfall nobody read, so the customer silently kept credits above the cap. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit | One transaction behind a single completion marker keyed on the cycle. The shortfall is clamped and reported as`unforfeitable`, because credits held against an in-flight request cannot be seized without breaking it — the honest outcome is to take what can be taken and say how much could not. **Fixed.** |
| A-7 | **F-62, closed.** See F-62 for the defect. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit | Deterministic `[expiresAt asc, id asc]`ordering;`limit`bounds RELEASES rather than rows examined, with failures excluded and the next batch fetched; and the result reports`attempted`and`exhausted`— the difference between "no leak left" and "hit the bound, more to do", which a bare count cannot express. An explicit`maxAttempts`is honoured verbatim; the default gets a floor of one batch, because`limit * 4`for a small limit gives up before looking at anything. **Fixed.** |
| A-8 | **A retried settle or release surfaced a 500 for work that had succeeded.** Both read the reservation status through an UNLOCKED findUnique, so two concurrent calls both saw OPEN and both proceeded. They serialised on the wallet lock, by which point the loser was committed to writing: it decremented every hold a second time and reused the winner's idempotency key, hitting a CHECK violation or a P2002. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit | The constraints were right — they are what stopped the double charge. What was wrong is that a retry is a normal event and must be a no-op. The reservation row is now locked before its status is read, so the loser waits, sees a terminal status and returns. Eight concurrent settles, eight concurrent releases, and settle racing release each resolve to exactly one outcome with no rejections. **Fixed.** |
| A-9 | **An idempotency key named any request wearing it, not one request.**`grant`returned the stored bucket for ANY caller — so workspace B replaying A's key was handed A's bucket and told the grant had happened.`consume`returned the CALLER's consumption and skipped the increment, so a key reused across workspaces meant B's usage was silently never recorded: free quota, invisible in the counters precisely because the branch writes nothing.`refund`interpolated`amount`straight into`usedValue - ${amount}`, so a NEGATIVE refund incremented the counter, and had no idempotency key at all. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit | The immutable request fields — workspace, type or feature, and amount — now decide whether a replay is the same request; a mismatch is a CONFLICT. Refunds require a positive whole number and their own key, and are recorded as a negative `UsageEvent`. Every one of the eight new assertions fails against the previous implementation. **Fixed.** |
| A-10 | **Security-material changes were not atomic with their audit events, and support mode could overlap.** Only invitation ACCEPTANCE was audited: the customer's Activity Log showed people arriving with no record of who invited them, and a revocation left no trace. Support mode's `start`was three statements under a comment asserting one live session per operator per workspace, which nothing enforced — two concurrent starts produced two live grants whose accesses cannot be attributed to either. A failure after the INSERT left a LIVE SUPPORT GRANT WITH NO AUDIT EVENT. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit | Created, resent and revoked each write an event inside the same transaction as the change; a resend names the invitation it superseded, and no event carries the token. Support mode is one transaction, takes the workspace row`FOR UPDATE`, and is backed by a partial unique index on `(workspaceId, platformUserId) WHERE endedAt IS NULL`— the guarantee that holds if a future caller forgets the lock. Atomicity is proven by an injected failure: a trigger refusing the audit insert, installed as the migrator because the platform role correctly has no DDL rights. **Fixed.** |
| A-11 | **The workspace directory was the silent cap F-53 rejected, surviving.**`WorkspaceAdminService.list`took 200 rows with no total and no navigation. Three smaller caps (invitations at 200, support history at 50, the audit log at 100) stated nothing, or stated a claim rather than a number. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit | The directory is paginated on the F-53 contract. The other three now show "the most recent N of Total", which is the difference between a bounded read and a hidden one. Full pagination of the AUDIT LOG is **scheduled, not done**: it is append-only and platform-wide, so paging it usefully needs filters (actor, action, workspace, date range) that are their own piece of work. Invitations and support history are **scheduled** on the same basis, and are bounded per workspace rather than platform-wide, so the cap is not reachable in practice the way the directory's was. |
| A-12 | **The isolation fixtures manufactured fake credit leaks.**`createIsolationFixtures`gave every fixture workspace a reservation expiring in one hour, so each became an abandoned reservation sixty minutes later — twenty per full isolation run, accumulating for ever. That was the supply line behind F-62. | Independent post-Phase-3 audit remediation (measured: 0 secrets and 20 reservations per run on a fresh database) | The deadline is a century out, so the row stays for the isolation tests and leaves the abandoned set entirely. A fixture that puts noise into the one metric docs/BILLING-AND-CREDITS.md §10.3 requires to stay at zero is wrong even once the sweeper survives it. **Remaining, and scheduled:** the fixtures still leave`platform_user`, `workspace`, `audit_event`and`configuration_version` rows behind, which cost disk rather than correctness — nothing reads those under a bound. The token-and-`afterAll`pattern in`tests/support/secret-fixtures.ts` generalises directly when it becomes worth doing. |

| **F-77**              | **No storage vendor is approved, so production cannot accept an upload.** The `ObjectStore` boundary is provider-agnostic and the filesystem implementation behind it is real, durable and cross-process, which is enough for development, test and CI. It is not enough for production: a container disk does not survive a deployment. D-105 makes this fail closed rather than silently lossy.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Phase 5B-1 (`packages/storage`)                   | **Owner decision.** Pick an object-storage provider and a data region consistent with D-03, then write the adapter behind the existing interface. No code outside `packages/storage` should need to change.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Before any production release that exposes the Asset Library |
| **F-78**              | **No production virus scanner is selected.** Quarantine, the CLEAN gate, the failure path and the audit trail are all real and tested against `MockVirusScanner`; the thing that decides CLEAN is not. Until one is configured, production refuses uploads (D-106) — so this and F-77 gate the same release.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Phase 5B-1 (`packages/assets/src/scanning.ts`)    | **Owner decision.** Choose between a hosted scanning API and a self-managed engine, and record the licence, the update channel and where the bytes go. A hosted API means customer files leave the platform, which is a privacy decision, not only a technical one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Same release as F-77                                         |

Every candidate decodes untrusted bytes in native code.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Phase 5B-1 (`packages/assets/src/derivatives.ts`) | Review one encoder properly — maintenance, licence, CVE history, offline installation, and whether it can be sandboxed or resource-capped. Then add the kind to `ENCODABLE_KINDS` and let the existing worker stage do the rest.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | When thumbnails are needed by a consuming module             |
| ~~F-80~~ **RESOLVED** | **Phase 5A left three PLAIN foreign keys that act as cross-tenant existence oracles.** `brand_source_chunk.sourceDocumentId`, `brand_ingestion_job.sourceDocumentId` and `brand_brain_message.conversationId` reference a parent by id alone. PostgreSQL evaluates referential integrity with RLS BYPASSED, so a write referencing another workspace's row SUCCEEDS where it should be rejected, and the difference between success and a constraint error tells the caller whether that id exists. Probed directly against the test database: all three confirmed. The Asset Library avoids it entirely (D-99).                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Phase 5B-1, while designing D-99                  | **Done** (F-80 corrective pass). Migration `20260914200000_f80_brand_brain_composite_foreign_keys` replaces all three with `(workspaceId, sourceDocumentId)` and `(workspaceId, conversationId)` against new `(workspaceId, id)` uniques on `brand_source_document` and `brand_brain_conversation`; `ON DELETE CASCADE` is preserved and no row is written, rewritten or deleted. A pre-flight anti-join refuses the migration — naming tables and counts, never ids — rather than repairing, because a cross-tenant row is an incident and not a cleanup task (D-113 explains why the pre-flight has to lift FORCE RLS to see anything at all, and what would otherwise have shipped: a constraint marked valid over data that violates it). **Evidence:** `tests/isolation/f80-brand-brain-composite-keys.test.ts` (21 tests) proves the three refusals from inside the caller's OWN workspace, that a real foreign id and a fabricated one fail with an IDENTICAL SQLSTATE, constraint and message — so the oracle is closed rather than moved — and that the relationships and their cascades still work. `tests/isolation/f80-migration-upgrade.test.ts` (9 tests) builds a database at main, DEMONSTRATES the defect by posting into another tenant's conversation successfully, shows the migration refusing while that row exists and changing nothing, then upgrading cleanly with every valid row preserved by id — and proves a migrations-only database has zero drift. | **Done**                                                     |
| **F-81**              | **SVG is refused (D-108).** Customers do upload logos as SVG, and telling them the format is unsupported is a real product gap, not only a security stance.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Phase 5B-1 (`packages/assets/src/file-safety.ts`) | If SVG is wanted, it needs a sanitiser with the same dependency review F-79 describes, plus serving from an isolated origin or with a restrictive CSP. Do not add the type without both.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Owner decision                                               |
| **F-82**              | **Asset Library artwork is a design-system EXTENSION, not a port.** The approved demo routes `media` to a placeholder, so under D-98 the screen was built from the platform's own tokens, components and interaction patterns and recorded in `docs/UI-FIDELITY-CONTRACT.md` §6.3.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Phase 5B-1 (`apps/dashboard`)                     | The owner may refine it during the F-76 UI parity pass. The extension is a starting point, not a claim that the design is settled.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | F-76 parity pass                                             |
| ~~F-83~~ **RESOLVED** | **The rest of Brand Brain carried FIVE more plain foreign keys of exactly the F-80 shape** — not the two first recorded here. The original entry was written after querying four tables; querying the whole module found `brand_knowledge_candidate.sourceDocumentId`, `brand_knowledge_candidate.targetItemId`, `brand_knowledge_item.sourceDocumentId`, `brand_knowledge_item.conflictsWithItemId` and `brand_knowledge_version.knowledgeItemId`. Two reach further than any F-80 key: `targetItemId` crosses the D-65 GOVERNANCE boundary (it names the approved item a candidate would EDIT, so a foreign id pointed the review diff at another tenant's canonical knowledge and an acceptance would have written against it), and `knowledgeItemId` attaches APPEND-ONLY history, which nothing can correct afterwards. | F-80 corrective pass                              | **Done** (same change as F-80, migration `20260914200000_f80_brand_brain_composite_foreign_keys`). All five are composite on `workspaceId` against `brand_source_document(workspaceId, id)` and the new `brand_knowledge_item(workspaceId, id)`. **Referential actions preserved exactly**, which for the three `SET NULL` keys required naming the column: a bare composite `ON DELETE SET NULL` nulls EVERY referencing column including `workspaceId`, which is `NOT NULL`, so the parent delete would have failed outright — measured, not assumed (D-114). **Evidence:** the catalogue test now asserts the whole module has NO single-column key to a tenant-owned parent, plus each key's preserved `ON DELETE` and each `SET NULL` key's `confdelsetcols`; the upgrade suite demonstrates the governance defect at main (A's candidate targets B's approved item and SUCCEEDS), proves both `SET NULL` deletes keep `workspaceId`, and proves an item with history still cannot be deleted because append-only wins.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | **Done**                                                     |

### Current execution Phase 4 additions (identity, authentication and security)

| ID       | Item                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Raised in                                     | Recommendation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | When                              |
| -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------- |
| **F-87** | **THE PER-SOURCE DEFAULTS ASSUME ROUGHLY ONE PERSON PER ADDRESS, AND SHARED EGRESS BREAKS THAT ASSUMPTION.** D-250's shipped defaults allow 30 sign-ins per source per five minutes. Behind a corporate NAT, a carrier-grade NAT or a single office egress, a few dozen colleagues arriving at nine o'clock are ONE source — and the thirty-first of them is refused a sign-in for a reason none of them can see or act on. This is not hypothetical: the first full end-to-end run after the limiter landed failed 76 tests on "Too many attempts", because the whole browser suite is one address behaving entirely legitimately. The per-ACCOUNT ceilings are unaffected and are where brute-force protection actually lives; only the per-source dimension has this property. | Current execution Phase 4, first full E2E run | **An owner decision, not a correctness one, so Phase 4 does not take it.** The numbers are versioned configuration, the schema already bounds them (per-source sign-in at most 500 per window), and the right values depend on deployment topology nobody has chosen yet — whether a CDN or proxy fronts the apps, and therefore what `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` resolves an address to at all. Decide the ceilings together with that topology, before the first real customer traffic. Until then the defaults are deliberately conservative, which fails toward refusing rather than toward not counting. | Before production launch          |
| **F-88** | **A CLOSED SET DECLARED IN ONE PACKAGE AND RE-TYPED IN ANOTHER WILL DRIFT, AND DID.** The API's internal delivery route carried its own hand-written `z.enum` of email templates, with a comment saying it was "mirrored here on purpose". Phase 4 added a seventh template and the mirror was not updated, so the dashboard sent `auth.password_reset.unknown` and the API refused it — meaning the branch that exists to make a reset request answer identically for a registered and an unregistered address was the only one that could not send. Fixed by deriving both the union and the route's schema from one runtime list.                                                                                                                                              | Current execution Phase 4, first full E2E run | **Done** (`EMAIL_TEMPLATE_KEYS`). Recorded because the PATTERN is the risk, not the single instance: any closed set that a second module re-declares by hand has the same shape. Worth a sweep for others — locale codes, audit actions, job names — each of which is a runtime list somewhere and a literal union somewhere else.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Sweep: with the F-21 / F-22 sweep |

### Owner Control Center Simple + Advanced Mode additions

| ID       | Item                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **F-89** | **The background job queue is not measured from the Control Center.** Nothing reads BullMQ counts (the API readiness probe only checks that `REDIS_URL` is set). Simple System says "Not measured here" rather than drawing a light. A queue-depth reader belongs with the metrics backend D-217 leaves open.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| **F-90** | **Revenue, MRR, profit and AI provider cost have no source of truth.** Usage & Billing names them as not shown. Provider cost exists per request in micro-minor units but in each model's own `costCurrency`, and summing across currencies would be a conversion D-08 forbids.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **F-91** | **A real AI provider's first activation needs gate fields the Hub cannot set.** `ai.providers` validation refuses `status: active` without `noTrainingGuarantee`, a reviewed `dataRetentionPolicy` and `privacyReviewRef`, and `applyProviderRecord` creates new AI records with `noTrainingGuarantee: false` and `unverified` retention. Today only the development double exists, so nothing is blocked, but connecting a real provider on Staging will need these set in Advanced › Configuration, or a Hub form for them, before Switch on succeeds.                                                                                                                                                         |
| **F-92** | **The Integrations Hub list fails for a role with configuration read but not secret read** (`operations_viewer`), because `IntegrationsService.list` asks the Secret Service for credential presence. This is pre-existing in Advanced. Simple mode checks the permission first and says the states cannot be shown. The fix is for the service to report presence as withheld rather than throw.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **F-93** | **Configuration validation messages are English only.** Simple Plans shows them verbatim in the "Problems to fix" banner, including in Arabic. Localising them means stable issue codes from `packages/config`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **F-94** | **"Selected plans" across two domains is not atomic** (D-314). Grants are activated first and then the flag's global setting is cleared. If the second is refused, the screen shows the true state (the global setting still decides) and the owner can repeat the change.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **F-95** | **Changing a customer's plan pins the price in SAR** (`assignPlanAction` passes no currency, so `DEFAULT_PIN_CURRENCY` applies). A plan with no SAR price cannot be assigned from either mode. This is pre-existing. The customer's own currency should be passed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **F-96** | **Two pre-existing sharp edges found in the audit, unchanged here.** (1) `toggleKillSwitchAction` writes into whatever `feature-flags` draft is open and then activates it, so other pending flag edits go live with the switch. (2) `saveIntegrationConfigurationAction` checks only `configuration.manage` while the service it calls also activates (needs `.activate`) and writes secrets (needs `secret.manage`); today only roles holding all three can reach it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **F-97** | **Staging validation of this pass is blocked on Railway access, not on the code.** Staging exists (owner comment on PR #37, 2026-09-23). It deploys through Railway's GitHub integration from a branch configured in the Railway UI, which the repository does not record, and the session that built this pass had no Railway CLI, token or connector. The pass is verified by unit tests, the real-PostgreSQL isolation suite, the owner E2E suite against a production build with seeded data, and CI on the PR head. What remains is for the owner to point Staging's `admin` service at this branch (or merge it into the branch Staging tracks) and walk the owner flow there. Production was not touched. |
